1 · Pending decisions

OI-32`docs/cmp/` is a designed home that was never built - materialize it, or retire `detail_doc`

One line

RE-POINTED 2026-08-18 by the owner's direction (NOT a ruling): they are considering a GENERATED approach - one living source of what a component does, derived as a list of the SRs and LLRs tied to that component rather than a hand-maintained document. Rule between the hand-maintained home, the generated view, and retirement

What is being decided

OWNER DIRECTION, 2026-08-18 - A DIRECTION, NOT A RULING, and the brief is re-pointed to it rather than settled by it.

The owner is CONSIDERING A GENERATED APPROACH. What they want is ONE LIVING SOURCE OF "THIS IS WHAT THIS COMPONENT DOES" - but rather than meticulously maintaining it, it would be A GENERATED LIST OF ALL REQUIREMENTS INSIDE A COMPONENT: the SRs and LLRs tied to that component, assembled by a generator.

THE ACKNOWLEDGED GAP, in the owner's own framing: it DOES NOT SHOW INTERNALS. And the owner argues that may be a BENEFIT rather than a cost - it is not trying to show implementation. A component view that lists what the component is OBLIGED to do, and stops there, is a different artifact from a design document, and the owner's position is that the different artifact is the one that stays true.

ON KNOWLEDGE PACKS - the second thing a component row carries today (components.toml's knowledge cell) - the owner wonders whether those could be DERIVED TOO, though it might take more attributes. Their words: IF SRs WERE TAGGED WITH THE HAT THAT DERIVED THEM, KNOWLEDGE PACKS COULD BE TIED TO HATS RATHER THAN COMPONENTS. That would "dissolve knowledge packs as something to infer, and instead set it as something derived from the SR or LLR based on how the need surfaced in the first place."

NET: BOTH PIECES OF INFORMATION A COMPONENT WAS MEANT TO CARRY BECOME DERIVED - what the component does, from the requirements tied to it; which knowledge applies in its space, from the hats that derived those requirements. That is a different answer from every option this brief was drafted with, and it is added below as option (d).

The direction is recorded here, and the same direction is recorded on OI-33, which it re-points harder: if the component spec is GENERATED, the maintenance protocol OI-33 exists to design is answered by the generator rather than by a close ritual.

The original brief follows.

THE COLUMN SHIPS, THE HOME DOES NOT. Measured 2026-08-18:

  • project-trajectory/registries/components.template.toml:9 ships detail_doc = "docs/cmp/example.md" - a shipped value naming a directory the kit never creates. Its own notes cell (:10) glosses the field ("links an optional md detail file (empty = light row, no file)") and enumerates what trace.py checks on a CMP row; DetailDoc is not in that list.
  • PROCESS_OPTIONS.md's Component layer (:2267, field list :2278-2284) names the COLUMN - "an optional DetailDoc" - and never the DIRECTORY. The string docs/cmp appears nowhere in that file. Whole-repo it has seven hits and every one is a template placeholder (components.template.toml:9), a test fixture (tests/test_trace_golden.py:85), or a 2026-08-18 log entry recording that the directory does NOT exist.
  • ls docs/cmp -> No such file or directory.
  • All four live CMP rows omit the key entirely - not empty, ABSENT (docs/requirements/components.toml, CMP-006..009). Each row's durable design is instead crammed into a single notes string.
  • bootstrap.py does not scaffold it: MAPPING copies only the registry (:1662-1663) and GITKEEP_DIRS (:1934-1976) creates docs/work/*, docs/handbacks and docs/log.d - not docs/cmp. No mkdir in the file targets it.
  • NO SCRIPT READS IT. Three hits across all of project-trajectory/scripts/, all column-name translation: spine_carrier.py:259 (snake -> display), spine_carrier.py:404 (the CMP key tuple, schema membership only), migrate_carrier.py:212 (the CSV -> TOML inverse). Nothing resolves the path, checks that it exists, renders it or links it. A populated detail_doc with a typo in it is silent.

THE CONSEQUENCE IS MEASURABLE RATHER THAN HYPOTHETICAL. Rule R-F sends a closed WI's spec-of-record to docs/archive/specs/ (PROCESS_OPTIONS.md:1733-1741; the mover is spec_move.py, ARCHIVE_DIR at :72, the dated destination at :340), and 114 specs now sit there. Durable component design therefore ends up ARCHIVED, and LIVING rows cite it there:

  • SN-027 acceptance (stakeholder-needs.toml:241) - "Spec of record: docs/archive/specs/parallel-wi-dispatch.2026-07-20.md + docs/concurrency-restructure.md";
  • SR-049 rationale (system-requirements.toml:301) - docs/archive/specs/derived-gate-model.2026-07-20.md;
  • SR-043 rationale (system-requirements.toml:277) - docs/archive/INTEGRATION_PLAN.md Phase 4. CORRECTED HERE: that path is one level ABOVE specs/, so an earlier reading of SR-043 as a third archive/specs citer was wrong; the pointer-into-archived-material shape is the same, the folder is not.

The single strongest piece of evidence is not the empty directory. It is that derived-gate-model.2026-07-20.md is read by SIX live scripts from inside the archive - derive_gate.py:11,32, check.py:599,1221, check_trajectory.py:1656, trace.py:49. A document the code depends on is not archived material; the archive is simply where R-F sent it because nothing else had a destination.

THE RULE SIDE ALREADY ADMITS THE GAP IN WRITING. docs/enforcement-audit.md:38 records that R-F mechanizes the POINTERS while "whether durable spec content was absorbed into a spine/architecture home before archiving is Reviewer-tier (the honest gap)". It names no component home, because none exists.

WHY THIS ROW EXISTS NOW: the 2026-08-18 provenance ruling took the SN tier to zero citation frames with exactly one carve-out - these paths - left standing because "with docs/cmp/ held, these can only be dropped, which loses the design pointer" (docs/log.d/2026-08-18-provenance-rule.md §4). The owner held docs/cmp/ for the sitting. This is that hold, on the surface where a hold can be ruled.

Blast radius

DIFFERENT UNDER EACH ANSWER, and only one of the three is free.

  • MATERIALIZING costs: a GITKEEP_DIRS entry plus a scaffold README in bootstrap.py, so every adopting repo gains a directory; the directory named in PROCESS_OPTIONS.md's Component layer, which is a shipped process change and a RESYNC entry; and a resolve obligation - check_doc_refs should refuse a detail_doc that points at nothing, because today nothing validates the cell at all.
  • RETIRING costs: detail_doc deleted from components.template.toml:9, from spine_carrier.OFFSPINE_KEYS (:404) and both translation maps (spine_carrier.py:259, migrate_carrier.py:212), and from tests/test_trajectory_arch.py:171 and tests/test_trace_golden.py:85 - a small, clean edit. The cost is not in the code: it forces a disposition for the three living pointers above, and with no destination the only available disposition is DELETION, which loses the pointer. That is exactly the loss the sweep declined to take.
  • DOING NOTHING costs: the three rows keep citing archived material; the next durable component design archives the same way; and SN-027's frame stays outside docs/provenance-allow while every other deferred marker is declared there - the residue review wrote the exact allow line and handed it to another lane, and no lane has applied it (docs/log.d/2026-08-18-review-round-residue.md §N4 and its Handoffs block).
  • UNTOUCHED under every option: the gate arithmetic, every status cell, every spine id. Nothing here opens a re-attest window.
Options
  • (a) MATERIALIZE docs/cmp/ AND POPULATE IT, promoting the durable design that is already written. FOR: it is the only option that gives the three living pointers somewhere to point, and the promotion candidates are already identified, sized, and each maps onto a component whose OWN notes cell names the module in question - parallel-wi-dispatch.2026-07-20.md (994 lines: worker ceiling, isolated worktrees, one serial fail-closed integrator, pause/drain, crash recovery from git history alone) -> CMP-008 "W3 Autonomy", whose notes enumerate dispatch, integrate, lane, handback; derived-gate-model.2026-07-20.md (227 lines: the derived gate, hybrid derivation plus cache, --check freshness) -> CMP-006 "W1 Registry & conformance", whose notes name derive_gate; docs/archive/INTEGRATION_PLAN.md Phase 4 (230 lines: the subagent-spawn gate, the off|ask|deny dial, the launcher-held override) -> CMP-007 "W2 Gatekeeper", whose notes name subagent_gate. Plus the two LIVE unhoused design docs sitting at docs/ root with no home at all - concurrency-restructure.md (702 lines) and concurrency-v2.md (1,073 lines), both cited from interfaces.toml and low-level-requirements.toml, neither archivable because they are still load-bearing. AGAINST: it creates a THIRD prose surface beside the registries and the log, and a detail doc with no maintenance protocol rots exactly the way an archived spec rots - which is why this option is only half an answer on its own, and why OI-33 exists.
  • (b) RETIRE detail_doc. FOR: the kit's own doctrine is that an unread column is a false promise, and this one has shipped inert since it was minted; deleting it makes the promise unrepresentable rather than documented, which is the standard this repo holds everything else to; and it is genuinely the smaller edit. AGAINST: it deletes the only destination the schema has ever offered for durable component design while leaving the need exactly where it was - and it forces the three living pointers to be dropped with nothing to replace them, which is the loss the provenance sweep explicitly refused to take. It also has no answer for derived-gate-model, a file six scripts read from the archive.
  • (c) LEAVE IT AS-IS. FOR: zero cost, nothing is broken today, and the column is harmless because nothing reads it. AGAINST: harmless-because-unread is the argument that was refuted for interfaces.csv's undeclared Status at OI-13, and it is weaker here - this column ships to every adopter naming a path the kit never creates, which reads as guidance to anyone who opens the template.
  • (d) GENERATE THE COMPONENT VIEW - the owner's direction, added 2026-08-18 and stated as a first-class option. detail_doc stops being a hand-maintained prose file and becomes (or is replaced by) a GENERATED per-component view: the SRs and LLRs tied to the component, rendered as one page, regenerated and freshness-gated like every other derived artifact here. FOR: it is the only option that cannot ROT - a generated view is stale-detectable by the same --check mechanism the other nine artifacts use, where a prose file's staleness is undetectable by construction, which is the whole of OI-33's problem; it needs no new authoring surface and no new ritual, so it does not tax a close; it makes "what does this component do" answerable from the registries that already have to be correct for the gate to pass, so the answer inherits every check the spine already carries; and it composes with the hat direction, which turns the knowledge cell into a derived fact too, leaving a CMP row carrying only what is genuinely irreducible. AGAINST: it DOES NOT SHOW INTERNALS - a requirement list says what a component is obliged to do and nothing about how the parts inside it fit together, so a reader who needs the mechanism (why the dispatcher drains before it pauses, what the crash-recovery invariant is) will not find it; the owner's counter is that this is a BENEFIT rather than a gap, because the view is deliberately not trying to show implementation, and a ruler has to decide whether that holds for the five documents option (a) would have promoted, which are exactly mechanism documents. It also does not answer the archived-material problem on its own: derived-gate-model.2026-07-20.md is read by six live scripts from inside docs/archive/, and no generated requirement list replaces a document that code depends on - so if (d) is ruled, the disposition of those five documents is a SEPARATE question this row still owes an answer to. Finally it needs an SR->component derivation path that does not exist today (see the feasibility note on the recommendation).
Recommendation

THE RULING IS OPEN BETWEEN (d) AND (a), AND THE OWNER'S DIRECTION POINTS AT (d). The original recommendation was (a) and is preserved below unchanged, because its evidence is unaffected by the direction; what follows first is what the direction changes and what it would take to build.

FEASIBILITY OF (d), GROUNDED IN WHAT THE REPO ALREADY HAS - measured 2026-08-18, so a ruler is choosing against facts rather than a sketch.

WHAT COULD BE BUILT TODAY, WITH NO NEW ATTRIBUTE AT ALL: an LLR-tier component view. component is populated on 161 of 161 live LLR rows, it is the membership tag the shipped components template already prescribes ("membership is a Component tag (;-joined CMP ids) on the primitive rows (LLR/IF/ASSET/PART)"), and the four live components partition it CMP-008=53, CMP-009=52, CMP-006=35, CMP-007=21. 57 of 125 IF rows also carry the tag. So "here is every design row this component owns, with its module and its symbol" is a generator anyone could write this week.

WHAT IS MISSING FOR THE SR HALF, and it is the honest gap: SR ROWS CARRY NO component CELL - 0 of 72 - and none is prescribed, because the template puts membership on the PRIMITIVE tier and an SR is not a primitive row. So the SR half of the owner's "all requirements inside a component" is NOT directly available; it has to be DERIVED, and the only available path is SR -> LLR -> component. Measured on that path: 60 of 72 SRs have at least one LLR child and therefore a derivable component set; 12 have none and would appear in no component view at all; and 6 SRs' children span MORE THAN ONE component, so the derived value is a SET rather than a value - a requirement can legitimately be "inside" two components. None of that refutes the direction; all of it has to be decided before a generator is written, because a view that silently drops 12 requirements is worse than no view.

WHAT IS MISSING FOR THE KNOWLEDGE HALF: AN SR-TIER HAT FIELD. docs/requirements/hats.toml exists and holds SIXTEEN hats, each with a ratified charter. 18 live SR rows ALREADY RECORD their deriving hat - but in PROSE, inside rationale, as Hat-derived (hat.MAINTAINER) and the like; eleven distinct hats appear across the 18, and five of the sixteen derive nothing. Nothing mechanical reads any of it. Zero LLR rows record a hat at all. So the owner's "if SRs were tagged with the hat that derived them" is a NEW CELL - a hats list on the SR tier (and, for the LLR half of the same idea, on the design tier) - whose FIRST 18 VALUES ARE ALREADY WRITTEN and only need lifting out of prose. That is the same 18-row population OI-34 rules on, which is why the two are one thread.

SO, PLAINLY, WHAT EACH HALF NEEDS: the requirements half needs an SR->component DERIVATION PATH ruled (probably SR->LLR->component, with a stated answer for the 12 childless SRs and the 6 multi-component ones); the knowledge half needs an SR-TIER HAT ATTRIBUTE minted and the 18 prose attributions migrated onto it. Neither is designed here, and NEITHER IS THIS BRIEF'S JOB - EXECUTION IS ITS OWN WI. What this brief owes the owner is the choice, and the choice is now between a hand-maintained home that cannot be checked, a generated view that cannot show internals, and retirement.

ONE THING (d) DOES NOT DISSOLVE, and it should decide part of the ruling: the five documents option (a) would have promoted are MECHANISM documents, and one of them - derived-gate-model.2026-07-20.md - is read by six live scripts from inside docs/archive/. A generated requirement list does not house them. Whichever way this rules, those five need a disposition.

--- THE ORIGINAL RECOMMENDATION, WRITTEN BEFORE THE DIRECTION, PRESERVED ---

(a) MATERIALIZE, and promote the five named documents rather than starting empty.

The deciding evidence is not the empty directory - an unused column is a small sin. It is derived-gate-model.2026-07-20.md being read by six live scripts from inside docs/archive/. That file is not history; it is the specification of a mechanism the harness runs today, and it lives in the archive because R-F had nowhere else to send it. When the archive is load-bearing, the archive is not the problem - the missing destination is.

(b) is the honest alternative and a ruler may reasonably take it: an unread column deleted is a real simplification, and this repo has retired columns for exactly that reason before. The argument against it is narrow and should be weighed on its own terms - (b) does not remove the NEED, it removes the only NAME the need has. The three living pointers do not become correct when the column goes away; they become undeclared.

TWO GUARDS ON (a), because a directory of prose is easy to create and hard to keep true:

  • Promote WITH the pointer, not beside it. Each of the three living rows should end up citing docs/cmp/<component>.md rather than an archived path, in the same change that promotes the content - otherwise the repo gains a new surface AND keeps the old citations, which is worse than either.
  • Do not rule (a) and then leave OI-33 unruled. A detail doc with no maintenance protocol is an archived spec that has not been archived yet; the two decisions are one program, and (a) alone would move 3,000 lines of design into a home nobody is obliged to keep correct.

OWED EITHER WAY, and it should not wait for this ruling: SN-027's acceptance frame is the one deferred marker with no entry in docs/provenance-allow. The exact line was drafted at the residue review and handed to a lane that has not applied it. Whichever way this rules, the live exception surface should carry the exception rather than the log carrying it alone.

OI-33The component-spec maintenance protocol - components are the living spec, and work items tag on functionality

One line

RE-POINTED 2026-08-18 by the owner's direction (NOT a ruling): if the component spec is GENERATED from the SRs and LLRs tied to the component, the maintenance protocol this row exists to design is answered by the generator - rule between a close ritual over a hand-maintained spec and a derived one that needs none

What is being decided

OWNER DIRECTION, 2026-08-18 - A DIRECTION, NOT A RULING, and it re-points this row harder than it re-points OI-32.

The owner is CONSIDERING A GENERATED APPROACH. They want ONE LIVING SOURCE OF "THIS IS WHAT THIS COMPONENT DOES" - but rather than meticulously maintaining it, it would be A GENERATED LIST OF ALL REQUIREMENTS INSIDE A COMPONENT: the SRs and LLRs tied to that component. The acknowledged gap is that it DOES NOT SHOW INTERNALS, and the owner argues that may be a BENEFIT - it is not trying to show implementation.

On knowledge packs the owner wonders whether those could be DERIVED TOO, though it might take more attributes: IF SRs WERE TAGGED WITH THE HAT THAT DERIVED THEM, KNOWLEDGE PACKS COULD BE TIED TO HATS RATHER THAN COMPONENTS - which would "dissolve knowledge packs as something to infer, and instead set it as something derived from the SR or LLR based on how the need surfaced in the first place."

WHY THIS RE-POINTS THIS ROW HARDER THAN OI-32. This brief was written to design a MAINTENANCE PROTOCOL for a hand-authored document, and its five obligations - (a) scope the work, (b) leave the spec at the right detail, (c) in the right location, (d) with superseded decisions REMOVED, (e) without restating the WI's own scope - are all obligations on a HUMAN OR AGENT AUTHOR. A generated view has none of them. (b) and (c) are the generator's layout. (d), named in this brief as the hardest of the five, DISSOLVES ENTIRELY: a superseded decision is removed from a generated view by amending the requirement row it came from, which is an act the spine already forces and already gates. (e) dissolves too - a generator cannot restate a work item's scope, because a work item's scope is not among its inputs.

So the honest statement is that the owner's direction does not answer this brief's question; IT DELETES MOST OF IT. What survives is narrower and worth naming: a derived view is only as true as the rows it derives from, so the obligation moves from "update the component doc at close" to "leave the REQUIREMENT ROWS correct at close" - which is an obligation the repo already has, already enforces, and already fails at in a measurable way (LLR-037 published a deleted symbol until it was caught by a census, not by a check). That is a smaller and much better-posed question than the one this row was minted to ask.

The original brief follows.

THE MODEL BEING RULED ON IS THE OWNER'S, stated in their own framing: COMPONENTS ARE THE LIVING SPEC SOURCE, and work items TAG ON functionality. The difficulty is theirs too, and it is the core of this decision rather than context for it. A work item must be minted so that it:

(a) SCOPES ITS OWN WORK - the thing a WI has always had to do; (b) leaves its component's spec UP TO DATE AT THE CORRECT LEVEL OF DETAIL; (c) in the RIGHT LOCATION within the component spec; (d) REMOVING decisions and implementation detail that has since changed or mutated; (e) and NOT simply restating the work item's own scope into the component doc.

THE FIVE PULL AGAINST EACH OTHER, and the tension is structural rather than a matter of care. A WI is a TIME-BOXED record: it opens with a scope, closes with a verdict, and its spec-of-record is ARCHIVED at close by R-F (PROCESS_OPTIONS.md:1733-1741; spec_move.py:340). A component spec is DURABLE: it outlives every WI that ever touches it, and its correctness at any moment is every decision still standing MINUS every decision since overturned - a state no single WI can see. Nothing today connects the two artifacts at all.

So component knowledge takes one of exactly two bad paths, and both are visible in the tree today. It ROTS INSIDE AN ARCHIVED WI SPEC - 114 specs now sit in docs/archive/specs/, three living spine rows point into archived material (SN-027, SR-049, and SR-043 into docs/archive/INTEGRATION_PLAN.md), and one of those archived files is read by six live scripts. Or it is DUPLICATED into a second home, at which point the copies drift and the repo's own anti-duplication doctrine is broken by the very mechanism meant to serve it.

THE GAP IS ALREADY ADMITTED IN WRITING, which is why this is a decision rather than a discovery. docs/enforcement-audit.md:38 records that R-F mechanizes the pointers while "whether durable spec content was absorbed into a spine/architecture home before archiving is Reviewer-tier (the honest gap)". R-F's own statement adds the sharper half: the "prose-only close ritual is skipped by autonomous agents". That names the failure exactly - the one step that would carry knowledge forward is the step an unattended loop does not perform, and this repo runs an unattended loop.

(d) IS THE HARDEST OF THE FIVE and deserves naming separately, because every option below is easy on (b) and (c) and hard on (d). ADDING to a spec is monotone and cheap. REMOVING a decision that has since mutated requires knowing that it mutated - and the closing WI is usually the only party that knows, at the one moment they are least motivated to write it down. A protocol that only adds produces a spec that is a superset of every decision ever taken, which is not a specification; it is a transcript.

(e) IS WHERE THE ANTI-DUPLICATION DOCTRINE BITES, and the failure mode is not laziness. A WI author handed a mandatory "update the component doc" box will discharge it with the most available correct-sounding text, which is the WI's own scope statement. That produces a component spec that reads as a CHANGELOG OF WORK ITEMS - precisely the shape the 2026-08-18 provenance ruling just finished removing from every registry cell, re-created one surface over. Any option taken here has to say what (e) means operationally or it will be discharged this way by default.

DEPENDENCY, stated plainly so it is not discovered late: this is DOWNSTREAM OF OI-32. There is no location to write into until docs/cmp/ exists. If OI-32 rules RETIRE, this item is moot and should be struck rather than left standing as a pending obligation against a home that will not be built.

Blast radius

THE WI CLOSE RITUAL, for this repo and every adopter - and it lands on the LOOP as hard as on the human. At human_ratification_through = 4 attestation surfaces to the owner while build work flows, so a deliverable added to close is a deliverable an unattended session must produce, in the same close where R-F already records that agents skip the prose-only half.

  • (a) hardens docs/work/ close for every project and needs the anti-restatement rule written into the SHIPPED ritual, not only recorded here - a RESYNC entry and a downstream migration.
  • (b) puts the obligation at spec_move.py, which becomes a refusal rather than a mover; that is a behaviour change to a script every adopter runs at every close.
  • (c) needs a JOIN THAT DOES NOT EXIST: CMP -> member modules -> LLR.Module -> the WIs that touched those modules. Every piece is present (LLR.Component is populated on every row, LLR.Module names ~70 modules, check_trajectory already resolves module paths) and nothing joins them - and the join is only ever as good as LLR.Module's hand-authored cells, which are validated by nothing.
  • NO RE-ATTEST WINDOW under any option: nothing here moves a status cell, closes an enum, or touches a spine id. The cost is process and prose, not ratification.
  • The honest ceiling on all of it: docs/enforcement-audit.md is explicit that content quality is Reviewer-tier. No option below can check that a component spec is CORRECT. They differ only in whether they make the omission visible.
Options
  • (a) A MANDATORY COMPONENT-UPDATE DELIVERABLE IN THE WI CLOSE RITUAL, with (e) written as an ACCEPTANCE CONDITION rather than as advice: the WI does not close until its component's detail_doc states the standing design in component voice, and the reviewer's question is stated in the ritual - "would this sentence read the same if this work item had never existed?" FOR: it puts the obligation where the knowledge actually is, at the hands of the only party who can answer (d), at the one moment they still know the answer; it re-uses a gate the repo already has instead of building a new one; and turning (e) from advice into an acceptance condition is precisely what distinguishes it from the prose-only ritual R-F already admits gets skipped. AGAINST: it is a prose-quality obligation, and a check can see that the file CHANGED, never that it changed CORRECTLY - so the enforcement is Reviewer-tier by construction; and it taxes every close, including the many that touch no durable design at all, which is how a ritual earns its reputation for being skippable.
  • (b) A SEPARATE DURABLE-CONTENT PROMOTION STEP AT ARCHIVE TIME - spec_move.py refuses to archive a spec until it is marked either "absorbed into CMP-nnn" or "nothing durable". FOR: it closes exactly the gap enforcement-audit.md:38 names, at the exact moment the content is about to leave the live tree; it is mechanizable as a REFUSAL rather than a warning, which is the strongest enforcement tier available here; and the disposition is recorded per spec, so the record survives whichever way it goes. AGAINST: archive time is the LATEST possible moment and usually the worst one - the work is finished, the context has drained, and a forced binary under those conditions produces "nothing durable" as the path of least resistance. It also does nothing at all for (d): a spec being absorbed says nothing about which older decision it supersedes.
  • (c) A STALENESS DETECTOR - flag a CMP whose detail_doc has not been touched by any WI that changed its member modules. FOR: it is the only option that is honestly mechanical, it requires no new human ritual, it points at exactly the right rows, and it produces EVIDENCE about how large the problem is, which none of the others do. AGAINST: it is a proxy and a weak one - it cannot distinguish a WI that changed behaviour from one that fixed a typo, so it will cry wolf and then be silenced, which is the failure mode this repo has a standing rule against; the join it needs does not exist yet; and it detects the ABSENCE of an edit, never the presence of a wrong one, so it is silent on (d) and (e) both.
  • (d) DO NOTHING - components stay light rows and durable design continues to live wherever it lands. FOR: zero cost, and the repo has run this way its whole life without the wheels coming off. AGAINST: it is the state that produced 114 archived specs with living rows pointing into them and one archived file read by six scripts; and it makes the owner's stated model - components ARE the living spec - false by construction, which is the one outcome a ruling here cannot leave in place.
  • (e) DERIVE THE COMPONENT SPEC AND CARRY NO PROTOCOL AT ALL - the owner's direction, added 2026-08-18 and stated as a first-class option. The component's "spec" becomes a GENERATED view of the SRs and LLRs tied to it, regenerated and freshness-gated like every other derived artifact; the knowledge half becomes a derived function of the HATS that derived those rows rather than a hand-set knowledge cell. There is then no close ritual to design, because there is no hand-maintained document to leave correct. FOR: it dissolves (d), the hardest of this brief's five obligations - a superseded decision leaves a derived view when the requirement row it came from is amended, which is an act the spine already forces and already reviews, rather than an act depending on the closing author remembering that something was superseded at the one moment they are least motivated to write it down; it dissolves (e), the anti-restatement rule, because a generator has no access to a work item's scope statement and so cannot restate it - the failure mode this brief predicted (a component spec that becomes a changelog of work items) is UNREPRESENTABLE rather than forbidden; it adds no tax to any close, which is what makes prose-only rituals get skipped, and R-F's own admission is that the skipped party is the unattended loop; and its staleness is MECHANICALLY DETECTABLE by the same --check shape nine artifacts already use, where a prose spec's staleness is detectable by nothing, which is the honest ceiling this brief records on every other option. AGAINST: it moves the failure rather than removing it - a derived view is exactly as true as the rows it derives from, so a WI that closes leaving a stale LLR now produces a CONFIDENTLY WRONG generated page instead of an obviously old document, and this repo has a measured instance of precisely that (LLR-037 published a symbol deleted five weeks earlier, found by a census rather than by a check); it shows no internals, so the mechanism knowledge that lives in the five documents OI-32 would have promoted has no home under this option and needs its own disposition; and it needs attributes that do not exist yet, so unlike (a) it cannot start tomorrow.
Recommendation

THE RULING IS OPEN, AND THE OWNER'S DIRECTION POINTS AT (e). The original recommendation was (a)+(c) and is preserved below unchanged. What follows first is what the direction changes, and what it would take.

THIS ROW IS STRICTLY DOWNSTREAM OF OI-32 AND THE DEPENDENCY IS NOW SHARPER THAN WHEN IT WAS WRITTEN. Before the direction, the dependency was locational: no protocol until there is a place to write. After it, the dependency is EXISTENTIAL: if OI-32 rules the generated view, THIS BRIEF'S QUESTION LARGELY CEASES TO EXIST, and this row should be re-scoped rather than ruled - re-scoped to the one obligation that survives, stated below.

WHAT SURVIVES UNDER (e), AND IT IS WORTH MINTING EVEN IF NOTHING ELSE HERE IS: a derived view inherits the correctness of its inputs, so the close obligation becomes LEAVE THE REQUIREMENT ROWS CORRECT, not leave the component document correct. That is not a new rule - it is what the spine already demands - but it is currently enforced only where a checker can reach, and the repo has a measured miss: LLR-037 carried status_size_warning as a live design claim for five weeks after the symbol was deleted, and it was caught by a census rather than by any check. The code_symbol anchor rule would have caught a DANGLING symbol; it did not catch this one, because the cell described the symbol in prose while its code_symbol token pointed elsewhere. That is a concrete, cheap, generator-independent thing to want, and it is the honest residue of this brief.

FEASIBILITY OF (e), GROUNDED IN WHAT THE REPO ALREADY HAS - measured 2026-08-18. docs/requirements/hats.toml exists and holds SIXTEEN ratified hats. 18 live SR rows already record which hat derived them, but in PROSE inside rationale (Hat-derived (hat.SECURITY)), covering 11 of the 16 hats; nothing mechanical reads any of it, and ZERO LLR rows record a hat at all. Component membership today is a component tag on the PRIMITIVE rows - 161 of 161 LLR rows carry one, 57 of 125 IF rows do - exactly as the shipped components template prescribes; SR rows carry NO component cell (0 of 72) and none is prescribed. So a component view could be built TODAY from the LLR half alone; the SR half needs a derivation path (SR->LLR->component is the only one available: 60 of 72 SRs have an LLR child, 12 have none and would appear nowhere, and 6 have children in more than one component so the derived value is a SET), and the knowledge half needs a NEW SR-TIER HAT ATTRIBUTE whose first 18 values are already written in prose. EXECUTION IS ITS OWN WI in either case; nothing here designs it.

--- THE ORIGINAL RECOMMENDATION, WRITTEN BEFORE THE DIRECTION, PRESERVED ---

(a) AS THE OBLIGATION, WITH (c) AS ITS INSTRUMENT, and NOT (b).

THE OBLIGATION BELONGS AT CLOSE because that is the only moment (d) is answerable. The knowledge that a decision has MUTATED - not been added to, but superseded - exists in exactly one place: the head of the person or session that just changed it. An hour later it is reconstructible only by reading the diff; a month later it is gone. Every option that defers the obligation is trading the one input it cannot get back for a more convenient moment to ask for it.

(b) IS REFUSED ON ITS TIMING, not its mechanism. Its mechanism is the best available - a refusal beats a warning, and spec_move.py is the right chokepoint - but it asks the question after the work is finished, when "nothing durable" is both the cheapest answer and unfalsifiable. A gate whose easy answer is also its wrong answer is a gate that measures compliance rather than content. If a ruler prefers (b), the fix that makes it defensible is to require the disposition to NAME the absorbing section, so "nothing durable" becomes a claim someone can check rather than a checkbox.

(c) ALONE IS REFUSED because it measures the wrong thing - an untouched file is not a wrong file, and a detector that fires on correctness-neutral edits gets silenced. But (c) IS THE RIGHT SENSOR FOR (a), and that pairing is the actual recommendation: a check that a CMP's detail doc went untouched while its member modules changed cannot judge the edit, but it can tell you THE RITUAL WAS SKIPPED - which is the exact failure R-F already names and currently detects not at all. Build (a) as the obligation and (c) as its warn-first detector, and say plainly in the enforcement audit that the judgement stays Reviewer-tier. That is a smaller claim than "we enforce component specs" and it is the true one.

ON (e), THE ANTI-RESTATEMENT RULE, because this is where a well-intentioned (a) fails: state it in the SHIPPED close ritual as a test the author applies to their own text, not as a principle. The test that works is temporal - a component spec sentence must be true of the system as it stands, with no reference to the change that made it true. "WI-nnn added X" fails. "The dispatcher admits at most N workers" passes. This is the same rule the 2026-08-18 provenance ruling landed on every registry cell, and it should be stated as the same rule rather than re-derived - one doctrine, two surfaces.

SEQUENCING: this cannot execute before OI-32, and it should not be ruled in a way that presumes OI-32's answer. If OI-32 rules MATERIALIZE, rule this in the same sitting - a home with no maintenance protocol is an archived spec that has not been archived yet, and promoting 3,000 lines of design into an unmaintained directory is a step backwards from leaving it where it is. If OI-32 rules RETIRE, strike this row rather than leaving it pending against a destination that will not exist.

OI-36Scope of the provenance sweep at the IF tier - sweep the 49 Contract citations and the 8-frame worklist, or scope them out

One line

rule whether the 49 pre-existing work-item/decision citations in IF Contract cells and the 8 remaining citation frames in IF/LLR/TC cells are swept under the 2026-08-18 provenance ruling or scoped out with a stated reason - today they are outside it by a log sentence that nothing pins; recommendation is (a) sweep the 8 now, (b) hold the 49 for the interface lane

What is being decided

TWO POPULATIONS, DIFFERENT IN KIND, CURRENTLY HELD BY THE SAME SILENCE.

POPULATION ONE - THE 49. if_contract_advisories (trace.py:1525-1559) reports a work-item id or a decision citation in an IF Contract cell. Re-measured live 2026-08-18: 46 Contract names WI-### findings plus 3 Contract cites decision findings = 49 (the function emits 111 findings in total; the other 62 are its connective-prose and 500-character arms, which are different rules and not at issue here).

Their exclusion is recorded in exactly one place - docs/log.d/2026-08-18-provenance-rule.md:213, a row in the disposition table reading "outside this ruling's scope (the Contract cell has its own rule and its own backlog)". AND NOTHING HOLDS THAT. Unlike the 17 label carve-outs, which tests/test_trace.py:1792 pins by asserting every allow entry still bites, and unlike the 8-frame worklist, which is reproducible from the detector on demand, the 49 is a SENTENCE IN A LOG FRAGMENT. There is no allow entry, no test, no baseline. If one is dropped or ten are added, no artifact changes and no one is told. It happens to still be 49 today; that is a coincidence, not an invariant.

Note that if_contract_advisories takes no allow parameter at all (trace.py:1525, versus if_note_advisories(ifs, allow=()) at :1619) - the Contract arm cannot be adjudicated row-by-row even if someone wanted to.

POPULATION TWO - THE 8. Recorded as a worklist at docs/log.d/2026-08-18-review-round-detector.md:155-158, and reproducible: with the exception list applied, the citation-frame detector reports exactly 8 findings, every one outside the detector lane's own files. Verified live:

  • IF-043 notes (interfaces.toml:641) - edit-history stamp INVESTIGATED.
  • IF-117 notes (:1449) - WI-429, decision id D-6, date stamp 2026-08-17c. PARTLY allow-listed: docs/provenance-allow:31 silences only REVERTED TO THE SR 2026-08-17 and says in terms that "its other tokens are NOT covered here and stay on the worklist".
  • IF-127 notes (:1586) - RE-AFFIRMED 2026-08-17, dates 2026-08-15h, 2026-08-17c.
  • IF-128 notes (:1600) - OWNER RE-POINTED.
  • IF-130 notes (:1625) - OI-30, RESOLVED 2026-08-17, CORRECTED, dates 2026-08-17c, 2026-08-16b.
  • LLR-158 detail (low-level-requirements.toml:1593) - RE-POINTED.
  • LLR-178 detail (:1830) - MINTED.
  • TC-153 method (test-cases.toml:1514) - RE-POINTED.

IF-127 and IF-130 are on this list for a specific reason worth carrying into the ruling: their allow entries were RETIRED on 2026-08-18 (docs/provenance-allow:33-42) after it was found that the entries, written to protect a "PROVISIONAL, standing for the review sitting to overturn" marker the detector never reported, were in fact suppressing OTHER tokens - OI-30, RESOLVED 2026-08-17, two ruling dates - "that no reviewed reason had ever named". So these two are not un-swept leftovers; they are tokens that were being silenced by an entry written for something else, now correctly visible.

THE COMMON QUESTION, and it is one question: the ruling says no provenance in a living registry cell, on any spine tier, in normative and reason cells alike. Fifty-seven cells currently carry provenance and are not being swept, on the authority of two log sentences. Either the ruling reaches them, or it does not and the reason should be written where a reader will find it - which today means docs/provenance-allow or the rule's own text, not a log fragment.

OWNER QUESTION, 2026-08-18, AND THE ANSWER - recorded on the surface so the ruling is not re-derived from a conversation. The owner asks whether the 49 can RIDE WI-455, so that it happens at once rather than as a separate pass.

ANSWER, IN TWO PARTS.

FIRST, IT CAN, AND THAT SUPPORTS (b). WI-455's lane IS STILL OPEN - docs/work/active/wi455-architecture-retirement/ - so there is a live lane to fold the 49 into, and that lane already owns the Contract cell's other rules. Folding the provenance sweep into it means the 46 rows are rewritten ONCE, by one author, against one set of intentions, which is exactly the double-labour argument (b) rests on. Nothing about the 49 needs its own lane.

SECOND, THE CAVEAT, AND IT IS THE REASON THIS IS NOT SIMPLY A YES: THAT LANE'S REMAINDER IS ITSELF BLOCKED BEHIND WI-469. WI-469 re-authors the 27 file-as-endpoint Consumes rows and states in its own title that it "unblocks wi455's column-drop: re-author first, then drop what has become derivable." So the 49 do not merely ride WI-455 - THEY INHERIT WI-455'S BLOCKER. Riding the lane buys one pass over the rows and costs an unbounded wait, where the wait is bounded by a work item that has not started.

WHAT THAT MEANS FOR THE RULING. It does not change (b)'s recommendation; it changes what (b) OWES. If the 49 ride WI-455, the hold must be recorded on a LIVE surface with the blocking chain named - docs/provenance-allow or the rule's own text, naming WI-455 and its WI-469 dependency - because a hold that is invisible AND indefinite is the exact shape the brief above argues against. If the sitting is not willing to wait on a two-deep chain, that is the case for (a), and it should be taken knowingly rather than by the hold expiring quietly.

Blast radius

PROSE ONLY, AND THAT IS THE HONEST ANSWER FOR BOTH POPULATIONS - no row's meaning changes, no status cell moves, no id is minted or retired, no re-attest window opens under any option. The differences are in labour and in coupling.

  • THE 8 are eight cells, all warn-only findings, and the reason each survived is that it belongs to a lane that was not the detector's. Sweeping them is an afternoon of careful restatement, and the restatement is the whole cost: each token sits inside a sentence that carries real reasoning, and IF-130's CORRECTED at the adversarial round in particular records that an earlier mint cited a nonexistent fallback - dropping the frame without restating the correction would lose a real fact.
  • THE 49 are coupled to WI-455's interface lane, which already owns the Contract cell's own rule, its length ceiling and its connective rule. Sweeping them separately means two passes over the same 46 rows, and the second pass would re-open cells the first just rewrote.
  • IF-117 is coupled to a pending decision rather than a lane - its remaining tokens are the record of an unresolved design gap, which is the subject of its own open item. Sweeping it before that is ruled would delete the marker for the question.
  • THE COST OF SCOPING OUT is not zero and is easy to under-weigh: the exclusion currently lives in log fragments, which are history rather than a working surface. A reader running trace.py sees 57 warnings and no indication that 57 of them are sanctioned - which is the shape that trains a reader to ignore the warning class.
Options
  • (a) SWEEP BOTH POPULATIONS under the same ruling, now. FOR: the ruling says what it says, and a rule with a 57-cell unstated exception is a rule the next reader cannot apply; it clears the citation-frame class to zero across every tier, which is a checkable state rather than a narrative one; and it removes the reader-training problem where a permanently warm warning class stops being read. AGAINST: it drives a prose campaign through 46 IF Contract cells that WI-455's interface lane is about to rewrite for other reasons, so most of that work is done twice; and it would sweep IF-117's tokens, which are the only live record that a design gap is open.
  • (b) SWEEP THE 8, HOLD THE 49 FOR THE INTERFACE LANE, and record the hold where a reader meets it - an entry in docs/provenance-allow or a clause in the rule, not a log fragment. FOR: the 8 are genuinely finishable now and each belongs to a lane that has closed; the 49 are coupled to a lane that is live, so holding them costs one pass instead of two; and moving the exclusion from a log sentence to the live exception surface is the part that is owed regardless of which way this rules. AGAINST: it leaves the citation-frame class non-zero for the duration of the interface lane, so the checkable state does not arrive; and a hold recorded as an exception is a hold that can be forgotten, since nothing dates it or expires it.
  • (c) SCOPE BOTH OUT WITH A STATED REASON - declare that the Contract cell has its own rule and its own backlog, and that the 8 belong to their lanes, then say so in the rule's text. FOR: it is the honest description of what is happening anyway, and it stops the pretence that the sweep is complete; the Contract cell genuinely does have a separate rule with a separate ceiling and separate connective checks, so treating it as one subject is defensible. AGAINST: if_contract_advisories takes no allow parameter, so there is no mechanism to record a per-row exception even if one were wanted - the exclusion can only be a blanket one; and a blanket exclusion for a whole cell type is how the Contract cell became a changelog in the first place, which OI-14's census measured in detail.
  • (d) DO NOTHING - leave the exclusion in the log fragments. FOR: zero cost. AGAINST: the 49 rests on a sentence nothing pins, and a population held by nothing drifts silently - which is precisely the finding that retired the IF-123/127/130 allow entries this same week.
Recommendation

(b) SWEEP THE 8 NOW, HOLD THE 49 FOR WI-455'S INTERFACE LANE, AND MOVE THE HOLD ONTO A LIVE SURFACE - with one carve-out inside the 8.

The two populations differ in the one way that decides the answer. The 8 belong to lanes that have CLOSED, so nothing will rewrite those cells again and a sweep now is a sweep once. The 49 belong to a lane that is LIVE and that already owns the Contract cell's other three rules; sweeping them separately means rewriting 46 cells that WI-455 is about to rewrite anyway, and the second author inherits prose the first one chose for different reasons. That is not a preference, it is the same double-labour argument that reordered OI-12's sequencing and OI-14's part ordering.

CARVE IF-117 OUT OF THE 8. Its remaining tokens are not residue - they are the only live record that a design obligation has no stating row, which is the subject of its own pending item. Sweeping the frame before that is ruled would delete the marker and leave a clean cell describing a gap nobody can now find. Sweep the other seven; leave IF-117 until its own ruling lands, and say so in the allow file.

THE PART THAT IS OWED WHICHEVER WAY THIS RULES, and it should not wait: the 49's exclusion currently exists as one sentence in one log fragment, pinned by nothing, while every comparable carve-out in this repo is either an allow entry with a test behind it or a reproducible detector output. Put the hold on the live exception surface with the lane that owns it named, so that a reader running trace.py can tell a sanctioned warning from an unadjudicated one. Today they cannot, and the failure that retired three allow entries this same week was exactly that: entries nobody could tell were suppressing more than they claimed.

(a) IS THE PRINCIPLED ANSWER and would be right if the interface lane were not live. If the sitting expects WI-455 to slip, take (a) - a rule with an unstated 57-cell exception decays faster than a lane finishes.

(c) SHOULD BE REJECTED. Its premise is sound - the Contract cell does have its own rule - but if_contract_advisories takes no allow parameter, so scoping out can only be BLANKET, and a blanket exemption for a whole cell type is how that cell became a changelog in the first place. OI-14's census measured the drift: 24% of Contract cells naming a work-item id, rising while the item sat unruled. Sanctioning that is ratifying the drift.

OI-38SR-040's resume-surface tripwire - an obligation whose carrier was deleted: rebuild it, or strike the clause

One line

rule SR-040's resume-surface size tripwire, whose carrier was deleted with the serial resume driver and whose SUBJECT no longer exists either - the nearest live look-alike was examined and refused on measured evidence, so the row states an obligation nothing satisfies; recommendation is (b) STRIKE, with the two hat lenses answered explicitly rather than silently

What is being decided

THE CLAUSE. SR-040's shall (system-requirements.toml:260) ends: "...and warning (never blocking) when a lane resume surface exceeds the declared size threshold." Its acceptance (:265) states the fit criterion and then explicitly disclaims discharge: "NOTHING IN THE DELIVERED COORDINATOR DOES THIS, so this clause is an open obligation with no current carrier rather than a criterion any evidence today satisfies."

THE CARRIER WAS status_size_warning(status_path, limit) in agent_loop.py, deleted at commit c4aeab9f (2026-07-17, "WI-210: legacy-path retirement - one engine, one selection path"), whose message lists it among the things deleted WITH the serial driver. The module now carries a tombstone in its place (agent_loop.py:499-501):

# (status_size_warning retired with the serial driver, WI-210: no session # inherits status.md as its resume surface any more - status.md is a # generated integrator artifact whose size the generator owns.)

READ THAT CAREFULLY, because it is the fact that decides this item: THE PREMISE DISSOLVED, THE CODE DID NOT MOVE. There is no longer any "lane resume surface" that a session inherits. So the clause has no implementation AND its subject as originally drawn has no referent. Its dial, AGENT_STATUS_WARN_BYTES, occurs nowhere in live code or tests.

THE NEAREST LIVE CANDIDATE WAS EXAMINED AND REFUSED, ON MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN TASTE. check_docs.check_status_surface warns when docs/status.md passes a declared line budget, which sounds like this clause and is not it. It differs on ACTOR (a documentation checker, not the delivered coordinator the shall names), on MOMENT (the commit and gate bar, not run start or mid-run), and on SURFACE (a status page judged as lean prose for a human reader - which is the very "human noticing" the C-UNA-8 lens says is the wrong mechanism at 3am). It is already the carrier of a different requirement. And decisively, ITS THRESHOLD SEMANTICS ARE INVERTED: only the literal token off silences it (check_docs.py:777), so a declared 0 or -1 becomes the budget and every non-empty file exceeds it - the exact opposite of the retired warner, which returned None when limit <= 0. That was measured by exercising the function on declared 0, -1, off and 200, not read off the source.

So adopting it would have been carrier substitution across a different requirement, component, actor, moment AND with inverted semantics. It was refused, and the refusal is on the record rather than in someone's head.

WHAT IS AT STAKE IN STRIKING IT. SR-040's rationale says "only the resume-surface tripwire clause is orphaned", and the row's hat-derived parentage is that clause: two lenses reach it independently - C-UXE-2, that a view must stay usable as its content volume grows, and C-UNA-8, that at 3am the reader of a resume surface is another automated step, so growth must be legible from the artifact rather than from a human noticing. Both also explain the warn-not-fail severity: a smell is reported, not gated. Strike the clause and SR-040 loses its entire derived-requirement parentage, which makes its (Derived-requirement label, ... PROVISIONAL, unsigned.) parenthetical moot for this row - a coupling to OI-34 worth noting.

THE CHILDREN ARE ALREADY CORRECTED AND WAITING. LLR-037's detail (low-level-requirements.toml:394) records that the tripwire half is gone from the row and that the module has no size tripwire, and TC-040's method (test-cases.toml:423) records that a fourth clause it used to claim is performed by no test in its evidence file. Both flipped Approved to Modified on 2026-08-18. They are the subject of their own item, and neither can settle until this one does.

OWNER QUESTION, 2026-08-18, AND THE ANSWER - recorded here plainly because the brief above assumed it. The owner asked WHAT "SIZE" REFERS TO.

IT IS THE SIZE OF THE LANE RESUME SURFACE - the document an agent READS TO RESUME A LANE. Nothing else: not the size of the repository, not the size of a registry, not the size of a log. The clause's concern is that the artifact a session must read before it can continue grows until reading it costs more than the work, and that the growth is invisible because each addition is small.

THE TRIPWIRE WARNED AND NEVER BLOCKED. status_size_warning(status_path, limit) printed a warning when the surface passed a declared byte threshold and returned None when limit <= 0. It could not fail a run, fail a gate, or refuse a step. That severity is not incidental - both hat lenses behind the clause (C-UXE-2, C-UNA-8) reach a SMELL rather than a defect, and a smell is reported, not gated.

AND THE SURFACE IT WATCHED NO LONGER WORKS THAT WAY. When the serial resume driver was retired, docs/status.md stopped being a document a session INHERITS and became a GENERATED INTEGRATOR ARTIFACT WHOSE SIZE THE GENERATOR OWNS. So the question "how big is the resume surface" now has a different answer-holder: the generator decides what goes in it, and a size bound on it is a constraint on the generator's output, not a warning to whoever last wrote to a file by hand. That is the whole of why the brief above says a rebuild is a NEW requirement against a NEW subject rather than a restore of the deleted function.

Blast radius

NARROW, AND ASYMMETRIC BETWEEN THE TWO DISPOSITIONS - which is unusual enough to be the deciding fact rather than a footnote.

  • RE-ATTEST: SR-040, LLR-037 and TC-040 are all already Modified. The window is open; a ruling CLOSES it. No option here opens a new one.
  • STRIKING costs one reviewed Status-change commit and NO CODE: the final conjunct leaves SR-040's shall, the resume-surface paragraph leaves its acceptance, LLR-037's and TC-040's recording paragraphs go (they exist only to record the gap), and the two docs/provenance-allow entries retire with the marker they mark. TC-040 already passes on its three function-granular evidence tests, so nothing needs writing.
  • REBUILDING costs a DESIGN QUESTION FIRST, then code: what IS the current resume surface, and who inherits it? The tombstone says nobody inherits status.md any more, so a rebuild cannot restore the deleted function - it must answer a requirement-shaped question before a line is written. Then: a new warner in the coordinator (the shall's subject is "the delivered coordinator", so it must live there), LLR-037's struck half restored with a code_symbol token for the new function, TC-040's fourth method clause re-added with a function-granular evidence entry, and that test written.
  • WHAT NEITHER OPTION TOUCHES: SR-040's routing half - the per-phase command template map, the preflight, the reviewer dial in the banner - is live, carried by session_template/parse_map/read_declared, and verified by TC-040's three passing tests. Only the tripwire conjunct is at issue.
  • ONE COUPLING: SR-040 is one of the 17 rows carrying an unsigned derived-requirement label, and its label's whole justification is the clause under discussion. If this strikes, OI-34's population drops by one and the reason should be recorded there.
Options
  • (a) REBUILD THE TRIPWIRE inside the coordinator, against whatever the CURRENT resume surface is. FOR: the two hat lenses that reach the obligation are not answered by anything else, and they are real - a resume surface that grows without bound degrades an unattended run silently, and the lens that says a machine rather than a human is reading it at 3am is exactly right about this repo; and it keeps SR-040's hat-derived parentage intact rather than removing the row's only derived content. AGAINST: it is not a restore, it is a new requirement - the premise dissolved, so someone must first decide what surface is inherited and by whom, and that is a design question this item cannot answer for the owner; and it builds a warner for a growth problem nobody has yet observed on the current architecture, which is machinery ahead of evidence.
  • (b) STRIKE THE CLAUSE from SR-040's shall and acceptance, and let the two lenses be answered elsewhere or recorded as declined. FOR: it is the honest reading of what happened - the code was not deleted by accident, it was retired BECAUSE ITS PREMISE DISSOLVED, and a requirement whose subject has no referent is not an unmet obligation, it is a stale one; it costs no code and no test; and it removes the last thing standing between three already-Modified rows and their re-attest. AGAINST: it removes the row's entire hat-derived parentage, so two lenses that independently reached a real concern are answered by deletion; and if a resume surface DOES grow unbounded later, the record will show the obligation was struck rather than solved.
  • (c) RE-POINT TO ANOTHER CARRIER - adopt check_docs.check_status_surface as the tripwire's home. FOR: it exists, it warns, it reads a status surface against a declared threshold, and it costs nothing. AGAINST: REFUTED ON MEASUREMENT, not on judgement - different actor, different moment, different surface, already carrying another requirement, and INVERTED threshold semantics measured by exercising it on 0, -1, off and 200. Re-opening this means overturning a measurement, and doing it anyway would put one mechanism under two requirements that do not agree about what it must do.
  • (d) LEAVE IT STANDING as an open obligation with no carrier, as it is today. FOR: it is honestly labelled, and the row says plainly that nothing satisfies it. AGAINST: an unsatisfiable acceptance criterion holds three rows out of attestation indefinitely, and docs/status.md already names this as one of exactly two things the sitting must rule - leaving it is not a decision, it is the absence of one.
Recommendation

(b) STRIKE - and answer the two lenses explicitly in the same ruling rather than letting them disappear with the clause.

The deciding fact is the tombstone's own wording, and it is stronger than it first reads: the premise dissolved rather than the code moving. A requirement is unmet when the system fails to do something it should do. This clause is different in kind - its SUBJECT does not exist. There is no lane resume surface that a session inherits, so "warn when the lane resume surface exceeds its threshold" is not an obligation the system is failing; it is a sentence about an architecture that was replaced. Carrying it forward as an open obligation misdescribes the repo to every future reader, and rebuilding it would mean inventing a subject so the predicate has something to attach to - which is requirement-writing backwards.

(a) IS THE PRINCIPLED OBJECTION and it deserves a real answer rather than a dismissal: C-UNA-8 is right that at 3am the reader is another automated step, and a surface that grows without bound is a genuine failure mode for an unattended loop. The answer is that the concern survives the clause and should be RE-LODGED where the current architecture puts it. Today the resume surface is a GENERATED integrator artifact whose size the generator owns - so if the concern is to be carried, its home is the generator's own contract, not the coordinator's. That is a different row against a different subject, and it should be minted deliberately if the owner wants it, not smuggled in by keeping a dead clause alive.

SO THE RULING SHOULD DO TWO THINGS, not one: strike the clause, AND record whether the growth concern is re-lodged against the generator or declined. Striking alone leaves the record saying a hat lens was answered by deletion, which is the outcome the enforcement audit exists to make visible.

(c) SHOULD STAY REFUSED. It was investigated properly and refuted on measured evidence, including the inverted-threshold finding that was obtained by exercising the function rather than reading it. Overturning that needs new measurement, not a fresh opinion.

ONE CONSEQUENCE TO CARRY: if this strikes, SR-040's derived-requirement label loses its subject, since the label's justification IS the orphaned clause. Record that in OI-34's population rather than leaving a labelled row whose derived content is gone.

OI-39No SR states the CodeSymbol-anchor obligation - mint the requirement, or rule it not owed

One line

rule whether the LLR CodeSymbol anchor rule - the computation that decides when decomposition is FINISHED - is owed a stating SR, or stays a decision-registry rule with a code carrier; today it is normative only in OI-20's ruling, SR-147 states the carrier and SR-158's acceptance names only the verdict shape; the research round SPLIT the question - this row is the ENFORCED registry-to-code direction, the unenforced code-to-registry one is now OI-42 - and the recommendation is (a) MINT, written against the obligation rather than against Python or the checker, with NO back-link obligation minted here and the LLR owed either way (evidence: docs/knowledge/traceability-enforcement.md)

What is being decided

DISAMBIGUATION FIRST, BECAUSE THE RESEARCH ROUND FOUND THE ROW AND THE REQUEST ARE ABOUT TWO DIFFERENT OBLIGATIONS. "Traceability between code and requirements" names TWO directions, and they are not the same problem:

  • REGISTRY -> CODE. A DESIGN ROW NAMES A REALIZATION SYMBOL AND A CHECKER VERIFIES IT BINDS. This is ASSERT-AND-VERIFY: a human writes a falsifiable claim in the registry and a machine falsifies it. It is what check_doc_refs.symbol_findings does, it is hard under --strict, and IT IS WHAT EVERYTHING BELOW IN THIS ROW IS ABOUT.
  • CODE -> REGISTRY. A SOURCE COMMENT CITES A REQUIREMENT ID (Implements: SR-007, LLR-014). This is an ANNOTATION CONVENTION. It is mandated unconditionally in two SHIPPED documents, it is enforced by NOTHING, and it is adhered to in the kit's own scripts at 2 of 781 public symbols.

THE PHRASING THAT SENT THIS TO RESEARCH - code carrying back-links to its requirements - NAMES THE SECOND. OI-39'S OWN TEXT IS ENTIRELY THE FIRST. Reading them as one question is how a ruling here would silently mint an obligation nobody weighed: the anchor rule is the enforced direction and the Implements: mandate is the unenforced one, and they need separate answers. THE SECOND DIRECTION IS NOW ITS OWN ROW, OI-42, so this row can be ruled on its own merits. The evidence for both is in docs/knowledge/traceability-enforcement.md (reviewed 2026-08-18) and is not restated here.

WHY THE SPLIT IS EVIDENTIAL RATHER THAN TIDY-MINDED: the published evidence points OPPOSITE WAYS on the two directions. Verified assertion is the strong instrument (recovered links are not gate-grade: best classical IR F is about 48.7 across the standard benchmarks, and LLM recovery is no better and worse under a realistic candidate distribution). Code-side annotation is the weak one (no standard requires the representation, no surveyed commercial tool requires it, and every available proxy says such conventions are not maintained). A single ruling covering both would take the evidence for one and apply it to the other.

THE MECHANISM EXISTS, IS HARD-GATED, AND NO REQUIREMENT STATES IT.

THE RULE, as implemented in check_doc_refs.symbol_findings (:508-631) and stated in its own docstring (:512-514): "a live LLR row must carry at least ONE identifier-shaped CodeSymbol token that BINDS in one of the .py modules its Module cell names. Everything else about the cell is filed untraced." CodeSymbol supplies the tokens, Module supplies the search scope, and a ;-joined module list is read as a union. The verdict split (:606-630): no identifiers -> untraced; no token binds -> dangling, which GATES; some bind -> anchored, misses counted as untraced.

ITS WEIGHT IS STATED IN THE MODULE ITSELF (check_doc_refs.py:537-543) and is why this is not a bookkeeping question: "THE WEIGHT OF THIS RULE - IT IS THE DECOMPOSITION FLOOR. Under OI-21's ladder, rung 4 (LLR definition) TERMINATES WHERE A TOKEN BINDS. ... So this is not only a check on a cell - it is the computation that decides when decomposition is FINISHED, which is why it is hard under --strict (an advisory version would make D-9's Founded rung vacuous for one of four tiers)."

WHERE IT IS NORMATIVE TODAY: open-items.toml OI-20, ruled by the owner 2026-08-13, option (a) RATIFY AS BUILT. That is an OWNER DECISION REGISTRY ROW. It is authoritative and it is not a requirement - nothing traces to it, no LLR decomposes it, no TC verifies it, and it does not appear in any coverage arithmetic.

THE TWO SRs THAT LOOK LIKE THEY MIGHT COVER IT DO NOT, verified cell by cell:

  • SR-147 "One machine-parseable carrier for the requirement spine" (:549) states one obligation about REPRESENTATION - every spine tier in one machine-parseable form, reached by a migration proven cell-for-cell. Its acceptance (:551) is entirely round-trip migration fidelity. Neither cell mentions symbols, binding, CodeSymbol or discharge. IF-117's own notes concede it: "SR-147 ... does not state a symbol-binding oracle either."
  • SR-158 "Documentation drift reds or warns per its declared tier" (:672) has as its subject "a documentation surface that has drifted from what it claims" - and an LLR registry row is not a documentation surface. Its acceptance (:674) carries the one clause that touches this - "the reference and figure checks gate at the release bar, gating on dangling references alone, with untraced ones counted and never gating" - which states HOW A FINDING IS GRADED, never that an LLR must anchor to real code. Its design child LLR-038 covers the path and sym: tier only (findings_for/load_symbol_oracle/is_path_shaped) and NAMES NEITHER symbol_findings NOR module_bindings.

So the strongest checker in the repo - the one that decides when a tier is finished - has no SR and no LLR.

HOW THE GAP SURFACED. IF-117 is the seam behind the rule: check_doc_refs consumes gen_arch_map.module_bindings(tree) rather than reading the rendered arch-map, deliberately, because the committed map is a PUBLIC-API view that drops _-prefixed names and module constants, and 41 of this repo's live LLR rows name exactly those - reading the artifact would report real code as missing. An earlier batch re-point assigned IF-117's owner cell to LLR-023 by matching on MODULE NAME (gen_arch_map) rather than on contract; LLR-023 covers build_map/splice_region, i.e. the rendered arch-map, which is precisely what IF-117's contract disclaims. The owner reverted the pick to the req_refs SR on 2026-08-17 and FLAGGED THE RESIDUE rather than forcing an owner: no SR states the obligation itself. The adversarial round that caught it (docs/reviews/retier-v2/ROUND-2-SOL-TERRA.md:79, F4) calls it "a module-name match, not an answerable owner", and puts IF-117 in a class of 19 such picks, several of which are "merely co-located-module candidates".

The visible symptom is that IF-117's owner cell holds an SR where an LLR normally sits - the registry itself is signalling that no design row answers for this seam.

OWNER DIRECTION, 2026-08-18, AND WHERE IT LEAVES THE ROW - not a ruling, and the row stays pending.

THE OWNER ASKED FOR RESEARCH BEFORE RULING, AND LEANS TOWARD MINTING - which is (a). A RESEARCH BRIEFING IS IN FLIGHT; this row waits on it rather than on the sitting's calendar.

THE OWNER'S CONSTRAINT ON ANY ANSWER, AND IT IS BINDING RATHER THAN ADVISORY: IT MUST BE LANGUAGE-AGNOSTIC, NOT PYTHON-SPECIFIC. That constraint reaches further than the wording of one shall. The rule as implemented is Python-shaped in two places at once - symbol_findings searches .py modules, and "identifier-shaped" is a Python identifier - and the kit's own doctrine is a stack-agnostic core with a Python-first REFERENCE. So a minted SR must state the obligation in terms every stack can satisfy (a design row's named symbol RESOLVES in its named implementation unit) and leave the resolution mechanism to the declared stack profile, exactly as the harness commands already are. A row that says .py would ship a Python requirement to every adopter.

THIS SHARPENS THE BRIEF'S OWN FIRST GUARD rather than adding a new one: "write the SR against the OBLIGATION, not the checker." The language-agnosticism constraint is what makes that guard testable - if the shall can be read by an adopter whose implementation units are not Python files, it was written against the obligation; if it cannot, it was written against check_doc_refs.

UNCHANGED BY THE DIRECTION: the LLR is owed under either ruling. check_doc_refs.symbol_findings is a hard --strict gate with no design row, and that is a gap on the repo's own terms whichever way the SR question goes.

Blast radius

ADDITIVE, AND CHEAPER THAN IT LOOKS ON THE GATE - the cost is in the chain, not the ratification.

  • THE GATE DOES NOT MOVE. STATUS_VALUES is {Drafted, Approved, Modified} (trace.py:394), and the derived gate is the MIN over every in-scope row's own bar. The live basis is drafted=74 modified=151 over 390 spine rows with the computed value already floored. A 391st row at Drafted changes nothing.
  • IF-117 IS Drafted AND WAS NEVER ATTESTED, so re-pointing its req_refs/owner costs no re-attestation. SR-147 is Modified and SR-158 is Drafted - both already outside attestation.
  • THE REAL COST IS THE CHAIN: a new SR needs an sn_refs parent (SN-002 or SN-010 are the candidates), at least one LLR naming check_doc_refs.symbol_findings - which today has NO design row at all - and a TC. That LLR is arguably owed regardless of how this rules, because a hard-gating checker with no design row is a gap on its own terms.
  • RULING IT NOT OWED costs nothing mechanically and leaves the decomposition floor permanently un-required: the rule that decides when rung 4 terminates would be stated only in a decision registry, so a downstream adopter reading the requirement spine would find the ladder's terminating condition nowhere in it.
  • DOWNSTREAM: the rule is shipped machinery (check_doc_refs.py is in MAPPING), so an SR stating it is a kit-level requirement, not a this-repo note - which is an argument for minting it, since adopters inherit the gate without inheriting any statement of what it demands.

WHAT THE RESEARCH ROUND ADDS TO THE BLAST RADIUS, and it CHANGES THE SIZE OF (a) RATHER THAN ITS DIRECTION - full evidence in docs/knowledge/traceability-enforcement.md:

  • THE OBLIGATION IS SATISFIABLE OFF THE SHELF IN ANY LANGUAGE, so the owner's language-agnosticism constraint costs an adopter nothing. universal-ctags carries about 150 parsers, emits JSON Lines the stdlib parses, ships prebuilt Windows binaries, is GPL-2.0, and REQUIRES NO ANNOTATION FROM THE ADOPTER - it answers "does this name exist in this unit" for a stack the kit has never seen. (One sharp edge for whoever implements: BSD ctags also answers to ctags, so a probe must match the literal string "Universal Ctags".) Doxygen 1.16.0 (Jan 2026) goes further with NATIVE \requirement/\satisfies/\verifies, a bidirectional requirements.xml and dangling-reference warnings that can fail a build - but only about 12 languages. So an SR written against the OBLIGATION has a credible discharge path per stack profile; it is not a promissory note.
  • NO STANDARD CONSTRAINS THE REPRESENTATION, which is what lets the SR be written at the obligation altitude honestly. DO-178C section 11.21 makes Trace Data a lifecycle data item requiring bidirectional LLR-to-source associations - purpose: no orphan or dead code - and specifies NO representation; Trace Data is enumerated ALONGSIDE Source Code, not inside it. IEC 62304's normative chain omits source code entirely. (ISO 26262 and IEC 61508 could NOT be verified from primary text and are recorded as unchecked, not as support.)
  • WHAT DOES NOT MOVE: nothing here reaches the Implements: mandate, gen_arch_map.implements(), or any code comment. Those are OI-42, and the two rulings are independent in both directions.
Options
  • (a) MINT THE SR - one requirement stating the anchor obligation (an LLR is discharged when at least one identifier-shaped CodeSymbol token binds within its named modules), with an LLR naming check_doc_refs.symbol_findings and a TC, and IF-117's req_refs/owner re-pointed onto it. FOR: it puts the ladder's TERMINATING CONDITION inside the requirement spine, where the ladder's other rungs already live - a decomposition floor stated only in a decision registry is the one rung a reader of the spine cannot find; it gives IF-117 an answerable owner, retiring a flagged residue instead of carrying it; and it supplies a design row for a HARD-GATING checker that has none, which is a gap regardless of this ruling. AGAINST: it is a three-row addition plus an IF re-point during a program that is trying to close, and it mints a requirement for behaviour that already ships and already gates - so the row documents rather than shapes, which is the accretion pattern this repo has ruled against before.
  • (b) RULE IT NOT OWED - the anchor rule stays an owner decision (OI-20, ratified as built) with a code carrier, and IF-117's flag is struck. FOR: OI-20 IS a ruling, and a ruled decision with a working implementation is not obviously improved by a requirement restating it; the spine does not need a row for every mechanism, and proportionality (SN-012) argues against minting one for a rule nobody disputes. AGAINST: this rule is not "a mechanism" - it is the computation that decides when a tier is FINISHED, and the module says so. Leaving it out means the requirement spine cannot answer "when is decomposition done?" from its own contents, which is the one question a decomposition floor exists to answer; and it leaves a --strict-gating checker with no requirement and no design row.
  • (c) FOLD IT INTO SR-158 by widening that row's subject to cover registry-cell anchoring as one more documentation-drift class. FOR: smallest addition - no new id, and the checker already lives in check_doc_refs. AGAINST: it breaks the one-obligation rule on a row that currently satisfies it. SR-158's subject is "a documentation surface that has drifted from what it claims"; an LLR row is a specification, not a documentation surface, and the anchor rule's verdict is not drift - it is a decomposition-completeness judgement. Folding them would put two decisions in one row, which is the tiering defect PROCESS.md §3 names explicitly.
  • (d) DEFER PAST THE SITTING. FOR: keeps the sitting's scope small, and nothing is broken today. AGAINST: IF-117 stays flagged and its owner cell keeps holding an SR where an LLR belongs, which reads to every later reader as an un-migrated row rather than a declared gap.
  • (e) WIDEN THE MINT TO BOTH DIRECTIONS - one SR obliging the registry-to-code anchor AND a code-to-requirement back-link. FOR: it is the reading the raising phrase invites, and it would put the Implements: convention on the spine where the enforcement audit says it belongs. AGAINST: REFUSED ON MEASURED EVIDENCE, and this is the option the research round exists to kill. The two directions have OPPOSITE evidence (knowledge pack, "The two directions are not the same problem"): the anchor rule is verified assertion, while code-side annotation is required by NO standard, by NO surveyed commercial tool, and is adhered to in this repo's own scripts at 2 of 781 public symbols - so widening the row would mint an obligation the author of the row is already failing at 99.7%, in the same breath as one it already meets. It also breaks the one-obligation rule as squarely as (c) does. The second direction is OI-42's, and it is not obviously owed at all.
Recommendation

(a) MINT THE SR FOR THE ENFORCED DIRECTION ONLY - written against the OBLIGATION, not against Python and not against check_doc_refs - and DO NOT MINT A CODE-TO-REQUIREMENT BACK-LINK OBLIGATION. The LLR is owed under either answer.

THE FIRST THING THE RULING MUST DO IS PICK A DIRECTION. If the sitting rules "yes, mint traceability between code and requirements" without saying which direction, the implementer has to guess, and the two guesses have opposite evidence behind them. So the recommendation is stated as three separate answers.

MINT, FOR REGISTRY -> CODE. The deciding argument is the module's own claim about its weight, taken literally: this is the computation that decides when rung 4 of the ladder TERMINATES. Every other rung's terminating condition is stated in the spine. If this one is stated only in an owner decision registry, then the spine - the artifact whose entire purpose is to say what the system must do and how you know it is done - cannot answer the question for one of its four tiers. That is a hole in the thing itself, not a documentation nicety. The research round STRENGTHENS this rather than complicating it: verified assertion is the instrument the evidence actually supports. Automated link RECOVERY is not gate-grade (best classical IR averages F about 48.7 across the eleven standard benchmarks; LLM recovery is no better - LiSSA's best req-to-code F1 is .322 against VSM's .282 - and collapses under a realistic candidate distribution, P@1 32.5 to 14.4, with plain VSM winning). What this repo already does is the strong form: a human writes a falsifiable claim and a machine falsifies it. The row would state the strongest available mechanism, not aspire to a weak one.

WRITE IT AGAINST THE OBLIGATION, WHICH THE OWNER'S CONSTRAINT MAKES TESTABLE. The shall says that A DESIGN ROW'S NAMED SYMBOL RESOLVES IN ITS NAMED IMPLEMENTATION UNIT, with the resolution mechanism left to the declared stack profile - exactly as the harness commands already are. Not .py, not "identifier-shaped" in the Python sense, and not "check_doc_refs reports it" (which would name its own carrier and trip the altitude rule). The constraint is affordable rather than aspirational: universal-ctags answers "does this name exist in this unit" for about 150 languages, from JSON Lines the stdlib parses, with prebuilt Windows binaries and NO annotation required of the adopter. So the obligation-level wording has a real discharge path per stack, and DO-178C section 11.21 backs the altitude - it requires the ASSOCIATION and specifies no representation at all.

DO NOT MINT THE BACK-LINK OBLIGATION IN THIS ROW, and this is a positive recommendation rather than a deferral.
