Requiring source comments to cite requirement ids is required by no standard surveyed, by no commercial tool surveyed (Polarion OFFERS in-source tags for three languages; none requires them), and every available proxy says conventions of that shape are not maintained - about 48% of commits untagged in six Apache projects CHOSEN for good discipline, 13-20% of code changes triggering a comment update. This repo is its own strongest evidence: the mandate is stated unconditionally in two SHIPPED documents and adhered to at 2 of 781 public symbols in project-trajectory/scripts/*.py. Minting an SR for it would put a 0.26%-adhered, zero-enforced convention on the spine. That question is OI-42's, where it can be answered on its own terms.

THE ACCRETION OBJECTION IN (b) still deserves a head-on answer. It is true that this row would document behaviour that already ships, and this repo has correctly refused rows like that before. The difference is what the row would be ABOUT: a row restating "check X exists" is accretion; a row stating the CONDITION UNDER WHICH A DESIGN TIER IS COMPLETE is what a requirement spine is for. And the evidence that it is missing is not theoretical - a batch re-point assigned IF-117 an owner whose artifact its contract explicitly disclaims, and a module-name match was the best available answer BECAUSE no row states the obligation.

THE LLR IS OWED UNDER EITHER RULING and should not wait for this one. check_doc_refs.symbol_findings is a hard --strict gate with no design row anywhere. Even if the sitting rules (b) and leaves the obligation with OI-20, a gating checker carrying no design row is a gap on the repo's own terms - and it is the cheapest half of the work.

TWO GUARDS IF (a) IS RULED:

  • RE-POINT IF-117 IN THE SAME CHANGE. The residue exists because the seam's owner cell holds an SR as a placeholder; minting the SR without moving the pointer leaves the same flag with a better excuse. And record that the module-match class is wider than this one row - the round-2 review named 19 such picks, several of them co-located-module candidates rather than answerable owners.
  • STATE IN THE RULING THAT THE OTHER DIRECTION WAS CONSIDERED AND LEFT TO OI-42. Otherwise the next reader finds an SR about code-and-requirement traceability and reasonably infers the back-link mandate was ratified with it.

OI-40LLR-037 + TC-040 - the tripwire pair: rebuilt with SR-040's clause, or corrected shut

One line

rule the design/test half of SR-040's retired tripwire - both rows were corrected on 2026-08-18 against the live code after LLR-037 was found publishing a DELETED symbol and TC-040 was found Approved while claiming a clause no test performs; the pair follows OI-38 and cannot settle before it; recommendation is (b) correct shut, tracking OI-38's strike

What is being decided

WHAT THE PAIR SAYS TODAY, after the 2026-08-18 correction.

LLR-037 (low-level-requirements.toml:388-398) carries the live half of SR-040 - cmd_map parsed by parse_map, session_template(phase) falling back to AGENT_CMD, every map entry preflighted, the review policy read and printed in the banner - over module = agent_loop.py with code_symbol = "session_template/parse_map/read_declared". All three bind: session_template is defined at agent_loop.py:646; parse_map (:231) and read_declared (:203) are module-scope re-exports from agent_common, which gen_arch_map.module_bindings sees, so the anchor rule passes.

Its second paragraph (:394) records what was removed and why: the cell used to describe status_size_warning as "pure and warn-only (limit<=0 disables)" for A SYMBOL THAT NO LONGER EXISTS. In the row's own words, "an Approved design row publishing a deleted symbol into the generated knowledge bundle is worse than a gap, because it reads as a carrier."

TC-040 (test-cases.toml:419-429) verifies SR-040 and LLR-037 at Integration level. Its method (:423) records the matching correction: it "claimed a fourth clause - 'a bloated status.md warns without blocking' - that NO test in the evidence file performs, and the row was Approved while claiming it."

THE MECHANISM THAT HID IT IS THE MOST TRANSFERABLE FINDING HERE, and it is stated in TC-040's own cell: "The file-granular evidence cell is what hid it: naming a 63-test file rather than the functions leaves a stated clause and an absent test indistinguishable." The evidence cell is now function-granular - tests/test_agent_loop.py::test_cmd_map_broken_entry_fails_preflight, ::test_review_policy_surfaced_in_banner, ::test_model_map_entry_without_equals_fails_preflight - and all three pass:

$ .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q tests/test_agent_loop.py -k "test_cmd_map_broken_entry_fails_preflight or test_review_policy_surfaced_in_banner or test_model_map_entry_without_equals_fails_preflight" 3 passed, 62 deselected

BUT PASSING IS NOT VERIFYING SR-040, and TC-040's expected cell says so in terms: "the resume-surface tripwire clause is unverified BY CONSTRUCTION because it has no carrier". Grepping tests/test_agent_loop.py for the tripwire returns nothing. The pair is green AND honestly incomplete, which is exactly why both are Modified rather than Approved.

WHAT IS ACTUALLY BEING RULED HERE. Both cells currently carry a paragraph whose only job is to RECORD THE GAP - LLR-037's "THE SIZE-TRIPWIRE HALF IS GONE FROM THIS ROW" and TC-040's "CORRECTED 2026-08-18". Those paragraphs are provenance in living cells, allow-listed together in docs/provenance-allow ("ruled together or not at all") precisely because deleting them would erase the only note that the question is open. So this item decides two things at once: whether the tripwire's design and test are REBUILT, and whether those two recording paragraphs come out.

THE DEPENDENCY IS STRICT AND SHOULD NOT BE FUDGED: this pair is the design and test half of the clause OI-38 rules on. It cannot resolve first, and it cannot resolve differently. If OI-38 strikes the clause, the correct answer here is (b); if OI-38 rebuilds it, the correct answer is (a). This row exists because the pair carries its own decisions - what LLR-037's cell should say, whether TC-040's fourth clause returns, and what evidence granularity is required - which the parent ruling does not answer by itself.

Blast radius

SMALL AND ALREADY-PAID ON RATIFICATION, with one durable consequence worth ruling separately.

  • Both rows are already Modified (they flipped from Approved on 2026-08-18 as part of the correction), so the re-attest window is OPEN and any ruling here CLOSES it rather than opening one. SR-040 is Modified too.
  • CORRECTING SHUT costs one reviewed Status-change commit and no code: the two recording paragraphs go, the two docs/provenance-allow entries retire with them, and both rows re-attest Modified -> Approved on evidence that already passes. Nothing is written, nothing is deleted from the delivered code.
  • REBUILDING costs a new coordinator function, a code_symbol token added to LLR-037, the restored design sentence, TC-040's fourth method clause, a new function-granular evidence entry, and the test behind it - and it inherits OI-38's prior question of WHAT SURFACE the tripwire watches, since the tombstone says no session inherits status.md any more.
  • THE DURABLE CONSEQUENCE, independent of which way this goes: file-granular test evidence is what made a false Approved invisible. TC-040 is now function-granular, and nothing requires any other TC to be. That is a general finding about the evidence cell, discovered here, and it should be recorded where it applies rather than left as a fact about one row.
  • Under both options the corrected halves STAY corrected. Nobody is proposing to restore LLR-037's deleted-symbol sentence or TC-040's untested clause as they stood; the question is whether a REBUILT carrier earns them back.
Options
  • (a) REBUILD - track OI-38's rebuild: a new warner in the coordinator, LLR-037's design half restored against it with the new symbol named, TC-040's fourth method clause re-added with its own function-granular evidence and the test written to back it. FOR: it is the only option that leaves SR-040's clause verified rather than declared unverifiable, and it closes the pair honestly - a design row and a test that describe something real; the two hat lenses behind the clause (C-UXE-2, C-UNA-8) keep an answer. AGAINST: it is downstream of a design question nobody has answered - what the current resume surface IS - so it cannot start until OI-38 rules rebuild AND that question is settled; and it re-flips two rows that are one commit from being attestable.
  • (b) CORRECT SHUT - track OI-38's strike: delete the recording paragraphs from both cells, retire the two allow entries, and re-attest both rows on the evidence that already passes. FOR: the rows then say only what is true, which is what a design row and a test case are for; the three evidence tests already pass function-granularly, so nothing is claimed that is not checked; and it removes two provenance markers by resolving what they mark, which is exactly how the allow file says its entries should end. AGAINST: it closes the pair by removing the obligation rather than meeting it, and the record will show a design tier and a test tier that both narrowed to fit the code - which is the right move when the premise dissolved and a bad habit if it becomes routine.
  • (c) SPLIT THE PAIR - correct TC-040 shut now (its tests pass) and hold LLR-037 for the rebuild. FOR: it unblocks the half that is genuinely finished. AGAINST: REFUSED BY THE ALLOW FILE'S OWN TERMS - the two entries say "ruled together or not at all", and for a reason: a test case narrowed to what it verifies while its design row still claims an unbuilt carrier is precisely the mismatch that produced the false Approved in the first place, one tier down.
  • (d) LEAVE BOTH Modified pending the rebuild indefinitely. FOR: honest, and costs nothing today. AGAINST: it holds two rows out of attestation on a decision that is already the sitting's to make, and an indefinite hold is how a recorded gap becomes a permanent one.
Recommendation

(b) CORRECT SHUT, tracking OI-38's strike - and rule the two together in one sitting rather than in sequence.

The reasoning is OI-38's and does not need re-deriving here: the tripwire's premise dissolved, so its design row and its test have nothing to describe. What this item adds is the observation that THE PAIR IS ALREADY IN THE RIGHT STATE and is being held open only by the paragraphs that record the gap. LLR-037 now carries what the module actually does; TC-040 now claims only what its three named test functions perform, and they pass. Remove the two recording paragraphs and both rows are attestable on evidence that already exists. That is a genuinely clean close, not a papered-over one.

IF OI-38 RULES REBUILD, THIS BECOMES (a) AUTOMATICALLY and no separate deliberation is needed - which is why the two should be ruled in one sitting. The one thing a rebuild must not do is restore the old text: the deleted symbol and the untested clause were wrong on their own terms, independent of whether the tripwire returns.

THE FINDING WORTH KEEPING IS NOT ABOUT THIS PAIR AT ALL, and it should be ruled explicitly rather than left in a cell: FILE-GRANULAR TEST EVIDENCE HIDES A FALSE GREEN. TC-040 was Approved while stating a method clause no test performed, and it stayed that way because its evidence cell named a 63-test file instead of the functions. Any TC whose evidence names a file rather than test functions can carry the same defect today, and nothing checks it. Two cheap responses are available and neither depends on how this rules - a census of TCs with file-granular evidence, and a warn-first rule that a TC's evidence cell names test functions. Recording that here, on the row where it was found, is the minimum; ruling it is better.

WHAT SHOULD NOT HAPPEN: (c), the split. The allow file says these two are ruled together or not at all, and the reason is exactly the failure this pair demonstrated - a test narrowed to reality while its design row still claims an unbuilt carrier is the same mismatch one tier down.

OI-41A generated view can be perfectly FRESH and carry nothing - what makes open-items.toml the single point of review it is meant to be

One line

rule what mechanism reinforces that EVERY deferred-to-the-owner decision reaches open-items.toml - the owner's own question, raised because the ten briefs of 2026-08-18 only appeared when they asked; today the generated view can report itself up to date while carrying zero pending rows and ten genuine decisions sit on other surfaces; recommendation is (a) a defer-phrase checker that demands a resolving OI-###, with the allow file's grammar as its hardest arm

What is being decided

THE OWNER'S QUESTION, VERBATIM, BECAUSE IT IS THE ROW'S SUBJECT: "Why did these only show up on open-items when I prompted? Is there some other mechanism / reference that could help reinforce that ideally all items surface in open-items.toml for single point review?"

THE FAILURE IS NOT STALENESS, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE POINT. A GENERATED VIEW CAN BE PERFECTLY FRESH AND STILL CARRY NOTHING. Before 2026-08-18, gen_open_items --check reported UP TO DATE. It was telling the truth: docs/open-items.html matched its regeneration exactly. The registry behind it held 22 rows - 21 ruled and one pending, and that one was OI-000, the inert example placeholder. So the owner surface rendered No pending decision - the owner queue is empty while approximately TEN GENUINE OWNER DECISIONS were live in the repo, sitting in log fragments, docs/provenance-allow entries and session reports.

EVERY FRESHNESS GATE IN THIS REPO ASKS THE SAME QUESTION - does the artifact match its regeneration - and NONE of them asks whether the SOURCE was populated. A view derived from an empty registry is a correct view of an empty registry. The check cannot tell an empty queue from an unrecorded one, and the reassuring word it prints is the same in both cases.

THE STRONGEST LEAD, AND IT IS ALREADY IN THE TREE: SEVERAL SURFACES ALREADY ANNOUNCE THE DEFERRAL IN A DETECTABLE PHRASE. They are not silent about owing an open item; they say so, in words, and nothing reads the words.

  • docs/provenance-allow is the clearest case. Its header states the rule for the whole file - "Each row below owes an open-item row at the sitting; when the ruling lands, the marker and the entry go together" - and NINETEEN of its 34 entry lines repeat it verbatim in their own reason text: "Owes an open-item row at the sitting." Nineteen written promises of an open-item row, and until 2026-08-18 not one of them had one. The file's grammar is already parsed (<ROW-ID> <Cell> <token> then - <reason>), and tests/test_trace.py already asserts every entry still bites, so this surface has machinery, a schema and a test - and no field for the thing it keeps promising.
  • REGISTRY CELLS carry the same tell in three other spellings, each on a live row: OPEN QUESTION FOR THE SITTING (SR-043's rationale, SN-006's acceptance), FLAGGED FOR THE OWNER (IF-117's notes), and OPEN RULING (external.toml's header, on the mis-seeded B/REL watermarks).
  • THAT LAST ONE IS THE SHARPEST INSTANCE AVAILABLE and it is still live as this row is written: the B-08/REL-004 watermark correction is named as an OPEN RULING in external.toml and listed as item 1 of "What the sitting still has to RULE" in docs/status.md, and IT HAS NO OI-### ROW. A decision the repo announces twice, on two surfaces, in the owner's own working document - and it is invisible to the surface built for exactly this.

WHY THE PHRASES MATTER MORE THAN THEY LOOK. A rule that says "file an open item when you defer a decision" is a rule with no enforcer, and this repo's own enforcement audit records that prose is the weakest tier. But the authors here DID NOT SKIP THE PROSE - they wrote the deferral down, in a consistent form, on a live surface. The gap is not authorial discipline; it is that the announcement and the queue are two unconnected artifacts. That is a mechanizable gap rather than a cultural one, which is what makes this rulable.

THE HONEST LIMIT, STATED UP FRONT: no mechanism below catches a decision that was deferred SILENTLY - taken by nobody, written nowhere, noticed by no one. The phrase is the tell, and an author who defers without a tell is invisible to every option here. What is achievable is that a deferral ANNOUNCED ANYWHERE resolves to a row; the remainder is genuinely reviewer-tier and should be said so rather than papered over.

Blast radius

SMALL MECHANICALLY, AND IT DECIDES WHAT A SURFACE MEANS - which is the larger half.

  • (a) is one new advisory arm reading live surfaces for a DECLARED phrase vocabulary and requiring a resolving OI-###. It is the same shape trace_text.provenance_tokens already ships - a declared list of prose shapes, matched over cells - so the technique is inherited rather than invented, and the reader has a precedent for how such a warning behaves.
  • (b) is a grammar change to docs/provenance-allow: an entry gains a required OI-### field. That is a small parser edit plus 19 entries to fill, and it makes the promise UNREPRESENTABLE-WITHOUT-A-ROW rather than merely detected. It is kit-shipped (the file is scaffolded), so it is a RESYNC entry and every adopter's allow file gains a field.
  • (c) is a vacuity arm inside gen_open_items --check: report "0 pending rows while other surfaces defer" as a FINDING rather than as up-to-date. Cheapest of all, and it changes what a green from that check MEANS - which is a behaviour change to a step every commit runs.
  • NO SPINE COST UNDER ANY OPTION: nothing here moves a status cell, mints or retires a spine id, or opens a re-attest window. The cost is one advisory class and, under (b), one downstream migration.
  • THE COST OF DOING NOTHING is measured rather than hypothetical: ten decisions, of which the ten briefs of 2026-08-18 are the recovered set, plus at least one still-unrecovered instance (the B/REL watermark ruling). And the owner found it, not the repo - which is the failure mode a single point of review exists to prevent.
Options
  • (a) A DEFER-PHRASE CHECKER - scan the live surfaces (the registries, docs/provenance-allow, docs/status.md) for a DECLARED vocabulary of defer-to-owner phrases ("owes an open-item row", "OPEN QUESTION FOR THE SITTING", "the sitting must rule", "FLAGGED FOR THE OWNER", "OPEN RULING") and report any occurrence with no resolving OI-### nearby. FOR: it is the only option that reaches EVERY surface rather than one, and the four live instances outside the allow file are exactly the ones a per-file grammar cannot see; the technique already ships here, so the failure modes are known; and the vocabulary is DECLARED, which makes it reviewable and extendable rather than a guess. AGAINST: it is a phrase matcher, so it is evadable by wording - an author who writes "the owner should look at this" is caught by nothing - and it will produce false positives on prose that QUOTES the phrase (this very row does, four times), which needs an exemption story before it is anything but noise.
  • (b) REQUIRE AN OI-### IN THE ALLOW-FILE ENTRY GRAMMAR - an entry that says it owes an open-item row must NAME the row, and an entry naming a nonexistent or non-pending OI is a finding. FOR: it makes the bad state UNREPRESENTABLE on the surface where 19 of the instances live, which is this repo's stated preference over detection; the file already has a parsed grammar and a test asserting every entry bites, so the machinery is already there; there are no false positives, because the requirement is a field rather than a phrase; and it closes the loop in the other direction too - when a ruling lands, the entry names the row that ruled it, so retiring the marker and the entry together stops being a memory exercise. AGAINST: it covers ONE surface. The allow file is where the deferrals are DENSEST, not where they are only. It would not have caught SR-043's open question, IF-117's flag, or the B/REL open ruling - three live instances on three different surfaces.
  • (c) A VACUITY CHECK ON THE GENERATED VIEW - gen_open_items --check reports "0 pending" as a FINDING when other surfaces are deferring, instead of reporting up to date. FOR: it attacks the exact thing that went wrong - a green that meant "the view matches its source" being read as "the queue is empty" - and it is the only option that would fire even for a phrase the vocabulary does not know, because it compares a COUNT against a signal rather than matching text; it is a handful of lines. AGAINST: it names nothing actionable - "something, somewhere, defers" is a finding a reader cannot discharge without doing the search by hand; it depends on the same phrase detection as (a) to know that other surfaces defer, so it is not independent of it; and a zero-pending queue is a legitimate state that will now need an explicit way to be declared, or the check becomes a permanent warning that gets silenced.
  • (d) PROSE ONLY - state in PROCESS.md and the session protocol that a deferred decision is filed as an OI row before the session ends. FOR: zero mechanism; and it addresses the general case that no detector can reach, which is the silent deferral. AGAINST: the 19 allow-file entries prove the prose rule was ALREADY UNDERSTOOD and already written down - the authors said in terms that a row was owed - and it still did not happen. A rule whose stated form is already being followed, and whose outcome is still absent, is not short of prose.
Recommendation

(a) THE DEFER-PHRASE CHECKER, WARN-FIRST, WITH (b) AS ITS HARDEST ARM - one recommendation with two teeth, not two recommendations.

THE DECIDING ARGUMENT IS COVERAGE AGAINST WHERE THE EVIDENCE ACTUALLY IS. (b) is the more satisfying mechanism and it is genuinely stronger where it applies: a required field is a constraint, a phrase match is a detector, and this repo prefers the former every time. But (b) sees ONE FILE, and the instance that matters most right now is not in it. The B/REL watermark correction is announced as an OPEN RULING in external.toml and again as item 1 of the sitting's rule-list in docs/status.md, and it has no OI row TODAY - after a sweep that was looking for exactly this. A mechanism that would not have caught the one live miss remaining is not the answer to the owner's question, which was about ALL items reaching one surface.

SO TAKE (a) AS THE RULE AND (b) AS ITS FIRST ARM. Where a surface has a grammar, encode the requirement AS A FIELD - that is the allow file, and 19 entries fill in one pass. Where a surface has only prose - registry cells, docs/status.md - match the declared vocabulary and warn. The two are the same rule at two enforcement strengths, which is exactly the pattern the enforcement audit already uses to describe every other rule here.

WARN-FIRST IS NOT TIMIDITY, IT IS THE FALSE-POSITIVE STORY. The vocabulary will fire on prose that QUOTES a phrase rather than deferring - this row does it four times, and any log fragment describing the rule will do it again. Running warn-first for a program is how the exemption shape gets designed from real hits instead of guessed at. Promotion to error is its own act, and the allow-file arm (b) can be hard IMMEDIATELY, because a missing field has no false positives.

(c) SHOULD BE BUILT, AND NOT AS THE ANSWER. The vacuity finding is four lines and it fixes the specific misreading that started this - a check saying "up to date" about an empty queue. But it cannot know that other surfaces defer without (a)'s detection, so it is a PRESENTATION of (a)'s result rather than an alternative to it. Fold it in; do not rule it alone.

(d) IS REFUSED ON ITS OWN EVIDENCE, and this is the cleanest refutation available anywhere in this registry: 19 allow-file entries state, in their authors' own words, that the row owes an open item. The prose rule was understood, agreed, and WRITTEN DOWN NINETEEN TIMES. It produced zero rows. Adding a twentieth statement of it is not a plan.

THE LIMIT, RESTATED SO THE RULING DOES NOT OVERCLAIM: none of this catches a decision deferred with no words at all. What it buys is that a deferral ANNOUNCED anywhere resolves to a row - which is what actually failed here, since every one of the ten was announced somewhere and none of them was queued.

OI-42The `Implements:` back-link mandate - shipped unconditionally, adhered to at 0.26%, enforced by nothing, and feeding a harvester that fabricates links from prose

One line

rule the CODE-to-REGISTRY direction that OI-39 is NOT about - two SHIPPED documents require Implements: SR-/LLR- on every public symbol, the kit's own scripts carry it on 2 of 781, nothing checks it, and gen_arch_map.implements() fills the map's back-link column 97% from undeclared prose (SR-9, SR-10, LLR-1 harvested out of sorting examples); recommendation is (b) SOFTEN THE GUIDE AND TIGHTEN THE HARVESTER, with (a)'s existence scan as a cheap second arm

What is being decided

THIS ROW IS INDEPENDENT OF OI-39 AND SHOULD BE RULED WHICHEVER WAY OI-39 GOES. OI-39 is the REGISTRY-to-CODE direction (a design row names a symbol; a checker verifies it binds - assert-and-verify, hard-gated today). This row is CODE-to-REGISTRY (a source comment cites a requirement id - an annotation convention). The full evidence for why the two are different problems, with sources, is in docs/knowledge/traceability-enforcement.md (reviewed 2026-08-18) and is not restated here.

THE MANDATE IS UNCONDITIONAL AND IT SHIPS. PROCESS.md:161: "Code carries back-links (Implements: SR-007, LLR-014)". AGENTS.template.md:83: "Implements: SR-007, LLR-014 on implementing symbols"; and :102, inside the "Every public symbol: a docstring" bullet, "include Implements: SR-/LLR- so the back-link lands in the map". No applies-when, no warn-first, no exemption. Both files scaffold into every adopting repo.

THE ADHERENCE, MEASURED HERE 2026-08-18. Method: an AST walk over the 59 modules of project-trajectory/scripts/*.py counting module-level def/class whose name does not start with _ - which is gen_arch_map.scan_module's OWN public-API rule - with back-links counted as lines literally matching Implements:\s*(SN|SR|LLR|TC)-\d+, and ids resolved against the four spine registries via tomllib. Cross-checked by an independent route: grep -hE '^(def|class) [A-Za-z]' over the same files returns 782 and the single excess reconciles exactly - check_figures.py:10 is a docstring line beginning "class cheap instead:".

  • 781 PUBLIC SYMBOLS. 2 CARRY A LITERAL Implements: BACK-LINK - 0.26%. Both are in subagent_gate.py (decide :130, main :187) and both read Implements: SR-043, LLR-040.
  • REVERSE COVERAGE: 1 OF 161 LIVE LLR ROWS (0.6%) is named by any back-link in the scripts - LLR-040.

ENFORCED BY NOTHING, and the repo already knows: docs/enforcement-audit.md:65 classes the rule as "Prose (gap) - surfaced by the map but not required by any check", and finding 2 (:90) says closing it is "filed, not built". THAT ENTRY IS NOW ITSELF STALE on the detail: it says "the meta-repo's own scripts carry none and the column is empty". Two carry it, and the column is not empty - which is the next finding.

THE HARVESTER FABRICATES LINKS FROM PROSE, AND THIS IS THE PART THAT IS ACTIVELY WRONG RATHER THAN MERELY ABSENT. gen_arch_map.implements() (:178-189) regexes ANY SN|SR|LLR|TC-\d+ token out of a symbol's docstring OR the four comment lines above its def. It does not require the word Implements. Measured over the same 781 symbols:

  • 50 SYMBOLS GET A NON-EMPTY Implements COLUMN, carrying 62 (symbol, id) back-links - so 60 OF THE 62 (97%) WERE NEVER DECLARED BY ANYONE.
  • 13 OF THE 62 (21%) NAME NO LIVE REGISTRY ROW.
  • FIVE ARE PURE ILLUSTRATIONS. trace.id_sort_key's docstring explains "so SR-9 orders before SR-10" - a SORTING example - and the map records SR-9 and SR-10 as requirements that function implements. trace.triangle_findings' docstring poses the counter-example "a TC citing LLR-1 next to SR-2 when LLR-1 decomposes SR-1" - and the map records three more.

The column therefore does not report adherence to the convention; it reports a regex's reading of nearby English. This repo commits no rendered map today (the scaffolded docs/architecture.md target retired at WI-455), so the fabrication is LATENT here and SHIPS to every adopter who splices the map into AGENTS.md.

CITATION HYGIENE IN THE SCRIPTS, SAME METHOD: 300 three-digit spine-id citations (\b(SN|SR|LLR|TC)-\d{3}\b) across 83 distinct ids, of which 8 NAME NO LIVE ROW in 21 occurrences - SN-000, SN-013, SN-016, SN-030, SN-031, SN-032, SR-001, SR-141. Most are D-4-legitimate history and MUST NOT be flagged as defects. SR-141 is not.

THE DECAY IS MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED, AND IT IS FAST. docs/log.md (2026-08-11, WI-425) records that retiring SN-030/031/032 left 69 explanatory comments and docstrings across kit scripts and tests citing deleted ids, "a dangling pointer nothing mechanized catches". The campaign's own heading is "READ EVERY SITE, NO SEDDING": 56 sites were repointed by WHAT THEY NAME, 11 were KEPT AS ACCURATE HISTORY, 2 deferred, one flagged as low-confidence, and one turned out to be synthetic fixture text where repointing would have MANUFACTURED a citation. Then:

  • dispatch.py:310 STILL CITES SR-141, in _judgement_first's docstring, and SR-141 is not a live row - it merged into SR-148 on 2026-08-14 (docs/log.md:32342). git log -L 310,310:project-trajectory/scripts/dispatch.py shows THAT LINE WAS WRITTEN BY WI-425 ITSELF (08eb70fd, 2026-08-11, "repoint retired SN-030/031/032 citations"). The hand-audited repair went stale in THREE DAYS and nothing noticed for four more.
  • adjudicate_brief.py WAS BORN CITING RETIRED IDS: its SN-031/SN-030 section headers (:213, :289) and SN-032 module docstring landed in 6a1293c2 (WI-424) on 2026-08-11 - the day AFTER the sitting retired those ids and the same day as the sweep cleaning them up. A one-off audit cannot outrun concurrent authoring.

WHY MEANING CANNOT BE CHECKED, WHICH BOUNDS EVERY OPTION BELOW. Under ruling D-4 supersession is deletion and an id is NEVER re-minted, so a retired id in a comment is LEGITIMATE HISTORY - intake.py:185 narrating "SN-031 retired X" is a true sentence. A checker over code-side ids therefore either gates on accurate history (wrong) or needs a per-site human ruling (unscalable). EXISTENCE-checking is possible and cheap; MEANING-checking is not. That is a structural limit, not a budget one.

Blast radius

SMALL IN CODE, KIT-WIDE IN DOCTRINE - and the honest cost is that one option touches two SHIPPED documents every adopter inherits.

  • (a) EXISTENCE SCAN: one new advisory arm, stdlib regex over source comments, asking only whether each spine id names a live registry row or is explicitly marked as history. No dependency, language-agnostic by construction (it reads comment text, not syntax). It WOULD have caught dispatch.py:310. It needs a declared history-marker convention before it can be anything but noise, because 11 of WI-425's 69 sites were legitimately historical - that convention is the design work, not the regex.
  • (b) SOFTEN + TIGHTEN: a wording change in PROCESS.md:161 and AGENTS.template.md:83/:102 (both BYTE-WATCHED or capped - AGENTS.template.md is hard-capped at 10,000, so the edit must be net-neutral or shrinking), plus a guard in gen_arch_map.implements() requiring the literal Implements token before harvesting. THE HARVESTER CHANGE IS NOT COSMETIC: it would empty the map's third column for 48 of the 50 symbols that currently populate it, which is the honest state and will read as a regression to anyone who has seen the column full. tests/test_arch_map.py needs a case pinning that prose is no longer harvested.
  • (c) OFT-STYLE REVISIONED MARKER (artifact~name~revision): the largest change - the spine gains a revision field per row, the marker grammar changes, every existing marker is re-authored, and the kit gains a downstream migration. It is also the only option that reaches MEANING, by turning a requirement's revision bump into a reported defect at every stale covering tag.
  • (d) DO NOTHING is the CURRENT STATE, and it is not free: the shipped guide states a rule the kit itself follows at 0.26%, which is the paraphrase-drift/silent-conflict shape the instruction-file pack warns about - an agent reading AGENTS.template.md is told to do something the surrounding code demonstrably does not do.
  • NO SPINE COST UNDER ANY OPTION EXCEPT (c): nothing here moves a status cell, mints or retires a spine id, or opens a re-attest window.
  • INDEPENDENT OF OI-39 IN BOTH DIRECTIONS. If OI-39 mints its SR, this row is still open; if OI-39 rules not-owed, this row is still open.
Options
  • (a) ENFORCE EXISTENCE CHEAPLY - a stdlib regex scan asking only whether each spine id appearing in a source comment names a LIVE registry row, or is explicitly marked as history by a declared marker. FOR: it is language-agnostic BY CONSTRUCTION, since it reads comment text rather than syntax, so it costs an adopter no toolchain and no dependency; it would have caught the 8 dangling ids measured above, including dispatch.py:310's SR-141, which a hand audit wrote and then missed; and it stays strictly inside what D-4 permits - existence is decidable, meaning is not, and the check claims only the former. AGAINST: it needs a HISTORY MARKER convention first, or it fires on the 11-of-69 class WI-425 correctly kept, and inventing that marker is a real design decision rather than a regex; it verifies nothing about correctness, so a comment citing a live-but-wrong id passes clean; and it does not raise adherence at all - a file with zero back-links passes trivially.
  • (b) SOFTEN THE SHIPPED GUIDE TO MATCH OBSERVED PRACTICE, AND TIGHTEN THE HARVESTER. Re-word PROCESS.md:161 and AGENTS.template.md:83/:102 from an unconditional requirement to what it actually is - a recommended annotation where a symbol's requirement lineage is not obvious from its LLR row - and change gen_arch_map.implements() to harvest ONLY from a literal Implements: declaration, so the map's third column reports declarations instead of prose. FOR: it fixes the thing that is WRONG rather than the thing that is merely absent - a column 97% fabricated is worse than an empty one, because it is read as evidence; it removes a shipped rule that the kit itself fails at 99.7%, which is the exact instruction-file failure mode the repo has evidence for; it is the cheapest option in code; and it makes the enforcement-audit row honest rather than stale. AGAINST: it is a RETREAT, and the record will show a shipped obligation narrowed to fit observed practice - the pattern OI-40 names as "a bad habit if it becomes routine"; DO-178C section 11.21 does require the LLR-to-source ASSOCIATION (though not its representation), so an adopter in a regulated setting still needs an answer, and softening the guide without pointing them at one leaves them worse off; and the empty column will read as a regression.
  • (c) ADOPT AN OFT-STYLE REVISIONED MARKER - artifact~name~revision, so bumping a requirement's revision turns every stale covering tag into a REPORTED DEFECT. FOR: it is the only mechanism found anywhere in the research that addresses MEANING without a per-site human ruling - it does not decide whether a tag is still right, it DEMANDS the human decide, at the moment the requirement changes, which is exactly when the answer is cheap; and it converts silent decay into loud failure, which is this repo's stated preference. AGAINST: it is by far the largest change - a revision field on every spine row, a new marker grammar, every existing marker re-authored, a downstream migration; it makes the 0.26% adherence WORSE before better, since the harder the marker the fewer get written; and it presumes the back-link obligation is worth keeping at all, which is the question this row is actually asking.
  • (d) DO NOTHING - leave the mandate stated, unenforced, and the harvester as it is. FOR: nothing is broken today in the sense of a failing check, and docs/enforcement-audit.md already RECORDS the gap honestly, which is what that document is for. AGAINST: THIS IS THE CURRENT STATE, so choosing it ratifies rather than resolves - and the audit's own entry is now stale (it says the column is empty; it is not), so the one artifact that was catching this has stopped describing it accurately. Doing nothing also leaves the fabricated column shipping to adopters.
Recommendation

(b) SOFTEN THE GUIDE AND TIGHTEN THE HARVESTER - with (a)'s existence scan as a cheap second arm once a history marker is declared. NOT (c), and NOT (d).

THE DECIDING ARGUMENT IS THAT ONE HALF OF THIS IS NOT A GAP, IT IS A DEFECT. Everything else here is an absence - a rule nobody enforces, a convention nobody follows. The harvester is different in kind: it is SHIPPED MACHINERY PRODUCING FALSE STATEMENTS. trace.id_sort_key's docstring explains that SR-9 sorts before SR-10, and the generated code map records that function as implementing SR-9 and SR-10. trace.triangle_findings poses a counter-example about LLR-1 and SR-2, and the map records three more. 60 of the 62 back-links in that column were never written by anyone, and 13 of them point at nothing. A traceability artifact that invents links is worse than one that has none, because a reader can only discount what they know is empty. That half should be fixed regardless of how the rest is ruled, and it is a few lines: require the literal Implements token before harvesting.

THE OTHER HALF IS A CHOICE BETWEEN TWO HONEST STATES, and 2-of-781 decides it. A rule stated unconditionally in two shipped documents and followed at 0.26% by its own authors is not an under-enforced rule; it is a rule the repo has already declined by revealed preference over hundreds of opportunities. The options are to make the words true or to make the code true. Making the code true means writing 779 back-links and maintaining them - and the maintenance side is where the evidence is worst: no standard requires the representation, no surveyed commercial tool requires it, every proxy says such conventions decay, and this repo's own WI-425 measured 69 stale sites from ONE retirement, needed a full human read to fix them because D-4 makes a retired id in prose accurate history, and the fix ITSELF was stale in three days. Making the words true costs two sentences.

(c) IS THE BEST IDEA IN THE FIELD AND IS STILL REFUSED HERE, which is worth saying plainly rather than burying. OpenFastTrace's revision suffix is genuinely clever - it does not try to judge whether a tag is still correct, it forces a human to re-judge at the one moment the answer is cheap, and it is the only mechanism found that converts silent decay into loud failure. If this repo were going to keep the back-link obligation, (c) is how it should be kept. But it presupposes the obligation is worth the machinery, and 2-of-781 says it is not. RECORD IT AS THE DESIGN TO REACH FOR IF THE OBLIGATION IS EVER RE-ADOPTED, rather than losing it with the ruling.

(a) IS WORTH BUILDING, JUST NOT AS THE ANSWER. It is cheap, it is language-agnostic by construction, and it caught something real: dispatch.py:310 cites SR-141, written by the very campaign that was cleaning up dangling citations, three days before its target merged away. But note what it does NOT do - it does not raise adherence, and a file with no back-links at all passes it clean. It polices the ids that ARE written; it never asks for more. That is the correct scope given D-4, and it is also why it cannot be the whole answer. It should follow (b), not precede it, because a declared history marker is a prerequisite and inventing one is the actual work.

"DO NEITHER" IS THE CURRENT STATE, AND IT IS WHAT docs/enforcement-audit.md EXISTS TO CATCH. That is the sharpest thing this row has to say about (d). The audit already carries the row - "Prose (gap) ... not required by any check", closing it "filed, not built" - and the mechanism worked exactly as designed: the gap was named honestly and left visible. What it did NOT do is stay accurate. Its finding 2 says "the meta-repo's own scripts carry none and the column is empty"; two carry it, and the column is populated for 50 symbols, nearly all fabricated. So the single instrument this repo relies on to keep an unenforced rule visible has itself drifted away from the rule it is watching. Ruling (d) means trusting that instrument for another cycle after it has just been shown to go stale - and whichever option is ruled, THE AUDIT ROW SHOULD BE CORRECTED IN THE SAME CHANGE.

clear it to read every row in full — each amended row's remaining cells, and the SR text a chain-only amendment hangs from

2 · Ratification & re-attestation

SR-006Gate/tier harness enforces required stepsre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-006current content
SR-ID
SR-006
Title
Gate/tier harness enforces required steps
SN-Refs
SN-004;SN-008
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered harness shall run the required steps of the gate that must next be passed (the strictness selector cached in docs/gate) and fail that gate when a required tool is missing, reporting SKIP(missing) rather than silently passing — and, on a claimed work branch (one whose history holds a docs/work/active/<branch>/ claim), SKIP its generated-artifact freshness steps with a stated trunk-lane reason, generated artifacts being trunk-only, while every non-freshness step still runs.
Rationale
Realizes SN-004 (gates enforce their bar), SN-008 (no false green) and the dissolved edge expectation that a missing tool fails, never skips. A sanctioned SKIP class of this harness's own step plan is not a second crossing observable, it is the same one qualified. The skip is not a weakening: a work branch cannot commit generated artifacts at all (concurrency-restructure §5.2), so demanding their freshness there would force the very conflict the rule deletes; the skip is reported, never silent, and any signal ambiguity resolves toward the full trunk bar.
AcceptanceCriteria
Invoked for a bar, the harness runs that bar's declared steps; a required-but-absent tool fails the gate with guidance (never a silent pass), degraded to a reported SKIP only under the explicit lenient mode; a tier selection runs exactly the declared step subset; on a claimed work branch the declared freshness steps SKIP with a stated trunk-lane reason and the exit code untouched while every non-freshness step still runs; the closing commit and its successors keep the skip (the signal derives from branch history, not the working tree); the trunk lane never skips them.

Title
Per-module coverage-floor comparator
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/check_coverage.py
CodeSymbol
load_floors/load_report/evaluate
Detail

A failure names the offending module, and all floors holding exits 0 — the per-module floor is what keeps the global aggregate from hiding a thin high-risk module.

SR-Refs
SR-006;SR-170
Title
Work-branch lane signal + freshness skip
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/check.py
CodeSymbol
_claimed_work_branch/steps
Detail
On a claimed work branch the _TRUNK_FRESHNESS_STEPS set SKIPs with a §5.2 notice, exit code untouched; the plan listing still names every step. The claim signal answers in two stages - docs/work/active/<branch>/ on disk (the fast path), else the branch's own committed history (git log -1 -- docs/work/active/<branch>) - so the closing commit that archives the claim, and every commit after it, keep the skip. No git, a failing git, or an empty answer all read as the trunk lane: a false work-branch positive would switch the freshness gates off on the one branch that owns regenerating them.
Rationale
A false work-branch positive would relax the trunk's freshness bar, so every ambiguity resolves toward the trunk lane.

SR-007Declared stack profile, refused when it is brokenre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-007current content
SR-ID
SR-007
Title
Declared stack profile, refused when it is broken
SN-Refs
SN-003
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered harness shall read the toolchain (format/lint/test commands, tiers, coverage, arch-map mode) from the declared stack profile so a stack swap edits only that declaration, failing loudly on a malformed profile or a non-integer coverage threshold rather than proceeding on a bad one.
Rationale
Realizes SN-003 — the kit is stack-agnostic; the harness is re-pointed by declaration, not by editing a kit script. Reading a declaration and REFUSING a broken one are one obligation, not two: a reader that proceeds on a malformed profile has not read it. A MISSING DECLARED BINARY is deliberately no part of it — SR-006 already states that verbatim as its own ("fail that gate when a required tool is missing"), which is why LLR-008 decomposes from both rows rather than one.
AcceptanceCriteria
Each step's command resolves from the declared stack profile; changing the profile changes the commands run, with no script edit; a malformed profile or a non-integer coverage threshold produces a clear nonzero failure at the relevant step rather than a run on a bad profile (a missing declared binary fails under SR-006, which states that clause).

SN-Refs

Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered scaffold generator shall skip an existing file on a re-run unless an explicit overwrite is requested.
Rationale
Realizes SN-001 (safe drop-in onto an existing repo) and SN-007 — an adopter picking up kit updates must not lose their own edits, and a scaffolder that overwrites cannot be run twice, so it stops being run at all. SR-017Always-on secrets floorre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-017current content
SR-ID
SR-017
Title
Always-on secrets floor
SN-Refs
SN-009
Boundary-Refs
B-01;B-04
Requirement
When a session attempts to commit or push content matching a secret pattern, the system shall refuse at the hook floor — scanning every commit's staged diff, message and outgoing range regardless of the privacy toggle, and naming the finding — unless the declared secrets_scan dial is explicitly false.
Rationale
Realizes SN-009 — a credential that reaches a shared branch must be treated as disclosed and rotated, and expunging it from history is a rewrite every clone has to follow, so the cheap moment to catch it is before the commit and the expensive one is any moment after. Folding the scan into the privacy toggle was rejected: that makes the highest-severity check opt-in alongside a preference. The one opt-out is an explicit declaration, so turning it off is a visible act.
AcceptanceCriteria
A staged PEM header, token, or key pattern is flagged and blocks; a clean diff passes; the floor runs with privacy-check off, and is disabled only by an explicit secrets_scan = false.

M

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-018current content

SR-018
Title
Privacy gate two-axis
SN-Refs

Boundary-Refs
B-01;B-04
Requirement
When the declared privacy_check dial is on, the system shall additionally refuse, at the same hook floor, a commit or push carrying a PII/identity class — exempting configured author identities.
Rationale
Realizes SN-009 — identity and PII classes are a policy choice a repo makes about its own contributors, unlike a leaked credential, which is a defect in every repo. Making them always-on was rejected because the exemption list they need is project-specific: a repo that has not declared its author identities would have the layer firing on its own commit metadata, and the fix would be to silence the scanner rather than to configure it.
AcceptanceCriteria
With privacy-check on, an identity/PII class is flagged unless the author is a configured exempt identity; with it off, only the secrets floor runs.

SR-019Pre-commit hook floorre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-019current content
SR-ID
SR-019
Title
Pre-commit hook floor
SN-Refs
SN-005;SN-009
Boundary-Refs
B-01;B-04
Requirement
The pre-commit hook shall run the integrity + secrets floor on every commit, agent-neutrally — a LOCAL floor, bypassable by the session (git commit --no-verify), so it discharges "no unchecked write enters governed state" only as a PAIR with the hosted re-run of the same bar (SR-151/SR-152), never alone.
Rationale
Realizes SN-005 and SN-009 — a floor only CI enforces is one the local tree crosses freely, and a secret caught after the commit is already in history. Trusting the agent to run the checks was rejected: a floor exists for the times the agent forgets, so it is git-enforced and agent-neutral. Parents the interpreter probe: a hook that crashes cryptically because no python3 resolves, or because a Windows Store alias exits nonzero, has not run the floor — so probing by RUNNING a candidate and reporting clearly is machinery serving THIS obligation, and it decomposes to LLR-021 rather than standing as a requirement of its own.
AcceptanceCriteria
The hook runs the registry-integrity floor and the secrets scan on staged content and blocks a commit that fails either; a --no-verify commit bypasses it and is caught instead by the hosted re-run, which is what makes the pair — not this row — the honest unit of the claim.

Shared by pre-commit and pre-push (the module named here is the representative home; both hooks read the same probe). The equivalent obligation on the ROOT LAUNCHERS is stated on SR-160, not here: retired SR-021 spanned both audiences at once, and the launcher half is an observable of the front door rather than machinery of the hook floor.

Shared by pre-commit and pre-push (the module named here is the representative home; both hooks read the same probe). The equivalent obligation on the ROOT LAUNCHERS is stated on SR-160, not here: retired SR-021 spanned both audiences at once, and the launcher half is an observable of the front door rather than machinery of the hook floor.

SR-026Coordinator resumes headlessre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-026current content
SR-ID
SR-026
Title
Coordinator resumes headless
SN-Refs
SN-006
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered coordinator shall resume headless with stdin closed, never blocking on a prompt: a worker resumes from its explicit claimed assignment plus the committed trailer evidence on its branch, and the integrator derives claim and queue state from trunk history alone — the generated status surface never a session input.
Rationale
Realizes SN-006 and the dissolved edge expectation that an unattended run never blocks on a prompt, at launch or mid-run. The acceptance is deliberately narrower than it once was: it had carried two obligations the shall never stated - transient-limit backoff and stall abort - and an acceptance cell cannot mint a requirement. Both were MINTED rather than deleted, because each describes live behaviour worth keeping: SR-171 and SR-172. An unattended run blocked on a prompt is indistinguishable from a hung one and holds its lane until a human notices. Keeping a separate serial resume path beside the dispatcher was rejected: two authorities over one decision drift, and the divergence surfaces as a run that resumes differently depending on how it was started.
AcceptanceCriteria
A worker reconciles from its assignment + committed evidence and the integrator from trunk history; every mode runs with stdin closed and a prompt cannot wedge it.
Priority
S
Verification
Test
Status
Modified
Phase
1
LLR LLR-026current content
LLR-ID
LLR-026
SR-Refs
SR-026
Title
Headless session launch
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/agent_session.py
CodeSymbol
build_argv/run_session/_codex_lastmsg_setup
Detail
Runs the agent CLI headless: build_argv substitutes {model} into argv and, when a CmdTemplate carries no {prompt} placeholder (the default for both provider CLIs), routes the prompt to the child's STDIN (run_session writes it from a daemon thread then closes), while the no-wedge invariant holds — a fixed prompt plus EOF, never an interactive read. A codex session additionally captures that CLI's own --output-last-message file as the result (_codex_lastmsg_setup/_codex_lastmsg_read). stdin is otherwise closed so no prompt wedges the loop; the scope is the dispatcher and worker sessions, and the former status.md-resume read is retired rather than coexisting with them.
Rationale
A large prompt passed as argv dies at the OS command-line cap (8191 characters through a Windows .CMD shim, 32767 via CreateProcess) and a round's briefs run to roughly 70k, so stdin is the only delivery that survives — both limits verified live rather than assumed. The last-message capture exists because that CLI echoes its banner and the whole prompt into stdout, so stdout is not the result.

The assignment is the branch integrate.py's claim cut, and the session tag defaults to the branch name; the prompt never includes docs/status.md; a worker branch never edits root status, another branch's claims, the root log or generated artifacts; the result channel is committed trailer evidence (WI: / Blocked-WI: + BlockRef:).

LLR LLR-143current content

SR-Refs
SR-026
Title
Plain-launch drive loop (claim->build->integrate)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/dispatch.py
CodeSymbol
run/_resume_or_claim/_stranded_claims/_default_worker
Detail
The serial scheduling front end a plain agent_loop.py launch runs: each cycle re-derives the ready frontier (schedule.frontier), refuses on the tracked pause and on a dirty trunk at the cycle top, resumes a parked claimed branch else claims the next queued WI in build order (integrate.claim, branch name check-ref-format-validated), launches one worker session on the claimed branch's worktree (agent_loop.py --worktree --wi with the session dials forwarded), drains the serial merge queue (integrate.integrate) counting merges from finished_branches, and stops loudly on any composed refusal - stranded claims (active specs with no branch ref) refuse by name, NEEDS-HUMAN propagates as exit 7, a stall aborts, an empty frontier drains to exit 0. The session-config preflight applies only when work needs a worker. Never pushes.
Rationale
Ordering only, no new authority: every refusal stays where it already lives, and the driver is ~400 lines of composition against the 4,042-line dispatcher it replaces.

SR-027Coordinator preflightre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-027current content
SR-ID
SR-027
Title
Coordinator preflight
SN-Refs
SN-006
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered coordinator shall refuse a broken footing in preflight — not a git repo, no agent CLI, a private author under privacy-check, or a second coordinator in the same checkout while another holds its lock — exiting a typed nonzero code rather than hanging.
Rationale
Realizes SN-006 and the dissolved edge expectation that a preflight on a non-git working directory reports and exits nonzero rather than hanging — a run that starts on a broken footing fails later and further from the cause, and an unattended loop cannot be asked what went wrong. Continuing with a warning was rejected: each of these conditions makes every subsequent step meaningless, so a typed nonzero exit is what lets the caller act on it. A second coordinator in the same checkout is a FOURTH broken footing, not a separate obligation: it fails in the same direction, at the same moment, through the same refusal. Two coordinators in one checkout write the same working tree and the loser's commits interleave into the winner's diff where no review will separate them; coordinating by convention was rejected there for the reason that keeps it here — the failure is silent and shows up only as a garbled history. The lock machinery decomposes to LLR-029/LLR-030.
AcceptanceCriteria
Outside a git repo, with no agent CLI, or with a private author under privacy-check, preflight exits a typed nonzero code; a second coordinator on a held lock is refused so only one writer runs per checkout, preventing a two-writer race.

S

SR SR-028current content
SR-ID
SR-028
Title
Coordinator typed outcomes
SN-Refs
SN-006
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered coordinator shall end each session in a typed outcome code, guard a zero-commit HEAD, and report an all-ERROR stall as an unavailable agent rather than a work stall.
Rationale
Realizes SN-006 and the dissolved edge expectations that a repository with no commits yet (HEAD absent) does not crash the loop and that an all-ERROR run is an unavailable agent rather than a work stall — a coordinator ending in an untyped code forces its caller to parse prose; a session that produced no commit still has a HEAD to report, so without a guard an empty run is indistinguishable from a productive one in the record it leaves; and an all-ERROR run reported as a work stall sends a human looking for a blocked work item that does not exist. Treating an agent error as a stall was rejected for that reason: an unreachable agent and a blocked item need different responses, so they need different outcomes.
AcceptanceCriteria
Every declared end state returns its assigned outcome code, and an unrecognized end state fails rather than returning a success code; a repo with no commits does not crash the loop; an agent-CLI error is logged ERROR; an all-ERROR region is reported as an unavailable agent.

SR-040Per-phase routing and review dialre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-040current content
SR-ID
SR-040
Title
Per-phase routing and review dial
SN-Refs
SN-006
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered coordinator shall route each in-process session phase (PLAN/BUILD/REVIEW-A/REVIEW-B/DESIGN-CHECK/CRITIQUE) through its declared per-phase command template, falling back to the single declared command — surfacing the declared reviewer dial at run start without enforcing it, and warning (never blocking) when a lane resume surface exceeds the declared size threshold.
Rationale
Realizes SN-006 and the dissolved edge expectation that an unattended run never blocks on a prompt, at launch or mid-run — two samples of one model share blind spots, so a review by the family that built the change agrees with it for the wrong reasons; cross-family routing is what makes the second opinion independent, and one model for every phase was rejected on that basis. Dispatch stays keyed off the in-process session phase because the alternative, a per-phase code branch, makes adding a phase a code change and so discourages adding one. The resume surface's growth tripwire warns rather than fails because a growing resume surface is a smell and not a defect, and failing on a smell trains an operator to bypass the check. Hat-derived (hat.UX-ENGINEER + hat.UNATTENDED-OPS): only the resume-surface tripwire clause is orphaned, and two lenses reach it independently — a view must stay usable as its content volume grows (C-UXE-2), and at 3am the reader of a resume surface is another automated step, which needs the growth legible from the artifact rather than from a human noticing (C-UNA-8). Both also explain the warn-not-fail severity: a smell is reported, not gated. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.) THE TRIPWIRE'S CARRIER IS RETIRED AND THE OBLIGATION IS LEFT STANDING — investigated 2026-08-18, stated rather than quietly dropped or quietly re-pointed. The dial this row's acceptance used to cite and the pure warner behind it were deleted with the serial resume driver; the coordinator now carries a TOMBSTONE COMMENT where that function stood, and its own words say the PREMISE dissolved rather than the code moving — no session inherits the status surface as its resume surface any more, and that surface is a generated integrator artifact whose size the generator owns. So the clause has no implementation, and its SUBJECT as originally drawn has no referent either. THE NEAREST LIVE CANDIDATE WAS EXAMINED AND REFUSED AS A SUBSTITUTE. The documentation checker warns when the status surface passes a declared line budget, which sounds like this clause and is not it: it differs on ACTOR (a documentation checker, not the delivered coordinator this row's subject names), on MOMENT (the commit and gate bar, not run start or mid-run), and on SURFACE (the status page judged as lean prose for a human reviewer — the very "human noticing" the C-UNA-8 lens above says is the wrong mechanism at 3am). It is also already the carrier of a DIFFERENT requirement, so citing it here would put one mechanism under two requirements that do not agree about what it must do. Decisively, its threshold semantics are the INVERSE of this row's: a declared zero or negative budget makes it warn on EVERY non-empty file and only an explicit off token silences it — measured by exercising the function on declared values 0, -1, off and 200, not read off its source. Adopting it would have been carrier substitution across a different requirement, component, actor and moment, with inverted semantics, and it is refused on that evidence. WHAT THIS ROW THEREFORE IS: an obligation with no current carrier, and the ONE residue this sitting must still rule. Two honest dispositions, neither taken here because both are the owner's: rebuild the tripwire inside the coordinator against whatever the CURRENT resume surface is, or strike the clause and let the two hat lenses that reach it be answered elsewhere. It is not restated as satisfied by anything, because nothing satisfies it.
AcceptanceCriteria
A mapped session phase invokes its own command template and the default template does not fire; a broken map entry fails preflight before iteration 1; the banner surfaces the declared reviewer dial at run start. The resume-surface clause states its FIT CRITERION and explicitly NOT a discharge: a satisfied state would be the coordinator reporting, without halting the run, that a resume surface a resuming session inherits has passed its declared threshold — and NOTHING IN THE DELIVERED COORDINATOR DOES THIS, so this clause is an open obligation with no current carrier rather than a criterion any evidence today satisfies (the rationale records the retirement, and the live look-alike that was examined and refused).

SR-043Subagent spawn gatere-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-043current content
SR-ID
SR-043
Title
Subagent spawn gate
SN-Refs
SN-006;SN-012
Boundary-Refs
B-04
Requirement
While an unattended run is in progress, the delivered subagent-spawn gate shall refuse or defer subagent spawns per the declared subagent-gate dial in the single policy declaration home (off|ask|deny; absent=off; the legacy one-word file is still read through the migration window), honor a launcher-set SUBAGENT_GATE=allow override the model cannot set, and fail open on any error so a broken gate never wedges the tools.
Rationale
Realizes SN-006 (a walk-away run stays safe — bounded, supervised fan-out with the override held by the human, not the model) and SN-012 (opt-in: absent/off is a vacuous allow, so a non-adopter pays nothing). Supervision, not security — a model that can edit files can remove the hook (adapted from stop-subagent-fanout, MIT; spec docs/archive/INTEGRATION_PLAN.md Phase 4). Hat-derived (hat.SECURITY): C-SEC-2 asks that the irreversible actions an unattended run can take be enumerated, each naming the dial or human authority that permits it — which is this row exactly, for the one action that starts further actors. A direct hit, and the reason this row is not accretion: its parentage was real and lived only in SN-006's `why` cell until the safety half moved into that need's normative text at this same pass. OPEN QUESTION FOR THE SITTING, recorded rather than resolved here (2026-08-16 adversarial round, finding F1): with the bound now normative in SN-006, this row's fail-open-on-any-error clause is in tension with that bound — a gate error drops the ceiling the need promises — and the two candidate resolutions (fail closed, or keep failing open under a narrower ceiling that stays enforced when the hook errors) are both behavior changes an owner must rule; the fail-open behavior is UNCHANGED pending that ruling, because a gate that wedges every tool on its own bug is the failure this clause was written against. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
With the declared subagent-gate dial deny, a subagent spawn is refused (a deny decision at the tool layer, exit 2); ask defers to approval; off or absent, and the launcher-held allow override, admit it; a non-spawn tool is not gated; a malformed payload fails open (allow) and is logged.

SR-046Declared runnable capabilities reachable from the repository rootre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-046current content
SR-ID
SR-046
Title
Declared runnable capabilities reachable from the repository root
SN-Refs
SN-001;SN-035
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The launchers at the repository root shall expose every declared runnable capability consistently to an interactive operator, to a direct caller naming one capability, and to automated discovery - from a single declaration, on Windows and POSIX alike.
Rationale
The shall states ONE obligation rather than a menu specification. It had enumerated a numbered interactive menu, direct named launch, machine listing, empty-declaration text, exit-code passthrough, and the name/name.desc declaration grammar - things that fail independently, which is a decomposition wearing a requirement's clothes. It now states the one capability-level decision: every declared capability is reachable, the same way, from one declaration, by three kinds of caller. Numbering, listing format, declaration grammar, fallback text and exit handling are LLR-047's and the acceptance's. Realizes SN-001 - the evaluator's rungs make running the product never depend on recalling a command, and the capability set is now declared once in docs/stack.ini instead of hard-wired and duplicated across the platform launchers. A new SR formalizing the launcher surface, which previously existed only as process prose.
AcceptanceCriteria
A machine listing prints one name-and-description line per declared capability in declaration order; a direct call naming a declared capability runs that capability's command and returns its exit code; the no-arg menu launches a stdin-selected capability non-interactively and does not hang on closed stdin; an absent or empty capability declaration prints guidance and exits 1; a declared description renders in both the menu and the listing; the platform launchers delegate to the one selector rather than carrying commands of their own; a fresh scaffold ships the selector and the delegate launchers.
Priority
S
Verification
Test
Status

Phase

LLR LLR-047current content
LLR-ID
LLR-047
SR-Refs
SR-046
Title
Run capability menu reader
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/run_menu.py
CodeSymbol
load_capabilities/interactive_menu/direct/launch
Detail
load_capabilities parses the docs/stack.ini [run] section (configparser, interpolation=None, case-preserving optionxform; declaration order kept; a name.desc row attaches to its command and an orphan or empty-value command is dropped) into an ordered (name, command, desc) list; --list prints name<TAB>desc per row; direct(name) launches by exact-match name (exit 2 on an unknown name) and launch() runs the shell line via subprocess shell=True, passing the child exit code straight through; interactive_menu prints the numbered menu, reads a pick from stdin, and returns EOF-safe without hanging; an absent or empty [run] prints guidance and exits 1. The run.* launchers are thin delegates forwarding their args to run_menu.py.
Rationale
The command being run is the user's own declared line, so shell=True is the same trust boundary the declaration already establishes rather than a new one. EOF-safety is load-bearing: a menu that blocks on a closed stdin turns an unattended invocation into a hang, which is the failure this launcher exists to avoid.

SR-049Derived gate from artifact statesre-attest owed

SR SR-049current content
SR-ID
SR-049
Title
Derived gate from artifact states
SN-Refs
SN-004;SN-008;SN-029
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered harness shall derive the gate the project must next pass from the spine artifact states alone, caching it as a derived value — never accepting a hand-set one — with a post-attestation amendment dropping the derived gate exactly as a newly introduced draft does, and the pending-amendment count carried beside the draft count so the pending state never hides.
Rationale
SSOT applied to the gate itself (docs/archive/specs/derived-gate-model.2026-07-20.md): a hand-set marker can be bumped without the work; deriving it makes gate advancement mechanical (SN-004) and un-gameable (SN-008). The hybrid cache keeps the value readable on checkout; --check guards rot. SN-029 IS A PARENT HERE, and the join is recorded rather than inferred: the need's acceptance says in its own words that "an AMENDED requirement drops the derived stage exactly as a newly introduced one does", and this row's shall is the only place in the SR layer that states it — the join was simply unrecorded, so a reader asking which rows realize SN-029 got SR-139 (which tiers are held), SR-140 (what an acceptance record contains) and SR-148 (loop selection), none of which carries the drop clause. Nothing moved between rows; the citation now matches the text that was already here.
AcceptanceCriteria
The per-phase gates derive from the current artifact states alone (an amended row's phase reads the decomposed-unverified rung until re-attested); the freshness check exits nonzero when a state change is not reflected in the cached derived value's basis; the compared basis line includes the pending-amendment count beside the draft count.

LLR-ID
LLR-118
SR-Refs
SR-049
Title
Generated owner decision surface (registry + attestation depth)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_open_items.py
CodeSymbol
render/_attestation_cards/word_diff/mask_local
Detail
The RENDERED half of the Modified/Draft attestation regime whose gate SR-049 derives. gen_open_items renders (a) every pending row of docs/requirements/open-items.toml as a decision brief and (b) every SR whose Status is Draft (ratification owed) or Modified (re-attest owed) with its whole chain's per-cell before/after. Two contracts the emitted file carries: the attestation-baseline STAMP (a --since render must be reproducible by the freshness gate, or --check compares against a different history) and the check-the-baseline empty state (an auto-derived baseline sitting AFTER a pre-regime amendment renders no cells, which must never read as nothing-changed). The machine-local advisory region is masked before comparing. SCOPE and its deliberate narrowings: this module RENDERS and owns no second opinion — the attestation model is trace.reattest_model and the pending pointers are gen_trajectory.pending_block, both imported, so a disagreement with `trace.py --ratify` means this view is the defect and the brief wins. NOT claimed: that the rendered page is legible or well-composed.
Rationale
A human cannot bless a delta they cannot see, and the depth that makes a re-attest readable is a WORD-LEVEL diff — which the markdown surface this replaces could not mark, so a reviewer was asked to sign a change they could only read as a whole cell. Re-deriving the attestation model here was rejected: a second opinion that disagreed with the brief would leave the reviewer arbitrating between two surfaces. Both emitted contracts exist because their absence fails SILENTLY in front of someone about to attest, which is the worst place for a quiet failure.

Title
SN-coverage gate rung + uncovered basis count
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/derive_gate.py
CodeSymbol
sn_gate/sn_cited_ids
Detail

The compared basis line gains uncovered=N (ratified-and-uncited count) beside drafts=N/modified=N - additive for readers, but --check compares the line whole, so it is a cache-format change downstream repos regenerate through.
Rationale
Before this rung SN->SR linkage was not a gate input at all: an unanswered ratified need left the gate untouched, orphaning only at DevStg-Tests strictness, which is the ruled boundary. Measured safe before landing: the census on this repo read 25/25 SNs covered and zero ghost SN-Refs, so the rung lands green here and the uncovered case is constructed by fixture, not found.

SR SR-052current content
SR-ID
SR-052
Title
Dashboard accessibility (rubric-adjudicated)
SN-Refs
SN-024;SN-023
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The state view the delivered generators produce shall be operable and readable without a pointer or full color perception: every interactive element (tabs, expandable blocks, SVG nodes with detail panels) is keyboard-reachable and carries an accessible name (title/aria/visible text); no information is encoded by color alone (status/phase/type encodings carry a text or shape cue); text keeps readable contrast against its fill.
Rationale
Realizes SN-024 and SN-023 — an accessibility bar left unstated silently reads as no bar at all, so the obligation is stated here even though its clauses were not measurable when the row was written. They since became so, one clause at a time, which is why this row's method is mechanical: the thresholds are the child rows' and the acceptance is their chain. Hat-derived (hat.ACCESSIBILITY): keyboard operability, an accessible name per element, colour never the only channel, and a readable contrast floor are the charter's own subject, and no other lens in the roster names a measurable perceptual threshold — the UX pair reaches robustness (it does not overflow), never legibility. That dependency is CLOSED: the ACCESSIBILITY charter is ruled `always`, so the lens this row derives from is put to every decomposition — and it had already reached this row once SN-023 carried the `a11y` tag, which is why the gap is narrower than the label suggests. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
Operating the produced view with keyboard alone reaches and activates every interactive element — tabs, expandable blocks, SVG nodes with detail panels — with no focusable element hidden inside a container assistive technology skips; every interactive element announces an accessible name that is neither empty nor a bare id; with colour perception removed, every status, phase and type encoding still reads through its text or shape cue; every text-and-fill pair clears the declared contrast floor. One unreachable or unnamed element, one colour-only encoding, or one pair under the floor is a fail; the pass is the full sweep of every emitted view, never a sampled subset.

SR-Refs
SR-052
Title
Non-pruning SVG container roles (A2 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
CodeSymbol
_svg_role/_FOCUSABLE
Detail
The mechanized core of accessibility anchor A2 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-accessibility.md): no emitted <svg> declares the CHILDREN-PRESENTATIONAL role=img over a FOCUSABLE descendant, and any svg that does declare role=img carries its own accessible name (aria-label, or a first-direct-child <title> — per SVG-AAM a <title> nested in a <g> names the <g>, not the svg). Focusable means tabindex in any quoting OR a native SVG <a href>, which is tab-ordered without a tabindex. The role is chosen from the emitted BODY by _svg_role. SCOPE — and no wider: the container-role invariant as PROVEN by TC-104, over the artifact this repo ships plus the tiered fixture plus the predicate's own unit cases; it is NOT a proof over every reachable emitter configuration, so an emitter no rendered artifact exercises is unguarded. Name QUALITY belongs to LLR-108/TC-113.
Rationale
role=img prunes its subtree from the accessibility tree, so declaring it over focusable descendants hides every one of them from assistive technology while the page still looks correct. The predicate must treat a native SVG <a href> as focusable and not tabindex alone: a tabindex-only predicate passes a diagram of linked stage cards that are tab-ordered without one. Setting the role at each call site was rejected — deriving it from the body is what stops a future emitter silently reintroducing the conflict.

SR-052
Title
Accessible-name quality, not merely presence (A2 residue)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py
CodeSymbol
_render_drill breadcrumb label; every focusable/role=img emit site
Detail
The QUALITY half of accessibility anchor A2, mechanizing what LLR-101 leaves perceptual. Three rules over every focusable element and role=img graphic: it HAS an accessible name; that name is never a bare registry id; and navigation LANDMARKS are distinct. SCOPE, narrower than a first measurement suggests: A2 governs INTERACTIVE elements and meaningful graphics — a <title> on a decorative edge path is a tooltip, not an accessible name. Uniqueness is asserted for landmarks ONLY: a descend control for the same container legitimately recurs across drill layers, one visible at a time, so those repeats are one control reached by different paths. Whether a well-formed, unique, descriptive name is the RIGHT description belongs to SR-054's reading-task critique.
Rationale
A bare registry id tells a screen-reader user nothing about what the control is, and a reader listing landmarks hears a flat list in which two called 'Breadcrumb' are indistinguishable — which is what three drills produce unless the names are made distinct. Asserting over decorative graphics too was rejected: it would manufacture findings the accessibility standard does not make, and a check that cries wolf is one a reader stops reading.
