LLR LLR-112current content
LLR-ID
LLR-112
SR-Refs
SR-052
Title
Keyboard reachability of every wired control (A1 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py
CodeSymbol
emitted querySelectorAll wiring; tabindex + native-link emission
Detail
The mechanized core of accessibility anchor A1 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-accessibility.md): every element the emitted page WIRES an interaction to is keyboard-focusable. The wired set is DERIVED from the document's own DOM-selecting calls and then classified — a control selector (its matches receive per-element click/focus/dblclick/keydown listeners) may match only elements carrying tabindex=0 or natively tab-ordered (a button; an SVG a-with-href, the LLR-101 predicate), and each non-control selector records why its matches are not focus stops. A newly wired selector fails the owning test until classified. The derivation reads EVERY DOM-selecting API (querySelector/querySelectorAll/closest/matches, any quote style) and fails loudly on a non-literal selector argument, which a static read cannot classify. A control-selector wiring loop that attaches click or dblclick must attach focus or keydown beside it, statically asserted. Order-integrity: document order IS emission order, no positive tabindex may reorder the walk, and tabindex=-1 is legal only on the roving tablist buttons. SCOPE — and no wider: focusability, order-integrity and activation PAIRING of the wired set over the shipped artifact plus the seven-fixture emitter sweep. Runtime behaviour equivalence is not driven, and whether the order FEELS sensible stays a reader-experience judgement. Runtime-created controls (the breadcrumb buttons) are native <button>s, keyboard-operable by construction and outside the static walk — a recorded limit.
Rationale
A control reachable only by pointer is unreachable for a keyboard user, and the page still looks complete, so nothing reports it. Enumerating the controls by hand was rejected: a per-element list goes stale the moment a selector is added, whereas deriving the wired set from the document's own calls means a new selector fails until someone classifies it. Two narrowings are load-bearing and must not be relaxed back: the derivation has to read every DOM-selecting API rather than one spelling of one call, or it silently misses wired controls; and the key-handler half has to be asserted statically rather than deferred to a browser, or mouse-only wiring ships.

(see TC-117)

SR-052
Title
No information by colour alone (A3 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
CodeSymbol
tier_legend + sw/phase/status legend emitters; STATUS_GLYPH
Detail
The mechanized core of accessibility anchor A3: for every declared colour vocabulary (enumerated from the module constants, the U5/U2 closure, so a new vocabulary enters by existing), every member that actually PAINTS in a rendered document resolves to a legend swatch labelled in words in that same document, with CSS tokens resolved. Status members additionally pair a per-status glyph. The emitted JS detail-badge maps are SUBSTITUTED from TIER_FILL/STATUS_FILL rather than hand-copied, and three drift guards hold every copy equal to the constants: the emitted JS maps byte-match the Python palettes; the CSS status tokens match STATUS_FILL and are asserted theme-invariant; and the tier-legend bijection derives its expectation from the TIER_FILL dict, so a member dropped from the legend also fails. SCOPE and its narrowing: the worded cue must exist in the same document — that it sits within eyeshot of the painted element is layout this does not re-assert. The JS detail-panel badge stays out of the LEGEND rule because its background carries its concept as the badge's own visible text.
Rationale
Colour alone excludes any reader who cannot resolve it, and the failure is silent: the page looks informative to everyone who can. A raw hex scan was rejected as the detection method because tokenised fills under-report it — the status legend paints through a CSS variable, and a hex scan finds one member of four. The substitution and the three guards exist because a hand-copied palette copy is the recurring defect here: one stale hex shipped in three separate places, and each copy that a test shares with its emitter is a copy the test cannot catch. The same-document narrowing costs a reader nothing today (every painted member is cued in its own tab panel) and a re-attestation should bless it knowingly.

Title
Text-contrast floors, closed over the vocabulary set (A4)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
CodeSymbol
STATUS_FILL/TIER_FILL/OKF_TYPE_FILL/SW_NODE_FILL/PHASE_ACCENTS + _ring_ink
Detail
Accessibility anchor A4 bound. The WCAG 2.1 AA arithmetic is owned by six sibling tests — node fills vs label ink at 4.5:1, the emitted badge/boundary pairs, the no-opacity-discount rule, theme-token fills behind white text in BOTH themes, the hub-is-not-accent regression, and the per-fill focus ring at the 3:1 boundary floor — so this row adds the BINDING plus the gap none of them owned: the SET. Closed by reflection: every UPPERCASE module-level collection holding hex members must be one of the five declared vocabularies, and every member of the REFLECTED set clears 4.5:1 against its label ink (white, except the one declared queued dark-ink exception) and 3:1 for its computed focus ring. The walk is RECURSIVE (nested dicts, tuples, sets, bare strings), counts any 3/4/6/8-digit hex form as hex-bearing, and requires every member be CANONICAL 6-digit lowercase. The theme-token half is DERIVED from the emitted CSS: every fill:var(--x) token must carry a declared role, white-text-fill roles clear 4.5:1 under white in BOTH themes, container-fill roles keep --text readable in both. SCOPE — and no wider: contrast arithmetic over declared constants and emitted theme tokens (the ring boundary also binds under SR-054 T5 as LLR-105); a lowercase module-level palette would evade the reflection and is forbidden by the module's UPPERCASE-constant convention, a recorded reliance. Rendered-pixel measurement is not asserted.
Rationale
Each sibling test sweeps a hand-copied list of the palettes, so a sixth vocabulary could ship outside every floor check while all six stayed green — the arithmetic was right and the SET was the hole. A new vocabulary must enter the arithmetic by EXISTING rather than by being remembered, which is what reflection buys and a seventh hand-copied list would not. Two shapes of that same defect must not return: a flat-dict-only walk misses nested and non-dict collections, and a hand-listed theme-token set is the very defect this row claims to close — both were live here, which is why the walk is recursive and the token set derived.

SR SR-053current content
SR-ID
SR-053
Title
Dashboard UI uniformity (rubric-adjudicated)
SN-Refs
SN-024;SN-023
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The state view the delivered generators produce shall read as one system across its tabs and views: one type scale and spacing rhythm; one status/phase/type color vocabulary applied consistently wherever the same concept renders; uniform node, edge, legend, and detail-panel styling across the SVG emitters; the same interaction idiom (expand, hover, detail) wherever the same structure appears.
Rationale
Realizes SN-024 and SN-023 — how alike is alike enough is subjective at the margins, which is why this row states the coherence property and lets each clause of it be pinned separately rather than pinning the whole to one threshold and pinning the wrong thing. Every clause has since been pinned, so the method is mechanical and the acceptance is the child chain. Fan-out re-stamp: the child count is the anchor census of the uniformity bar as it was first written — five anchors, three of them leaving a mechanized residue — not a decision count; this row states one cross-view coherence property, and every child exists because an anchor was bound to a test rather than because a second contract was merged in. Hat-derived (hat.CONSISTENCY): one meaning given two treatments across tabs, emitters and states is that charter's failure class verbatim, and it is the only lens in the roster that asks it. STATED HONESTLY RATHER THAN CLAIMED: the charter did not exist when this row was written — three independent derivations (needs-only, frame-only, hat-aware) each failed to produce this obligation, which is why it was the one row the exercise could call underivable rather than merely underived — and it was drafted to close that gap. The charter is ruled in, so this row has a deriving lens and the disposition question it carried is settled. The narrower reading survives the ruling and is the one to re-open if the charter is ever cut: no stakeholder need states cross-view coherence, so the lens — not a need — is what reaches this row. (Derived-requirement label; charter ruled, row retained.)
AcceptanceCriteria
Across every tab, view and emitter of the produced state view: every font size resolves to a step of the one declared type scale and every spacing value to the one rhythm; every status, phase and type fill resolves to its one declared colour vocabulary, with no colour reused across unrelated vocabularies and no concept painted two ways in two places; node, edge, legend and detail-panel styling is uniform across the SVG emitters, with one highlight idiom and declared weight tokens throughout; the same structure answers to the same interaction idiom (expand, hover, detail) wherever it appears — one idiom per node role. A value outside the declared token sets, a second treatment of an already-styled concept, or a second idiom for the same role is a fail; the sweep is closed over every emitter, so a new emitter joins the bar by existing.

SR-053
Title
Single-sourced concept colour vocabulary (U2 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
CodeSymbol
SW_NODE_FILL/STATUS_FILL/TIER_FILL/OKF_TYPE_FILL/PHASE_ACCENTS
Detail
The mechanized core of uniformity anchor U2 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-uniformity.md). Every concept's fill is LOOKED UP from a declared module-level palette, so the same concept cannot render two hexes — there is only one place a hex can come from. Concretely: every #rrggbb appearing in a rendered dashboard is either a member of a declared palette collection or a `--token:#hex` theme definition (surfaces, text and borders, which encode no concept). SCOPE: this proves single-sourcing of the VOCABULARY, not that a concept was assigned a well-chosen hue — collision and perceptual separation belong to LLR-102.
Rationale
A vocabulary is one vocabulary only if every member is in it: a bare hex literal outside the declared collection is invisible to the collision sweep and to the ring-ink enumeration alike, so it evades both the checks that would catch it. That is not hypothetical — a four-kind vocabulary declared only three because one badge fill sat inline in its emitter. Auditing hues by review was rejected: review cannot see a member that was never declared.
TestRefs
(see TC-109)

SR-053
Title
One interaction idiom per node role (U4 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_views.py
CodeSymbol
_drill_layer_svg/arch_icicle/dag_svg/know_graph_svg node groups
Detail
The mechanized core of uniformity anchor U4 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-uniformity.md). Two node roles exist and each is spelled ONE way in every emitter that renders it: an IDENTIFIED node (icicle .cell, flat-DAG .wi, knowledge .knode) advertises its registry id through `data-id`; a DETAIL-BEARING drill node (.block, in both the When and How-SW drills) advertises its detail-map key through `data-node`; and a node of either role is made focusable the same way, `tabindex="0"` on the group. Neither role ever carries the other's attribute. SCOPE: ATTRIBUTE parity, not runtime behaviour parity.
Rationale
One node addressed two ways means every later consumer must handle both, and the second spelling is the one that gets forgotten. Proving that two emitters' handlers BEHAVE alike was considered and rejected as out of reach without a browser harness — what is provable from the markup is that they are wired through one vocabulary, and that is the narrowing this row accepts explicitly rather than claiming more than it checks.

SR-053
Title
Pairwise deltaE floor across every vocabulary (U5 residue)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
CodeSymbol
STATUS_FILL/TIER_FILL/OKF_TYPE_FILL/SW_NODE_FILL/PHASE_ACCENTS
Detail
Mechanizes what LLR-102 leaves perceptual: a PAIRWISE deltaE floor across every declared vocabulary, not only within PHASE_ACCENTS. Two floors, each a recorded judgement — 15 WITHIN a vocabulary (every member can sit beside every other in one legend) and 12 ACROSS (two vocabularies share no legend, so the floor is lower rather than absent). The declared tier<->okf mirror is exempt BY DESIGN and is asserted BYTE-EQUAL, so 'exempt' cannot drift into 'nearly equal'. SCOPE: perceptual SEPARATION of the declared palettes. Whether a given hue suits its concept is not asserted.
Rationale
'A collision the identity check misses' looked like a judgement call only because the formula was already there and nobody had widened the set it ran over — the same arithmetic on more members. A cross-vocabulary floor of zero was rejected on evidence: a reader conflated a pair drawn from two different vocabularies, so sharing no legend reduces the risk without removing it. Three live conflations were sitting in the shipped palettes at the binding — sw external/component at deltaE 8.6, SR/phase[3] at 9.5, Interface/phase[7] at 10.5.

SR-Refs
SR-053
Title
Declared weight/alpha/corner tokens (U3 residue)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
CodeSymbol
--w-*/--o-*/--r-* tokens; SVG_RX
Detail
Mechanizes what LLR-103 leaves perceptual. Fourteen CSS tokens plus SVG_RX name one step per role for stroke-width, opacity and corner radius, near-duplicates merged into the role's step, and every emitted value must resolve to a declared token. stroke-opacity is checked under the opacity scale deliberately — it is the same scale applied to a stroke. SVG_RX is enforced against both the source literals and every render, because the rx presentation attribute cannot read a CSS custom property portably. SCOPE: token membership, asserted over paint surfaces (style blocks and style/fill/stroke attributes), never over prose that quotes CSS.
Rationale
'Spacing and exact visual weight' was undecidable because the weights were never declared: the shipped page carried 8 stroke-widths, 7 opacities, 5 corner radii and 6 SVG rx values, of which five stroke widths were doing the single job 'draw a connector' and four rx values 'round a node box'. Nothing can be uniform against an undeclared scale, so declaring the roles is what turns the judgement into a check; leaving it to review was rejected because a near-duplicate weight is exactly what an eye does not catch.

Title
Declared type scale, no raw font-size literals (U1 residue)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py
CodeSymbol
--nlabel/--nsub/--nhead + --tiny/--xsmall/--small/--body/--lead/--display/--hero + --rel
Detail
Mechanizes what LLR-104 left perceptual. 'Do the sizes read as visually uniform' was undecidable because the scale was never written down: 18 raw font-size literals against 5 tokens, with .7/.75rem, .9/.95/.98rem, 1.05/1.1rem and 8.5/9px each being near-duplicate steps for ONE role 3-7% apart. Claiming a single scale across those units would be false, so the LLR states three. SCOPE: step membership over paint surfaces. Whether the chosen steps are well-proportioned is not asserted.

SR-054Dashboard usability (rubric-adjudicated)re-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-054current content
SR-ID
SR-054
Title
Dashboard usability (rubric-adjudicated)
SN-Refs
SN-024;SN-023
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The state view the delivered generators produce shall support its core reading tasks with low friction: a first-time reviewer finds the project state, the next work, and how the parts connect, each within one tab switch; views default to a legible density (start-collapsed per the greater-than-3 rule) and reveal detail without losing context; labels stay readable at default zoom with no clipped or overlapping text.
Rationale
Realizes SN-024 and SN-023 — task-level usability is perceptual (is this findable, is this legible), so a test can confirm an element exists and not that a reader can use it. The bar is therefore written from the stakeholder intent rather than from the test, because a test written by the builder encodes the builder's own idea of usable. Most of it turned out to be pinnable and is now held mechanically; one clause about a first-time reader's experience is not, and it rests on a recorded one-time judgement rather than on a standing re-judgement — the residue named at the child, and stated there as a limit rather than implied as coverage. Fan-out re-stamp: the child count is that same anchor census, not a decision count — this row states one perceptual property, and each anchor pinned to a test as it was mechanized left an LLR behind, so the fan-out rises as the row is mechanized. Hat-derived (hat.UX-DESIGNER + hat.UX-ENGINEER): findability is the designer's — a declared reader and the decision answered without hunting (C-UXD-1) — and legibility-as-robustness is the engineer's — real widths and real volumes, nothing clipped or overlapping (C-UXE-2). Both are `always` in this repo, so unlike its two siblings this row's deriving lens is reachable today. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
A first-time reviewer reaches each core reading task — the project state, the next work, and how the parts connect — through a labelled entry point within one tab switch; every view whose element count exceeds the declared threshold starts collapsed, and revealing detail preserves the return path to the parent context; at default zoom, over the real registry volumes rather than a trimmed fixture, no label is clipped, overflows its own box, or crosses an unrelated element. Any entry point missing or unlabelled, any oversized view starting expanded, any lost return path, or any illegible label is a fail. The one clause no automated check holds — that a first-time reader in fact finds the entry points usable — rests on a recorded human judgement, a stated limit rather than an implied coverage.

Verification

Modified

Byte-deterministic; --check unchanged. This row keeps ONLY what a test cannot pin: the mechanized cores have left for child rows that name the tests owning them — T2 start-collapse (LLR-099/TC-102), T3 breadcrumb-restores-parent (LLR-100/TC-103, the whole anchor, no residue here) and T1 labelled entry (LLR-115/TC-120, the whole anchor, no residue here). Do NOT restate a child's claim here: an anchor lives in exactly one row.
Rationale
A critique that re-judges what a test already enforces spends its budget twice and can disagree with the harness, so an anchor whose bar becomes operational leaves this row entirely rather than being kept as a duplicate. T3 and T1 are the two that left whole: a critique cannot verify T3's return path from a static shot set while a test can click the crumb, and T1's 'obvious' was always glossed as labelled-not-unlabelled, which is mechanizable once written that way.

Title

Module

Title
Interactive-control contrast in both themes (T5 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
CodeSymbol
_ring_ink/_ring_style/_cedge_marker
Detail
The mechanized core of usability anchor T5 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md): EVERY control painted over a node's own fill clears the WCAG 3:1 UI-boundary floor against EVERY node fill declared in STATUS_FILL/TIER_FILL/OKF_TYPE_FILL/SW_NODE_FILL/PHASE_ACCENTS, in BOTH themes — computed per fill (white or near-black, whichever contrasts more), never a single fixed hue. Fixing only the ring was rejected once the same fixed token was found on the descend ARROW — the very control a reader must find to descend a tier — measuring 1.06:1 light and 1.99:1 dark on a shipped phase fill. One control's fix does not generalise; deriving every such control from the host fill does.

Title
Task findability - labelled entry within one tab switch (T1 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_panels.py
CodeSymbol
build_html/_next_work_html/_nav
Detail
The mechanized core of usability anchor T1 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md). Each of the three core reading tasks the rubric concretizes — find the project state, find the next work, find how the parts connect — is reachable within one tab switch of the landing view, its entry point a LABELLED nav control or a NAMED surface on the landing view, never an unlabelled region a reader must discover by clicking. THE DESIGN CONSTRAINT that makes the check honest: it is judged against the RENDERED artifact carrying real registry data, NEVER the nav skeleton. The guard resolves each task's entry point from the emitted document (task 1 -> the landing Definition-completeness/Execution cards, 0 switches; task 2 -> the landing Next-work card, 0 switches; task 3 -> the How-SW nav tab, 1 switch), asserts each is LABELLED and within one switch, and asserts the next-work surface is NON-EMPTY on the zero-active/all-queued fixture. DELIBERATELY NOT: whether the reader subjectively knows what to do next; T1 leaves the critique entirely, with no residue under LLR-055/TC-055.

The rendered-artifact constraint is the load-bearing part and must not be relaxed to a structural check: a next-work surface can EXIST in the emitter and render nothing when no row is active, so a nav-skeleton check passes on exactly the artifact a reader finds empty.

Title
Viewport-fit by scale-to-fit with a legibility floor (T7 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
CodeSymbol
_svg_fit_style/SHRINK_FLOOR
Detail
The mechanized core of usability anchor T7 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md). LLR-ID

SR-Refs
SR-054
Title
Theme-lock: one theme mechanism and no mixed-family surface/ink pair (T6 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py
CodeSymbol
HTML_TEMPLATE theme tokens + every emitter's paint
Detail
The mechanized core of usability anchor T6 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md). The measurable content is FAMILY PAIRING, not the absence of literals: a fixed status swatch carrying fixed white ink is theme-locked CORRECTLY, since neither side flips. A page inverts mid-view in exactly two ways and this row forbids both. (1) A SECOND THEME MECHANISM below :root — the document declares exactly one prefers-color-scheme block, targeting :root, and one color-scheme, also at :root. (2) A MIXED PAIR — a theme-varying ink on a theme-invariant surface, or the reverse. The two families are DERIVED from the emitted document (the dark block's override set IS the definition of theme-varying), never hand-listed, and the dark block may not override a token :root never declares. Applied to CSS rules declaring both a background and an ink, to every emitted SVG node pairing an inline rect fill with an inline text fill, and to CSS-declared SVG text fills paired against the family their host nodes actually paint in. SCOPE and its deliberate narrowings: --ring is excluded because it is stamped FROM the host fill (LLR-105 owns its floor); the JS-composed badge idiom pairs background and ink at runtime from the invariant vocabulary, where LLR-114's arithmetic owns it; and the page CSS has exactly ONE rule declaring a classifiable background AND ink today (body), so that half is prospective while the sweep's weight sits on the node and text-fill halves. Whether the chosen palettes are PLEASANT stays perceptual.
Rationale
A per-component theme override is a seam the reader crosses while scrolling, and a mixed pair reads correctly in the theme it was authored in and inverts in the other — both are invisible to the author working in one theme. Forbidding colour LITERALS was the obvious rule and is the wrong one: it fails a correct fixed-on-fixed pairing and passes a varying-on-invariant one, so it would flag the safe case and miss the defect. Deriving the families from the emitted document rather than listing them means a new token joins a family by existing.
TestRefs
(see TC-122)

LLR-ID

SR-Refs
SR-054
Title
T4 label legibility, mechanized: ink inside its box, truncation with a reveal
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_panels.py
CodeSymbol
_drill_block_label/_fit_lines/_next_work_title
Detail
The mechanized floor of usability anchor T4 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md), widening the anchor's existing owner rather than adding a rubric anchor. The clause made objective: no character a block renders may fall outside that block's own rect, horizontally or vertically. The emitter FITS the sub-label to the column it will actually be drawn in — a second line when one will not hold it, then an ellipsis — with three text lines the ceiling, reusing the grid the id/name wrap idiom already proved fits row_h; a sub-label that already fits one line renders byte-identically to before. SCOPE — PROVEN: for every block of every drill layer in the emitted document, each label line's ink box lies inside its own node rect, measured from the emitted geometry with the emitter's declared per-character widths and the document's declared font sizes, entities counted as the single glyph they render. Second half: this emitter does not truncate the next-work title by a fixed budget — the card emits the whole clause and lets its own width do the fitting, the declared bound _NEXT_WORK_TITLE caps ONE item's HEIGHT rather than its text, and where it bites the remainder discloses through a NATIVE <details> cut at a word so head and remainder rejoin continuously when opened. DELIBERATELY NOT: whether an ellipsis a reader meets is ACTIONABLE — truncated-without-affordance stays T4's perceptual residue under LLR-055/TC-055, so T4 remains a live critique anchor; the full string keeps its homes in the block's title, aria-label and data-summary.
Rationale
A label is illegible AS a label the moment its ink leaves the box it names, which is the half of this anchor a reader cannot argue with. Column right-sizing (LLR-057) cannot absorb it: right-sizing clamps at MAX_TIER_COL, so a 400-character need asks for a 2000px column and gets 172 — the overflow is structural, not a tuning problem. A FIXED character budget was the shipped approach on the next-work card and is rejected: it spends the same budget at every width, cutting a title mid-word on a wide screen with the card half empty. Native <details> is the point of the reveal — operable by pointer AND keyboard with no script, in a file opened from disk, which is what makes the affordance assertable from the markup instead of needing a browser. The height bound is measured over 320 registry rows: median 44, p90 126, max 609 characters of leading clause.

Title
No edge through an unrelated node box (T8 objective floor)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/traj_graph.py
CodeSymbol
_route_edges/_detour_d/_polyline_hits
Detail
The mechanized floor of usability anchor T8 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md). T8 is two clauses and this row claims only the first: NO EDGE PASSES THROUGH AN UNRELATED NODE BOX. SCOPE — PROVEN: across the shipped dashboard plus one fixture per emitter, every emitted wire, sampled as the polyline a reader's eye follows, clears every node rect that is not its own source or target. MEASURED at the binding: 264 shipped wires, 0 through a box. DELIBERATELY NOT: crossing count, lane separation, or where a crossing falls — that residue stays under LLR-055/TC-055, which is why T8 remains a live critique anchor. Separately and NOT claimed here: at the router's own 3px clearance rather than the bare box, one wire does graze, so _detour_d exhausting its lane candidates and returning the least-bad fallback is a reachable state rather than a theoretical one; that wire's identity and the lane-separation work belong to the open work item.
Rationale
A wire crossing an unrelated box reads as a connection that is not there, which is a wrong answer rather than an untidy one — geometry a reader cannot argue with. The claim about the ARTIFACT is deliberately separate from the router's own unit tests and is why this row exists: _detour_d returns a LEAST-BAD fallback when no candidate lane clears, so 'the algorithm detours' does not imply 'nothing shipped through a box', and the artifact-level claim had only ever been hand-verified once.

SR-070Generated views are offline, deterministic and drift-checkablere-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-070current content
SR-ID
SR-070
Title
Generated views are offline, deterministic and drift-checkable
SN-Refs
SN-010;SN-012
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered generator set shall derive each artifact it produces from the tracked registries alone — self-contained and usable without network access, byte-stable across regeneration from unchanged sources, and carrying a freshness contract that fails when the committed copy has drifted from those sources — omitting a view whose source registries the adopting repository does not carry rather than emitting an empty one.
Rationale
Realizes SN-010 and the dissolved edge expectation that a generated artifact drifting from its source fails its --check, by isolating one independently reviewable obligation formerly carried by SR-038; with SN-012, since a repository that carries no source registry pays nothing for the view that registry would have fed. ONE DECISION PER ROW: the INTEGRITY of a generated artifact and what the state view must SHOW are two unrelated obligations, and this row holds the first alone. They fail independently: a view can be perfectly offline, deterministic and drift-checkable while showing nothing about the work in flight or how the parts connect. The content half sheds along the conjunction SN-023 itself draws ("progress AND how its parts connect") to SR-168 and SR-169; this row keeps the artifact-integrity contract alone. The subject is "each artifact the generator set produces" rather than "each view" deliberately: the knowledge-export bundle is not a view, and a fourth row minted to hold it would be the filler tier the ruling forbids. The child/parent phase spread is untouched and is intended — this split is by observable class, not by phase, a partition that was considered and rejected.
AcceptanceCriteria
Regeneration from unchanged sources is byte-identical; a generated artifact opens and stays usable with no network access and no external fetch; a committed copy that has drifted from its sources fails its freshness contract while a current one passes; a repository lacking a source registry omits that view and stays byte-stable rather than emitting an empty one.
Priority
M

Modified

emit/concept/_doc_title_and_summary/on_disk

SR-137One policy home, with a checked shapere-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-137current content
SR-ID
SR-137
Title
One policy home, with a checked shape
SN-Refs
SN-028
Boundary-Refs
B-01;B-04
Requirement
The kit shall read every process policy dial from a single policy declaration home whose line shape is a checked contract (one key = value per line under a bare [section] header; no dotted keys, inline tables or multi-line strings), refusing — never resolving by precedence — a repo in which any dial is declared both there and in its legacy one-word file.
Rationale
Two grammars read this file — `tomllib` in Python and the three git hooks in pure sh, so a Python-less box still fails closed on a declared privacy gate (M-42). TOML is far more expressive than a `grep -E` can follow, so every shape only one reader understands is a silent flip of a security gate; the first cut shipped five such shapes, three of them fail-OPEN. Narrowing the shape and CHECKING the narrowing is what makes one file safe for two readers. ONE HOME: the two-grammars-agree observable — the same answer from the hooks' sh and `tomllib` over the adversarial file-shape table — is SR-031's and SR-031's alone. Both rows claimed it and the texts had already DIVERGED, only SR-031's naming the trailing-comment decoy that once made the privacy gate fail OPEN, which is the whole reason the table exists. The partition that survives: SR-031 states the package-wide coherence property (every enforcer reads one value per dial, both grammars agreeing) at B-05; this row states the dial's HOME and its CHECKED SHAPE, and the refusals a session gets back, at B-01/B-04. What stays here is the shape check's own fail-safe direction — a shape only one reader understands is refused AND still reads ON in both — because that is a property of the narrowing, not of the comparison. The crossing attribution, read against external.toml: B-02 carries exactly three things - rulings, attestations and Status flips - and this row's shall contains none of them. Its subject is the dial file's HOME and SHAPE, so its observables are the config edit arriving as a governed write through the hook floor (B-01) and the refusal going back out naming the key and both files (B-04). The contrary reading - that declaring a policy dial is itself an authority act - was weighed and REJECTED on the partition it would destroy: the dial's MEANING is SR-139's and that row keeps B-02, so putting B-02 here would give one authority observable two homes, which is the defect this campaign exists to remove. One honest strain is recorded rather than hidden: B-04's `carries` names the hook floor and subagent_gate specifically, while this row's refusals also fire at the dispatcher preflight, intake's adjudication arm and the integrator's verdict gate - session-facing guardrail verdicts of the same class, but not the two the crossing enumerates. If that enumeration is meant as exhaustive, B-04's own text is what needs widening, not this row's attribution.
AcceptanceCriteria
A dial declared in both homes produces a refusal naming the key and both files, at every guarded entry point (the dispatcher's pre-claim preflight, intake's adjudication arm, the integrator's verdict gate); a dotted key, inline table, multi-line string or hook-read key under the wrong section is refused by the shape check AND still reads ON in both readers; a wrong-typed or out-of-range dial is refused rather than defaulted.

SR-140Each acceptance is recorded by a copy riding its own approval commitre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-140current content
SR-ID
SR-140
Title
Each acceptance is recorded by a copy riding its own approval commit
SN-Refs
SN-029
Boundary-Refs
B-02
Requirement
The kit shall record each acceptance as a byte-identical copy of the accepted registries riding the same reviewed commit as the approval itself - so the commit, its date and its author come from git rather than from a cell, and the transition accepted and the acting reviewer that accepted it are distinguishable BY QUERY, an approval delegated to automation on a tier the declared ratification level released from a human one.
Rationale
Attestation STATE is already the row's own Status - derive_gate computes the gate from those cells and reads no other source - so a separate ledger was a second home for a fact the row already carried, and three of its seven columns duplicated cells outright (Decision=ratified|meaning IS Status; superseded IS SupersededBy). What the ledger got right is the BASELINE ITSELF: 'has this been ratified' was answered by walking git for the newest commit where a row read Verified, which is sound only while every amendment flips its row in the same commit - precisely the sanctioned path the staged-amendment guard deliberately ignores, and precisely what the ratification ladder deletes, so the walk returns HEAD and the diff is empty BY CONSTRUCTION on exactly the rows a sitting exists to judge. The anchor is RE-BASED onto the mechanism that was actually ruled. The on-row anchor this row used to require - a digest of the row's own NORMATIVE cells, written on the row, never in a second registry, and refused when written in the same commit as the text it anchors - is SUPERSEDED AND NOT BUILT, and the repo-lock decision's anchor half goes with it. The baseline is instead a whole-file copy of the accepted registries into docs/archive/last_approved/, riding the SAME reviewed commit as the approval (design of record: docs/plans/2026-08-15-baseline-snapshot-design.md §A2/§A3/§F3). Three things follow. First, the commit, date and author the digest design wanted written on-row come FROM the approval commit, so no cell has to carry them and no cell can lie about them. Second, the record is diffable by every git tool and by eye - a record only a script can read is a record only a script can audit. Third, THE SAME-COMMIT CLAUSE REVERSES SIGN, and the reversal is honest rather than a softening: under the anchor design a stamp written beside its own amendment HID the amendment, so it was an error; under copies the copy MUST ride the approval commit, because riding it is the only thing that makes "this copy is what was blessed" decidable. The anti-laundering property the old clause bought is carried instead by the MIRROR INVARIANT (§F3 - any commit touching the record must leave it byte-identical to its live counterpart, so the only way to write text INTO the record is to write it live first, in a reviewed commit) together with the SEED RULES (§A3 - the first copy refuses to create itself without an explicit seed, so the initial blessing cannot happen by accident, and an absent record is None rather than {} so it can never read as "re-bless everything with no diff shown"). The contradiction was caught by an independent adversarial read: this row still required the abolished design while LLR-173 claimed to implement it. A requirement its own design row contradicts is worse than no requirement, because it reports green. ONE DECISION PER ROW, and the three clauses fail in different directions, so they separate cleanly: RECORDING an acceptance (this row), REPORTING text that has moved away from the record (SR-178), and REFUSING a record written from anything but the live text (SR-179). A record that exists and rides its approval commit while nothing ever compares live text against it satisfies this row and not SR-178; a comparison that reports drift faithfully against a record a hand edit wrote satisfies SR-178 and not SR-179. The one-SR-per-pair concern is answered rather than overridden: three rows on one (SN-029, B-02) pair is what a need with three separable obligations looks like, and the alternative - one row whose partial pass has no id to report against - is the defect the split rule exists to prevent. The children partition with the obligations: LLR-173/TC-167 decompose the record here, LLR-158/TC-153 the comparison basis under SR-178, and LLR-178/TC-173 the mirror invariant under SR-179. SN-029's DELEGATED-APPROVAL RECORD is this row's, and stays: "each automated approval on a released tier appends a durable record (row, transition, acting reviewer, commit) distinguishable from a human approval by query - releasing a tier means the review is delegated, never skipped" had ZERO textual occurrence in the SR layer while `orphans=0` held (neither `delegated` nor `acting reviewer` appeared in any of the 64 rows then live). Two of its four fields are supplied by the mechanism rather than by a cell: the ROW is in the copy, and the COMMIT is the approval commit. The transition (the copy's Status against live's) and the acting reviewer (the approval commit's author) are the half that had no home, and they belong HERE rather than on SR-139 because this row is the one that says what an acceptance record contains and where it lives, while SR-139 says which tiers are held. The query-distinguishability is the load-bearing part: a record that cannot separate a machine approval from a human one turns a released tier into an unauditable one, which is the failure the need exists to prevent.
AcceptanceCriteria
A row claiming approval that no acceptance record records, or whose recorded copy reads at a status making no such claim, is reported rather than passing silently — an approval that never rode a record; once a record exists, a registry absent from inside it is itself a finding, so the vacuous state is exactly one and it is "nothing has been approved yet"; an acceptance recorded with no acting reviewer, or one whose acting reviewer cannot be told apart from a human's by query, is a finding — releasing a tier delegates the review, it does not skip it; the recorded copy is byte-identical to the accepted text and rides the same reviewed commit as the approval, so the commit, its date and its author are answerable from history rather than from any cell; and the FIRST record refuses to create itself without an explicit seed, reachable from no automated step, so the initial blessing cannot happen by accident and can only ride the acceptor's own reviewed signing commit.

SR-Refs
SR-140
Title
The last_approved snapshot: the record of what was blessed
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/baseline_snapshot.py
CodeSymbol
copy_live/load_all/rows_for/exists/stamp/is_drifted/drifted_cells/unanchored_findings
Detail
The approval RECORD, sibling to LLR-158's comparison basis (which serves SR-178's drift rule) and to LLR-178's mirror invariant (which serves SR-179's refusal): SR-140 says an acceptance is recorded as a byte-identical copy of the accepted registries riding the approval's own reviewed commit - this is where the RECORDED side lives, and the two sibling rows are the readers that make it worth recording. copy_live(root, seed=False) mirrors seven registries (the four spine tiers plus interfaces/external/components, which carry human-only approval and state cells) BYTE FOR BYTE into docs/archive/last_approved/, preserving repo-relative paths so spine_carrier.resolve/carriers/stem resolve under the snapshot root verbatim, carrier fallback included; the stale other-carrier file for a stem is deleted in the same act, or the next read raises "exists under BOTH carriers". Whole files rather than extracted rows is what makes "snapshot file == live file at the copy commit" DECIDABLE - LLR-178 carries that mirror invariant, which fails a hand edit, a partial copy and a copy-then-amend-live on one comparison - and it is also what makes the UNANCHORED rule decidable, because the copy keeps each row's own Status cell, so a live row claiming approval whose snapshot copy reads below it is an approval that never rode a copy. Three refusals are load-bearing and each is the opposite of a convenient degrade: copy_live REFUSES to CREATE the directory without seed=True, because the first snapshot blesses whatever text is in the tree and must ride the owner's reviewed signing commit (reachable only from `intake.py snapshot --seed`); load_all returns None for an absent directory and RAISES on a file that exists and will not parse, because {} and None are opposite claims and the empty one reads as "re-bless everything with no diff shown"; and rows_for is the ONE place the None sentinel is collapsed, so no caller invents its own `or {}`. is_drifted/drifted_cells delegate the cell comparison to LLR-158's split_changed_cells and arm on the RATIFIED half only. stamp() is advisory and derived FROM GIT rather than from a file, so it can never quietly become the ledger this mechanism replaced; every arm degrades to ("", ""). SR-140's two remaining record fields come from the same two places and neither is a cell: the TRANSITION is the copy's Status read against live's (the comparison above), and the ACTING REVIEWER is the AUTHOR of the commit that wrote the copy - which is why the copy must ride the approval commit and why the author is queried from git rather than stamped, since a stamped reviewer is a claim and a commit author is a record. ADVISORY TODAY: unanchored_findings returns strings and joins no failure set - it is promoted to the integrity floor at migration step 7, and not before, because against a pre-seed or pre-rename snapshot it would red every row.
Rationale
The baseline had to leave git history. The derivation it replaces walked the registry for the newest commit at which a row read Verified, which is sound only while every amendment flips its row in the same commit - and the ratification ladder deletes the flip, so the walk returns HEAD and the diff is empty BY CONSTRUCTION, on exactly the rows a sitting exists to judge. A snapshot on disk is a baseline OUTSIDE the live file, which is the one thing a walk over the live file can never be. docs/archive/ is the placement rather than an accident: check_vocab.EXEMPT_GLOBS already exempts it, and the snapshot legitimately holds the PREVIOUS vocabulary, so anywhere else reds the vocabulary enforcer on every signing. The design's own argument for whole-file copies is that a human can open the record and `git diff` it - a record only a script can read is a record only a script can audit.

SR-144Every lane close is a terminal state with an immutable recordre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-144current content
SR-ID
SR-144
Title
Every lane close is a terminal state with an immutable record
SN-Refs
SN-027
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall close a lane that cannot finish into a TERMINAL partial state that merges like any other branch, with one immutable per-close report naming the claimed outcome, the reason, the commit range and the keep/discard split — refusing a report that declares neither a split nor an explicit deferral, and refusing a second close of the same event rather than overwriting the first.
Rationale
Five successive dedup mechanisms leaked because each reconstructed the return event from a MUTABLE proxy; a document that never moves dissolves the class instead of mitigating it. The refusals are what make "immutable" a property rather than a claim. The split is a field because its absence once merged rejected code onto trunk: silence about it is different from saying "I could not judge", and only silence is unactionable. Hat-derived (hat.UNATTENDED-OPS): a claim a dead lane never releases is C-UNA-3's deadlocked-until-morning case, and the immutable per-close record is C-UNA-5's answer to a failure that pages nobody — a close no one can read is externally indistinguishable from a run that finished. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
A partial close is clean under the real validator (the terminal Deliverable rule is exempt, because an early close leaves the spec's definition byte-identical by design); a terminal spec with no report is LOUD rather than silently un-disposed; a second close is refused and leaves no residue in the lane; a multi-spec close that refuses restores every report it wrote.

LLR LLR-144current content
LLR-ID
LLR-144
SR-Refs
SR-144
Title
Terminal lane outcomes (handback + quarantine)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/handback.py
CodeSymbol
close_partial/quarantine/report_path/render_report/_restore/diff_records
Detail
close_partial: commits the lane's work as-is, moves each claimed spec to the TERMINAL partial/ (nothing re-claims it, so nothing strands) and writes ONE IMMUTABLE per-close report under docs/handbacks/ naming the claimed outcome, the reason, the commit range and the keep/discard split. The report is the close EVENT's identity — immutable so it never moves, unique so a second close is a second document — which is what dissolved the starvation class five mutable-proxy dedup mechanisms died on. A second close is refused rather than overwriting the first, and any refusal restores every report the call wrote (schedule reads queued+blockref as blocked) and the driver cannot claim and return it forever. quarantine: the ruled red arm - reverts the product paths to the merge base and keeps the failing diff as a bar-inert .patch under docs/work/handback/, reading `git diff --name-status -z` as three-field RECORDS so a rename cannot revert the wrong path, checking every git return code, and restoring rather than reporting when a revert step fails. Both leave the branch finished, so integrate.branch_outcomes reads the outcome off the spec's DIRECTORY - complete/=merged, cancelled/=cancelled, any open folder=handback - and every lane ends in a merge.
Rationale
Making the outcome the directory leaves no fourth answer and no state file that could hold one; partial work lands in trunk where a future WI can find it instead of on a branch nobody will look at.

SR-144
Title
The close ritual and the disposition it owes
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/handback.py
CodeSymbol
close_partial/render_report/report_path/_restore/_split_lines
Detail
`close_partial` writes the report FIRST (it is the event's identity), refuses when one already exists, and restores EVERY report this call wrote on any refusal — so a multi-spec lane cannot half-close. `intake._close_drafts` titles the disposition on the report PATH and nothing else, and says so LOUDLY when a terminal spec has no report rather than minting zero rows quietly. Every partial and cancelled close earns exactly one disposition row — a re-sweep mints none, a genuinely second close is a second report and so a second row, and a disposition row never mints a disposition for itself (refused at both ends); a complete close is spot-checked at the declared rate rather than gated; continuing partial work drafts a successor carrying explicit lineage that survives the id change, never re-opening a terminal row.
Rationale
The path is the only token in the event's identity that cannot move. An earlier cut folded the report's `claimed_outcome` in as well, which re-read a MUTABLE field on every sweep, so one edit minted a second disposition for the same close — the starvation class returning through a new proxy.

SR-146;SR-144;SR-148
Title
The adjudicator briefs' evidence, and the refusal that keeps a half-filled one unsent
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/adjudicate_brief.py
CodeSymbol
compose/disposition_values/red_tc_values
Detail
The row's DECLARED `Brief` cell selects the template (`intake` writes it at every adjudication mint); `compose` fills it through `prompts.fill`'s strict both-ways check and `agent_loop.session_body` is the single fork BOTH routing arms take, so a claimed adjudication row gets the same brief whether or not a routing registry is configured. Each assembler returns `(values, None)` or `(None, reason)` and NEVER a partial: `disposition_values` walks SpecRef -> the closed spec -> its immutable per-close report -> that report's TYPED `commit_range` -> `git log`/`--name-status` clipped at the declared 80 lines, and refuses the clean-close spot-check arm because it writes no report; `red_tc_values` RE-RUNS `dispatch.red_tc_census` rather than remembering it, joins each line through `dispatch.parse_red_tc` to the TC row's Method/Expected/Evidence and to the SR/LLR text its targets name, and refuses a census that has come clean. A refusal falls back to the worker assignment and PRINTS why. `conflict` and `amendment` are deliberately unrouted, each with its missing derivation named in the module header.
Rationale
Two rules decide the shape. A judge's brief must not carry the claim under judgement as its premise - a leaked self-assessment anchors the judgement to it - so every value is registry-, git- or report-derived and the one claim shown - the lane's report - is the template's own labelled subject. And a HALF-filled brief is worse than the generic prompt: hollow sections read as an investigation that was run and found nothing, which is the expensive way to be wrong, so there is no placeholder path at all. The discriminator is a declared cell rather than a SpecRef shape because that inference is unsound - an amendment to a test-case row and a red-TC census row both cite the TC registry while their briefs give contradictory instructions - and rather than a Title substring because that is the `NEEDS-HUMAN` fold (LLR-161).

SR-146Prompts are reviewable files with a per-session audit trailre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-146current content
SR-ID
SR-146
Title
Prompts are reviewable files with a per-session audit trail
SN-Refs
SN-005
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
Every prompt the delivered loop launches shall be a shipped, reviewable file with strictly filled slots — listed by digest in a freshness-gated generated catalogue, each session recording which template it used and the fingerprint of what it rendered to.
Rationale
Prose steers the sessions this loop launches and had been reviewable only by reading Python source, which makes the process trusted rather than inspectable. The audit trail is the part that makes the move useful rather than cosmetic: without a per-session digest, "which instruction did this session see" stays an inference. (The argv-vs-shell-string transport decision is design, and lives one tier down in LLR-163.) Hat-derived (hat.SECURITY): C-SEC-5 requires that content composed for dispatch to an external model runner carry a DECLARED inclusion rule rather than an implicit one — a prompt assembled inside source is an unreviewed egress path no write-side gate covers, and shipping it as a reviewable file with a digest is what makes that rule readable. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
An unknown or unfilled slot is a refusal; a missing shipped template is a named preflight refusal rather than a first-session crash; the catalogue lists every shipped prompt with its digest and `--check` fails when stale; a line-ending difference is not a digest change; an operator override is named in the session record rather than only in the launch flags.
Priority
S
Verification
Test
Status
Modified
Phase
5
Aspect
process
LLR LLR-162current content
LLR-ID
LLR-162
SR-Refs
SR-146
Title
Prompts as loaded files, catalogued and fingerprinted
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/prompts.py
CodeSymbol
load/fill/strict_check/digest/catalog_rows/preflight
Detail
`load` reads a shipped template (or an operator override) and raises a named `PromptError`; `fill` refuses an unknown slot and an unfilled declared slot; `digest` normalizes line endings before hashing; `preflight` turns a missing template into a launch-time refusal. `gen_prompt_catalog.py` renders the catalogue with a `--check` freshness gate, and `agent_common.prompt_fingerprint` records the rendered result per session beside the template's own name.
Rationale
Normalizing before hashing is not cosmetic: the same template checked out CRLF on Windows and LF on POSIX is the same prompt, and a telemetry field that said otherwise would report a model change on every clone.

SR-146
Title
Argv arrays, and the adjudicator as a routed phase
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/agent_session.py
CodeSymbol
split_cmd/build_argv
Detail
`split_cmd` recognises a JSON array ONLY when the value parses as a list, so no existing template can change meaning; a non-string token raises rather than being stringified. The prompt-transport decision reads the resulting TOKENS, so it is identical for both forms. Alongside it, `ADJUDICATE` joins `agent_route.WEIGHT_PHASES` and `agent_loop.DEFAULT_PHASE_TIER` with its own `route_intent` arm: cross-family like the reviewers, tier pinned by the row's measured estimate, and deliberately deaf to the implementer's escalation overrides.
Rationale
A silent coercion in a registry is a launch that does not match its declaration. And an adjudicator inheriting the implementer's escalation would silently re-tier a judgement because a different row's build went badly.

SR-146
Title
The generated prompt catalogue and its freshness gate
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_prompt_catalog.py
CodeSymbol
render/main
Detail
`render` is PURE — it takes no arguments and reads only the shipped templates — so `--check` and the write path cannot disagree about what "current" means. `--check` compares on NORMALIZED line endings, for the same reason the digest is taken that way: a CRLF checkout is not a stale catalogue. The file is declared in `docs/stack.ini [generated]` as kind `promptcatalog`, so the trunk lane owns it and a work branch never commits it.
Rationale
A generated artifact nobody re-generates is a document that lies, and a lying catalogue is worse than none: it invites the operator to conclude the wrong thing about the session they are debugging. Same contract `gen_arch_map --check` and `gen_skills_index --check` hold.

SR-147One machine-parseable carrier for the requirement spinere-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-147current content
SR-ID
SR-147
Title
One machine-parseable carrier for the requirement spine
SN-Refs
SN-002;SN-012
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered harness shall hold every spine tier in one machine-parseable representation, reached by a migration proven cell-for-cell over the live registries before the new representation becomes authoritative.
Rationale
THE ROW STATES ONE OBLIGATION, and the migration history - the two prior carriers and the cutover - is no part of it: normative history and a merged decision have no place at the requirement tier, and a rationale is where that account may live. The two-carrier split has no recorded rationale and costs on both sides. Reading 32 need rows takes ~166 code lines across 14 functions in 8 modules - six bespoke scanners, two of them F5 twins pinned by nothing but a docstring, which have already drifted once and rendered a phantom root in the dashboard - while reading all 436 SR+LLR+TC rows takes csv.DictReader. CSV also cannot represent the cells that actually exist: five hold a literal pipe, 552 hold commas, the longest is 1,553 characters, and an embedded newline has no representation at all. A structured carrier turns three integrity rules into properties of the parse - a duplicate id becomes a decode error, a ref list becomes a typed array (retiring the split-on-whitespace rule that read `and` as an orphan), and an empty cell becomes an absent key, so `unset` and `set to empty` stop being the same value. The converter is the migration's evidence, not its convenience: SR-129's 140-cell lesson is that an unproven representation change is exactly where a registry silently loses cells. ONE ROW because the single-carrier claim and its converter-proof are one contract — the converter is the migration's evidence — and the converter's mechanics already decompose to this row's LLR chain; splitting would separate a claim from the proof that makes it checkable. Hat-derived (hat.TEST-ENGINEER): the single-carrier claim is checkable only because the converter proves it cell-for-cell BEFORE the authority flip — the charter refuses an obligation whose enforcer has never been shown to bite, and a carrier change is precisely where a silent loss hides. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
A migration check over the live registries exits 0 reporting the round-trip clean for every row of every tier; a cell that is dropped, truncated, re-typed or re-ordered is REFUSED with exit 1 and a finding naming the registry, the row id and the field; and the emitted text re-parses under the declared format's standard parser.

SR-Refs
SR-147
Title
The spine carrier converter (markdown + CSV -> TOML)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/migrate_carrier.py
CodeSymbol
convert/rows_to_toml/sn_to_toml/compare/toml_scalar
Detail
convert() walks the four tiers and returns (findings, written_paths), so the caller decides whether a lossy conversion writes. rows_to_toml emits `[<table>.<ID>]` with the id PREFIX RETAINED and BARE - TOML allows `-` in a bare key - because ~6,400 hand-authored citations use the prefixed token and a numeric key buys no type change (TOML keys are always strings, and `001` and `1` are two legal DIFFERENT keys, which is a near-miss the prefixed form does not have). cell_to_value types by COLUMN, not by guessing: REF_COLS become arrays, INT_COLS become ints, an empty cell becomes an absent key, and everything else stays a string - this is a carrier change, not a schema change. toml_scalar emits multi-line basic strings for anything long or newline-bearing (tomllib is read-only by design, so the kit emits its own, as it already does in wi_convert.toml_string and bootstrap.set_process_key). sn_to_toml keeps the edge-case tier's own lifecycle/scenario/expected fields as THEMSELVES and writes kind= from the heading, retiring section-as-state. compare() re-reads what was emitted and diffs it against an {id: {key: text}} projection built from either carrier, so ONE comparison serves both instead of a second copy that drifts.
Rationale
The round-trip check is the requirement, not a convenience - and a check that cannot fail would be the false green SN-008 forbids, which is why the loss detector is driven against real corruption classes rather than only against a clean run. Ref cells compare on the TOKEN LIST because the conversion re-joins them with the canonical separator and the original spacing was never part of the meaning; comparing raw text there would report a loss that is not one. The edge-case fold that traj_parse._sn_fields performs is a PRESENTATION rule the markdown table forced, so it stays in the exporters - baking it into the carrier would make the exporter's reading the only reading there is.

Title
The spine carrier's one reader, and the dual-home refusal
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/spine_carrier.py
CodeSymbol
SPINE_TABLE/SPINE_COLUMN/rows_from_toml/rows_from_text/resolve/load
Detail
PURE like trace_text (text in, rows out; no git, no argv) except resolve()'s existence check, because the dual-home refusal is the one rule that must not be duplicated. rows_from_toml presents rows under TODAY'S COLUMN NAMES via SPINE_COLUMN - the exact inverse of migrate_carrier.KEY - so the carrier change is data plus a loader rather than a sixteen-module rename; SPINE_TABLE is keyed on the ID COLUMN, not on a path, because a path carries a carrier suffix and therefore moves. An absent key stays absent rather than becoming "", and a decode error returns None rather than {}. resolve() RAISES when both carriers exist rather than picking by precedence, and load() raises on a carrier that exists and will not parse. carriers() returns BOTH paths so an applicability test spans the cutover.
Rationale
None-vs-{} and absent-vs-empty are the same defect twice: {} says the registry had no rows, which for a baseline read means re-bless everything with no diff and for a gate means nothing to check, so a carrier that cannot be read must be ABSENT rather than empty. The dual-home REFUSAL follows the rule bootstrap --migrate-config already applies to the policy dials: a precedence rule lets a half-finished migration keep working while quietly reading the stale half, so the conversion is never finished and nobody finds out. The vocabulary has ONE HOME rather than a copy per script: duplicating a vocabulary fails silently - the copy that has not learned a column returns a row missing that cell, which every consumer reads as "the cell is empty".

SR-148
Title
Autonomous loop work selection: what it derives from, and in what order
SN-Refs
SN-025;SN-029;SN-027;SN-006;SN-008;SN-010
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall select the work an unattended run does next from the repository's tracked registries and git history alone — shipping no hand-curated next-work or run-phase pointer surface for any live instruction or executable surface to read, write, validate, generate or link, and deriving from no prose surface and no predefined track — in this order: ready adjudication rows first, as a stable partition applied at admission rather than by renumbering the ruled rank table; then unresolved handback records; then the earliest incomplete spine tier in SN-to-SR-to-LLR-to-TC order; then implementation work after test-case layout is complete — with the eligible set and its order deterministic, an item whose declared safety, policy or plan-mode inputs are missing, undeclared or contradictory failing closed for that item alone, human holds applied only from the declared ratification level, nothing admitted past a human-held stop, and the status surface a session reads generated and freshness-gated rather than hand-copied.
Rationale
Without one precedence rule, the same repository can resume into implementation while returned obligations or prerequisite requirement tiers remain unresolved. That produces work against stale intent and makes repeated resumes select different next actions. A recorded selection class, source record and hold decision provide replayable evidence of the choice. Absorbs SR-141 (census F5, ruled overlap): both rows stated where adjudication ranks at admission and the acceptance texts had already diverged in nuance — one SR per (SN-025, loop selection order); SR-141's stable-partition mechanism lands as an LLR under this row, and its id retires under the never-reissue doctrine, with the forwarding record in the log. ONE SR PER (need, property), and SN-025's loop work-selection had been stated THREE times — here, in SR-153 ("Ready work derives from tracked state") and in SR-059 ("Retire next-wi; generated status surface") — with the ordering claim made twice and "no hand-maintained pointer" three times. SR-153 contributed the derivation source (tracked registries and git history alone), the determinism of the eligible set and its order, and the per-item fail-closed rule for missing, undeclared or contradictory declared inputs; SR-059 contributed the no-pointer-surface obligation in its strong form (no live instruction or executable surface reads, writes, validates, generates or links one) and the generated, freshness-gated status surface. Both ids retire; `sn_refs` unions to SN-025 + SN-029 + SN-027 + SN-006 + SN-008 + SN-010 so no need loses its carrier, and every shed clause is token-checked into this row's requirement or acceptance with the map recorded in the log. The alternative — an explicit three-way partition — was rejected for the reason census F5 gave when the same question was asked of SR-141: the partition already existed textually and still produced duplicated acceptance. RESTORED AFTER AN OVER-WIDE ABSORPTION: SR-059 required the deletion of the two pointer files in BOTH directions — "fresh scaffold AND migrated repo" — and the merge carried the fresh-scaffold half only, so an adopter upgrade could retain the retired authority files while this row still passed. The migrated-repo/resync half is back in the acceptance; it is the half that matters most, because a fresh scaffold never had the files to keep. Fan-out re-stamp: this row states one decision — what an unattended run's next work derives from and in what order — and its children are the successive stages of that one selection, not separable contracts; the width is the deliberate consolidation recorded above, which folded three rows stating the same invariant into one, so re-splitting to satisfy the bound would restore the duplication that merge was convened to remove.
AcceptanceCriteria
A ready adjudication row admits ahead of every other class, the ruled rank table unchanged (--explain still reports it) and non-adjudication rows keeping their relative order; an unresolved handback is selected before spine or implementation work, and processing it either resolves the record or mints a provenance-linked successor exactly once; with no unresolved handback, only the earliest incomplete spine tier is eligible; implementation work is ineligible while any required test case remains unlaid; at human_ratification_through = 0 no spine tier produces a human hold, levels 1 through 4 hold exactly their documented cumulative tiers, and a human-held attestation still drains and surfaces with the adjudication row waiting behind the stop; every selection records the chosen class, source identifier and hold decision. Two resumes over the same tracked state select the same eligible set in the same order; an item with missing or contradictory declared inputs is excluded with a stated reason while disjoint classified work stays eligible; no prose surface participates in the derivation; the ordering and exclusion reasons are exposed for replay. A fresh scaffold ships no next-wi/run-phase pointer and is parallel-by-default; no live instruction or executable surface reads, writes, validates, generates or links such a pointer; a build-out lane's phase resolves from its branch name with no pointer file; an upgraded or resynced repository that once carried such a pointer carries neither pointer file after the migration and no live dependency on either survives, so the deletion holds for a migrated repository and not only for a fresh scaffold; the status snapshot regenerates mechanically and its freshness is gated.

Title
Admission order and the red-TC census
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/dispatch.py
CodeSymbol
_judgement_first/_admission/red_tc_census/parse_red_tc/_implemented_ids
Detail
`_judgement_first` is a STABLE PARTITION over the frontier, applied inside `_admission` before the surface/dispatch split so it can never move work past a human-held stop. `red_tc_census` rides the existing finding-string seam with a distinct prefix; `_red_tc_line` and `parse_red_tc` sit adjacent because the grammar is a contract between two modules, and `intake` routes on the parser rather than re-splitting prose. red_tc_census names, as its own class, a test case that is not green while every requirement it verifies is cited by a work item recorded done — Approved, Drafted and Modified test cases are exempt, which under the narrowed enum is the whole closed vocabulary, so the census is structurally dead in a conformant repo (retire-or-re-arm is the owner's recorded judgement item, 2026-08-15 sitting sweep); an open, partial or cancelled citer makes nothing red, and a test case verifying a mix of claimed and unclaimed targets is not red; each named line routes to an adjudication row whose suggested tier is estimated from measured breadth, never defaulted; the census line's grammar has exactly one reader (parse_red_tc).
Rationale

SR-146;SR-144;SR-148
Title
The adjudicator briefs' evidence, and the refusal that keeps a half-filled one unsent
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/adjudicate_brief.py
CodeSymbol
compose/disposition_values/red_tc_values
Detail
The row's DECLARED `Brief` cell selects the template (`intake` writes it at every adjudication mint); `compose` fills it through `prompts.fill`'s strict both-ways check and `agent_loop.session_body` is the single fork BOTH routing arms take, so a claimed adjudication row gets the same brief whether or not a routing registry is configured. Each assembler returns `(values, None)` or `(None, reason)` and NEVER a partial: `disposition_values` walks SpecRef -> the closed spec -> its immutable per-close report -> that report's TYPED `commit_range` -> `git log`/`--name-status` clipped at the declared 80 lines, and refuses the clean-close spot-check arm because it writes no report; `red_tc_values` RE-RUNS `dispatch.red_tc_census` rather than remembering it, joins each line through `dispatch.parse_red_tc` to the TC row's Method/Expected/Evidence and to the SR/LLR text its targets name, and refuses a census that has come clean. A refusal falls back to the worker assignment and PRINTS why. `conflict` and `amendment` are deliberately unrouted, each with its missing derivation named in the module header.
Rationale
Two rules decide the shape. A judge's brief must not carry the claim under judgement as its premise - a leaked self-assessment anchors the judgement to it - so every value is registry-, git- or report-derived and the one claim shown - the lane's report - is the template's own labelled subject. And a HALF-filled brief is worse than the generic prompt: hollow sections read as an investigation that was run and found nothing, which is the expensive way to be wrong, so there is no placeholder path at all. The discriminator is a declared cell rather than a SpecRef shape because that inference is unsound - an amendment to a test-case row and a red-TC census row both cite the TC registry while their briefs give contradictory instructions - and rather than a Title substring because that is the `NEEDS-HUMAN` fold (LLR-161).

SR-ID
SR-149
Title
Retired-vocabulary refusal in authored surfaces
SN-Refs
SN-004;SN-010
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The harness shall report every occurrence of a retired process tag in a live authored surface — naming the file and line and the vocabulary that replaces it, at a severity that warns by default and fails under --strict — reporting none in a declared historical, generated or attestation-quoting surface, nor in a line or file marked as a declaration site.
Rationale
Realizes SN-004 (the ladder's vocabulary is the one the project is held to) and SN-010 (docs stay honest). This check is a CONDITION of the vocabulary conversion rather than a follow-up, on measured evidence: an earlier sweep removed a retired-tag construct from this repo's own open-items registry and it REGENERATED within days, caught only by an audit. A ~2,500-edit conversion held in place by attention is a conversion that comes undone. The carve-outs are as load-bearing as the rule: history is not rewritten and an attestation is never re-worded, because a swept sign-off makes a signed record claim something was signed that was not. Hat-derived (hat.MAINTAINER): C-MNT-3 gives every declared vocabulary value exactly one normative definition in one home; a retired tag surviving in a live authored surface is a second, contradictory definition of the same word, which is why the earlier sweep regenerated within days when only attention held it. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
A planted retired tag in a live authored file is reported with its path and line, at exit 0 under the default severity and exit 1 under --strict; a file under any declared carve-out class (archive, the project log, attestation briefs, closed and cancelled work-item specs, generated surfaces) reports nothing, including at arbitrary depth; a line carrying the allow marker or a file carrying the allow-file marker reports nothing; and the word "gate" used to mean a check that can fail reports nothing.

SN-Refs

Boundary-Refs

Requirement
The harness shall report each stakeholder-need `need` cell that contains an internal path, an implementation-only identifier or a process citation, naming the row and the offending phrase — at a severity that warns by default and fails only under --strict, exempting a name declared on a reviewed exception list that ships empty (a name that is itself a user-facing interface belongs in a need), and reporting nothing for acceptance or engineering-requirement cells, which SN-033 exempts by its own text.

AcceptanceCriteria

LLR LLR-170current content
LLR-ID
LLR-170
SR-Refs
SR-150
Title
Need-form checker
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/check_need_form.py
CodeSymbol
scan/need_findings/load_allow
Detail
Reads the needs registry through spine_carrier.load_needs (either carrier answers; an unreadable registry REFUSES loudly rather than scanning as empty), and a registry that is PRESENT but yields zero scannable need cells - an empty file, or real rows with no need field - is reported VACUOUS rather than clean, while an absent registry stays the pre-scaffold clean skip and a -000-only scaffold registry stays a blank form (REVIEW-A round 1 drove the hole: at DevStg-Reqs an emptied registry hard-failed nothing else in the harness). The three token classes are data (CLASSES), labelled with SN-033's own vocabulary so a finding names the class the acceptance names. Two scope decisions are deliberate and documented in the docstring: SN-### is NOT a process citation (the SN registry is the stakeholder's own document, so a need pointing at a sibling need keeps the reader at the stakeholder tier - the live SN-025 case), and a single-slash, dot-free token is a path only when it RESOLVES in the scanned tree (docs/archive names a real directory and reports; English either/or pairs like subjective/perceptual resolve to nothing and stay exempt - review round 1 narrowed the original blanket exemption). URL spans - scheme:// and scheme-less www. forms alike - are suppressed whole, so a path-shaped tail inside an external address never reports under any class; the span stops at prose delimiters (comma/semicolon) and the www. form requires a multi-label host, so a genuine token abutting a URL and a local www.assets/logo.png both still report (round 3); a sentence-final token's trailing full stop is stripped before the phrase is judged or reported, so its reviewed exception matches (round 2). One finding per token: a later class re-matching inside an already-reported span is the same phrase to rewrite. The exception list (docs/need-form-allow) requires the ' — <reason>' separator per entry, the literal em-dash with spaces (ALLOW_SEP) - a line authored with a plain hyphen, or with no separator at all, declares nothing, so a malformed entry can only ever fail LOUD, never silence a finding. Wired as check.py's need-form step at every bar with NO --strict promotion, deliberately unlike check_vocab's severity ladder: promotion is an owner ruling, and the wiring says so.
