Where work is declared as contested planning, the delivered loop content shall produce the selected decomposition through a bounded round of two independently authored rival plans — cross-critiqued, mechanically coverage-compared and arbitrated by fresh sessions — committing the selected outcome atomically as registry-valid queued rows and surfacing a non-converging round through the declared ratification level, never falling back to a single uncontested plan silently.
Rationale
Realizes SN-024 (a decomposition judged by a competing plan and an independent arbiter rather than its author), with SN-026 (the planner pair prefers distinct families), SN-002 (the selected outcome lands as registry-valid rows, not prose) and SN-006. One row because the dual-plan round is one delivered capability with one observable outcome class (SELECT or PAGE); its state machine, briefs, adapters and isolation rules are the component tier's. Fan-out re-stamp: the round is one delivered capability with one observable outcome class, and its children are the successive phases of that single round — planner pair, briefs, coverage comparison, arbitration, isolation, disposition — none of which an adopter can obtain, refuse, or observe independently of the round, so the child count measures the round's step count rather than a merge of contracts.
AcceptanceCriteria
A row marked for contested planning is refused on the direct build path; the round runs planner pair, cross-critique, mechanical coverage comparison and position-swapped arbitration as fresh sessions within declared budgets; SELECT files children that pass the registry validator atomically; PAGE preserves the round's evidence and maps through the declared ratification level.

SR-156Bounded lanes narrowing to one gated landingapproval owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-156current content
SR-ID
SR-156
Title
Bounded lanes narrowing to one gated landing
SN-Refs
SN-027;SN-008;SN-025
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall run ready work on bounded, mutually isolated lanes and narrow every lane back to the integration branch through one serial, fail-closed seam that runs the declared bar on the composed tree — parking a red candidate and stopping the queue loudly, draining claiming to a clean merged stop under a declared pause, and reconstructing claim and queue state from version-control history alone at any crash boundary, never a half-integrated authoritative state.
Rationale
Realizes SN-027 WHOLE — ready work fans out across bounded parallel lanes and that fan-out narrows to one serialized gated seam before anything lands, in the need's own words — with SN-008 (the seam is fail-closed) and SN-025 (recovery from version-control history alone). ONE DECISION PER ROW: "serial integration is one delivered contract" is sound for the SEAM, but lane execution, claim authority and the trunk-side mint of work-item identity are not the seam. The measured corroboration: no SR in the corpus stated SN-027's FIRST half at all (a grep of every requirement cell for bounded lanes, isolated working trees or a worker ceiling returned nothing), so the merge was hiding a coverage hole rather than expressing one contract — exactly what the fan-out detector exists to surface. This row therefore states the whole lane LIFECYCLE (claim, run isolated, close, land), which is what closes that hole; the trunk-only authority contract sheds to SR-170. The two fail independently: a perfectly serialized landing that lets a lane commit a regenerated artifact satisfies this row and not that one. OVERREACH RETRACTED: the widening had also taken "closing a lane that cannot finish into a terminal state", which is SR-144's shall verbatim in substance ("close a lane that cannot finish into a TERMINAL partial state that merges like any other branch") under the same SN-027 — two rows for one observable, against the one-SR-per-(need, property) rule, and the sign of it was LLR-144 sitting here while SR-144 stated its contract. The clause and its handback.py carrier are struck; what remains is this row's own half, that a terminal branch still lands through the one serial seam. LLR-144 re-parents to SR-144 with TC-138.
AcceptanceCriteria
A launch with independent ready work runs up to the configured lane ceiling concurrently in isolated working trees, and a single-lane setting preserves the serial semantic; a finished branch reaches the integration branch only through the serial seam with the declared bar green on the composed tree — a red parks the candidate and stops the queue loudly; a declared pause stops claiming and drains in-flight lanes to a merged stop; a lane closed under the terminal-close contract (SR-144's, not restated here) reaches the integration branch through this same serial seam and by no other path; a crash at any lifecycle boundary recovers from version-control history without double-assignment.

LLR-145
SR-Refs
SR-156
Title
Link-aware spec-move ritual (move + relink indivisible)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/spec_move.py
CodeSymbol
move_spec/expected_relink/archive_dest
Detail
move_spec moves a registry/spec file and keeps every markdown link in the repo true as ONE indivisible operation: the moved file's own relative targets are re-relativised against its new directory, every inbound link whose target resolves to the vacated path is redirected (link text untouched, #fragments carried, external/protocol-relative/bare-anchor targets left alone, root-relative targets excused only in the rebase half, line endings preserved via newline=''), and every rewritten file is staged with the move so the ritual lands as one changeset. integrate.claim and handback.close_partial call it for the claim and close moves; the CLI performs the worker's terminal close move and the dated docs/archive/specs/ archival. expected_relink is the pure oracle integrate._abandoned_claim compares byte-for-byte to excuse a crashed claim's own relink writes without widening its conviction to any .md edit.
Rationale
A bare move strands both link directions and the break surfaces late, on the composed tree (measured: one probe archival took the broken-link count from 4 to 8 - 3 outbound, 1 inbound). Making move+relink one indivisible operation is a constraint that leaves the two-thirds state unrepresentable, rather than a check that detects it after the parallel work is done.

Title
Lane mechanics: worktree, worker subprocess, §A2 refresh subprocess
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/lane.py
CodeSymbol
ensure_worktree/worker_argv/spawn_worker/run_worker/spawn_refresh
Detail
One lane's mechanics extracted from the dispatcher (docs/concurrency-v2.md §A4.2): ensure the lane worktree (integrate.lane_worktree, the one home both the worker and the refresh agree on), build the worker argv with the plain launch's session dials forwarded explicitly, launch the worker subprocess (spawn_worker non-blocking for the lane table and dispatch._launch's own default; run_worker its blocking form - a DECLARED SEAM with no in-kit caller, kept for a synchronous single-lane run, and the shape dispatch._launch's worker= injection substitutes), and run the §A2 station refresh as the lane's OWN subprocess (spawn_refresh - the invoker's integrate.py refresh CLI under the harness interpreter) so N lanes' bars overlap instead of queueing at the dispatcher (§A4.3). Reports outcomes, decides none: the tree signal (specs moved out of active/<branch>/) plus exit codes are the whole protocol - no state file, no back-channel.
Rationale
The §A4.2 split keeps the deciding half (dispatch.py) apart from the running half, so at lanes=1 the dispatcher degenerates to the serial loop and the concurrency is a table size, not a second code path.

Title
Dispatch-lock claim rung + the spine batch claim
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/integrate.py
CodeSymbol
_dispatch_lock/claim/_claim_refusal/_abandoned_claim
Detail
The §A4.1 authority flip's integrator half (docs/concurrency-v2.md §A4.1): _claim_refusal's safety_class != ordinary arm is DELETED (admission is the dispatcher's decision) and the claim REQUIRES the dispatch lock (agent_common.dispatch_lock_path, out/agent-loop.lock - the lock a live dispatcher holds for its process lifetime), acquired at ONE site (_dispatch_lock, mirroring _slot's one-site discipline for the merge slot's own lock) with the ladder run before it so the clean-trunk rung never refuses over the lock's own file: a hand claim on an idle station works, a hand claim during live lanes is unrepresentable, and the dispatcher's in-process claim passes dispatch_lock_held=True. claim/_claim_refusal/_abandoned_claim/_claim_delta are batch-aware: a spine batch is ONE claim commit moving every batched spec (subject joins the ids; the single-WI subject stays byte-identical), and integrate() takes an optional branches= restriction so the dispatcher merges each lane's branch as its own refresh completes.
Rationale
A constraint instead of a check: the state the old refusal guarded against (a claim written beside live lanes) is now unrepresentable, and the batch makes N spine changes cost one re-attest window (§A4).

SR-ID
SR-157
Title
Spine and work-registry rules red the harness verdict
SN-Refs
SN-002;SN-012;SN-025
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered harness shall report every declared spine and work-registry rule violation — broken joins, integrity and schema faults, leftover placeholders, requirement-form findings and work-item coherence contradictions — naming the at-fault row and cell, gating the declared failure set at the declared gate while advisory classes never change the exit code.
Rationale
Realizes SN-002 (the chain is mechanically verified, not asserted — this row is the boundary-level home of the verdict SN-002's acceptance asks for), SN-012 (optional registries and opt-outs cost a non-user nothing) and SN-025 (queue-overlap visibility). One row because the adopter-observable contract is one: registry rules produce named findings at a declared severity; which rules, at which severities, is the component detail the rows below it decompose. Deliberate pair (census F6): the PB-format invariant itself is SR-015's; this row's decomposition carries the checker. Fan-out re-stamp: the delivered contract is one — a named, row-and-cell-attributed finding at a declared severity, gating or advisory — while the rule inventory it ranges over is open by this row's own acceptance ("a rule added at one of those sites is in this row's scope by default"), so the child count tracks the number of declared rules rather than the number of decisions this row makes, and would climb back through any bound a split bought.
AcceptanceCriteria
Each declared registry rule violation is reported with row-level attribution; the declared strict set exits nonzero at its gate and advisory classes never join the exit code; absent or placeholder-only optional registries and declared opt-outs are vacuous; the declared verification vocabulary (including Critique) is accepted and an unknown value fails. The declaration this is read against is the rule inventory the delivered checkers themselves publish — the id shapes, the required cells and the closed vocabularies (with the interface and component entries advisory by their own recorded ruling), the strict flag families and their stated exit contracts, the gating form and provenance findings beside the never-gating paraphrase and acceptance advisories, and the work-item coherence rules with their declared opt-out — each recorded at its declaration site, not restated here. A rule added at one of those sites is in this row's scope by default: an unreported one is a finding against this row, not an amendment to it.

LLR LLR-133current content
LLR-ID
LLR-133
SR-Refs
SR-157
Title
Spine stand-alone rule
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/trace_text.py
CodeSymbol
provenance_findings
Detail
provenance_findings flags a WI id or a process-doc citation across the normative cells of all three spine registries, joining the --strict failure set via exit_code. The normative cells are the SR Title/Requirement/Rationale/AcceptanceCriteria, the LLR Title/Detail/Rationale, and the TC Method/Expected/Parameters; pointer columns are out of scope, and each finding names the registry, row and cell. Not a finding: an artifact path, a rubric, a sibling spine id, and a port-script name — the ten declared external ports (check.py, bootstrap.py, agent_loop.py, check_vendored.py, gen_cases.py, gen_release_checklist.py, subagent_gate.py, run_menu.py, integrate.py, trunk_step.py) — the script-name carve-out reaching no wider than that set.
Rationale
A citation is a pointer a later reader may be unable to follow, and the row's reason travels with it: when the work item is closed and archived, the row keeps its claim and loses its justification, which is the failure this catches. Applied to every spine registry rather than the SR alone, because the layer the rule could not originally see held the largest pocket of violations — a narrower scope would guard the cleanest layer and miss the dirtiest.

LLR LLR-134current content
LLR-ID
LLR-134
SR-Refs
SR-157
Title
Requirement form rules
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/trace_text.py
CodeSymbol
form_findings
Detail
form_findings applies six rules — one obligation per requirement, no non-obligation modal, no actorless passive, no unfalsifiable term, no open-ended clause, no obligation keyword in an LLR Detail — skipping Draft rows, and joins the --strict failure set via exit_code. The negative set holds: a Draft row, a zero-obligation row, a project's own declared alternate obligation keyword, and a multi-clause AcceptanceCriteria are not findings — stated keyword-free here deliberately, since the rule cannot distinguish a mention from a use.
Rationale
A row carrying two obligations reports neither when one fails, and undecidable wording is signed on impression rather than evidence — that is the failure all six rules exist to prevent. Draft is skipped because a Draft row is exempt from the child-completeness rules elsewhere in this harness and is by convention the state a requirement occupies while still being written, so an unfinished acceptance criterion is expected there rather than defective; flagging it would make the state unusable for the drafting it exists to allow. This cell says "obligation keyword" rather than quoting the keyword itself because the rule cannot distinguish a mention from a use, and an exemption a checker cannot see is one an author can reach for.

(see TC)

LLR LLR-135current content
LLR-ID
LLR-135
SR-Refs
SR-157
Title
Paraphrase advisory
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/trace_text.py
CodeSymbol
paraphrase_advisories
Detail
paraphrase_advisories compares a child cell's significant words (alphabetic runs of four characters or more, lowercased) against its parent's and reports when the share of the CHILD's words that also appear in the parent exceeds the declared threshold — the child is the denominator, so a long parent cannot dilute a short echoing child. Reported in its own advisory pipe, which never joins a failure set. The two watched relationships are an SR Rationale against its own Requirement and an LLR Detail against its parent SR Requirement — the pairs where the parent is unambiguous; each finding emits a stdout WARNING plus a report line with the exit code unchanged.
Rationale
A child that re-words its parent adds a second home for one fact and no decomposition, so a later amendment updates one copy and the two disagree with nothing to report it. The advisory has its own pipe rather than sharing the acceptance-criteria counter because a shared count would report 'ac-advisories' about a finding that is not one, and a reader acts on the counter name.

LLR-160
SR-Refs
SR-157
Title
The mechanical queue-overlap pre-filter
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/check_trajectory.py
CodeSymbol
queue_conflict_findings/_title_tokens
Detail
Three signals over OPEN rows only — Jaccard over title token sets at a deliberately high bar, shared `SR-Refs`, and a shared anchor-stripped `SpecRef` — each reported once per pair per signal with both ids, in sorted order. Printed on the warn-only line beside backlog staleness; never appended to `errors`. A shared stopword alone is never a signal, and the findings stay out of the exit code even under --strict.
Rationale
A low similarity bar turns a useful warn into noise that gets ignored, which is worse than not having the warn: this rung catches the same job minted twice, not two rows in the same area.

LLR LLR-179current content
LLR-ID
LLR-179
SR-Refs
SR-157
Title
EARS statement-pattern advisory
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/trace_text.py
CodeSymbol
ears_advisories
Detail
ears_advisories reads the opening of each non-example SR Requirement cell and reports the row when that opening is a condition dressed in a keyword outside the four EARS condition keywords (When / While / If / Where) — a bare subject (the ubiquitous pattern) and an empty cell pass. Reported in its own advisory pipe, which never joins a failure set; the scan covers Drafted rows, unlike the gating form rules in the same module, and the SR tier alone.
Rationale
EARS puts the condition in front of the subject so a reader learns when a requirement applies before what it obliges, which is also what makes the opening scannable by eye and by tool; a row opening 'Before X, the system shall …' carries the same condition where nothing looks for it. Advisory rather than gating deliberately: which pattern a row IS — ubiquitous with a qualified response, or event-driven with the trigger fronted — is a judgement about the obligation, and the same sentence can be honest in either shape, so a gating version would overrule the author on the one question the author is better placed to answer. Drafted rows are in scope because an opening is finished the moment it is written and a warn costs a drafter nothing, and because both rows this rule found at landing were Drafted — skipping them would have shipped a guard that had never once fired.

(see TC-174)

SR-158Documentation drift reds or warns per its declared tierapproval owed

SR SR-158current content
SR-ID
SR-158
Title
Documentation drift reds or warns per its declared tier
SN-Refs
SN-010;SN-008
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered harness shall report a documentation surface that has drifted from what it claims — a broken intra-repo link, a missing vision declaration, a reference to an absent path or symbol, a stale generated doc, or a declared figure without its provenance — at the declared warn-or-gate severity for each class.
Rationale
Realizes SN-010 (navigable, honest documentation) and SN-008 (a green never hides a stale claim). One row because the delivered contract is one honesty verdict over the documentation surfaces; the individual checkers and their carve-outs are component details.
AcceptanceCriteria
A dead intra-repo link, an absent or repeated vision declaration, a stale generated doc under the staleness check, a dangling backticked path or symbol reference, and a declared figure marker missing its command or revision each produce a finding at their declared tier; the declared carve-outs (generated blocks, per-line and per-file allow markers, unmarked figures, URLs and placeholders) stay silent. The declared tiering, read off each class's declaration site rather than restated: link and vision findings gate from the first requirements bar on; orphans and staleness warn by default, orphans gating only under their declared strict flag; the reference and figure checks gate at the release bar, gating on dangling references alone, with untraced ones counted and never gating. A class whose severity is stated at no declaration site is undeclared, and this row is unsatisfied until it is declared there.

SR-165The component partition carries a reproducible derivation recordapproval owed

SR SR-165current content

SR-165
Title
The component partition carries a reproducible derivation record
SN-Refs
SN-040
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered planning content shall leave, with every component partition it produces, a machine-readable derivation record in the tracked component registry rather than session prose — the candidate partitions considered, the declared objective and constraints they were scored against, each candidate's score, the selected partition, and the human ruling that named the components and adopted it — such that a live component row whose record omits any of those elements, or a selection the recorded scores do not rank first without a recorded human override, is a reportable finding.

SN-040 had no citing SR, even as the P5 partition work performed exactly this record. B-05 alone: the human ruling is an element the record must carry, not an observable the system states at the authority input, which is SR-139's and SR-140's ground.
AcceptanceCriteria
A partition round writes the derivation record into the tracked component registry, never into session prose or a plan document alone; a live component row with no record, or a record omitting the candidate partitions, the declared objective, the constraints, any candidate's score, the selected partition or the human ruling, is reported naming the missing element; a selected partition the recorded scores do not rank first is reported unless the record carries the human override that chose it; the recorded ranking recomputes from the record's own objective, constraints and scores alone.

Title

Module

CodeSymbol

Detail
Extends the existing component pass — the one home that already reads docs/requirements/components.toml and already emits CMP findings — with the derivation-record rules SR-165 states, rather than adding a second reader of the same registry. Per live CMP row: the record must declare the candidate partitions considered, the objective and the constraints they were scored against, a score per candidate, the selected partition, and the human ruling that named the components and adopted it; a missing element is one finding naming the row AND the element. Two derived rules on top of presence, because presence alone is satisfiable by a record that says nothing: (1) THE RANKING RECOMPUTES — re-rank the recorded candidates from the record's own objective, constraints and scores and compare with the recorded order, a mismatch being a finding, which is what makes the record falsifiable without a reviewer re-running a model session; (2) A SELECTION THE SCORES DO NOT RANK FIRST is a finding unless the record carries a human override naming that choice, so a legitimate "the scores said A, the human chose B" is expressible and an unexplained one is not. Vacuous by construction on a project declaring no components, and on the example -000 rows. NOT BUILT YET: the record cells do not exist on the CMP schema today, which is why this row and TC-166 are Draft — SR-165 was flipped Inspection -> Test at act 7 precisely because the check is mechanizable, and this is the debt that flip incurred, recorded rather than silently owed.
Rationale
The check belongs where component_findings already is for the reason the census keeps applying: a second module reading components.toml would be a second answer to "is this partition recorded", and the two would drift. The recompute is the load-bearing half — an element-presence check passes on a record whose scores contradict its own conclusion, which is the exact failure SN-040 exists to catch (the next reviser cannot tell load-bearing boundaries from accidents). The override arm is what stops the recompute rule from forbidding human judgement: the requirement is that a departure from the scores is RECORDED, never that it is disallowed.
TestRefs
TC-166

Drafted

SR-166The delivered package materializes where its manifest declares it willapproval owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-166current content
SR-ID
SR-166
Title
The delivered package materializes where its manifest declares it will
SN-Refs
SN-001;SN-007
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered package shall be consumable at its own declared shipped-file manifest — every file the manifest names materialized at its declared destination in a fresh scaffold, and this repository's own instance carrying the structure of the template it ships — with a declared destination a fresh scaffold does not carry, and a structural divergence between the shipped template and this repository's instance, each failing rather than degrading the delivered package silently.
Rationale
Realizes SN-001 (an adopting team gets a working, gated, requirement-traced process without hand-building the tooling — which presupposes that what arrives is whole) and SN-007 (the kit is held to the standard it ships). §1R.6 names this as B-05's own artifact-shaped observable — the package "exists, is complete, and is consumable" — and records that its manifest is already pinned; the 2026-08 re-tier campaign minted fifteen rows and not this one, leaving the crossing the frame describes at greatest length with no requirement stating it, and MAPPING surviving only in SR-163's rationale. Verification evidence is named rather than owed: tests/test_bootstrap.py drives bootstrap.py's MAPPING against the file lists a fresh scaffold must carry, and tests/test_dogfood_sync.py pins this repository's own instance against the template it ships, so the manifest cannot declare one thing and the package deliver another. Deliberately distinct from its two neighbours: SR-163 asks why each shipped file EXISTS (SN-038, purpose coverage), SR-010 asks whether the generated scaffold runs GREEN, and this row asks whether what was promised ARRIVES WHERE IT WAS PROMISED. DELIBERATELY NARROW: as first written, this row ALSO required that a named source the package does not carry, and a shipped file the manifest does not name, each FAIL — and SR-163 already reports both classes under a configurable warn-versus-gate policy. Two rows prescribing different outcomes for one observable is a contradiction an implementer can only resolve by violating one of them, so the two manifest-PRESENCE clauses are shed here and referenced to SR-163; each was token-checked into SR-163's requirement ("missing files, stale inventory entries") and acceptance ("a shipped file absent from it", "a stale entry naming a file that no longer ships") before deletion, so no obligation vanished — only its second, conflicting statement. What is left is what SR-163 does not ask: not whether a file is accounted for, but whether it LANDS — at its declared destination in a fresh scaffold, and in this repository's own instance of the template it ships.
AcceptanceCriteria
A manifest entry whose destination a fresh scaffold does not contain fails naming the destination; and a structural divergence between the shipped template and this repository's own instance fails naming the surface. Manifest PRESENCE is deliberately NOT restated here — a named source the package does not carry, and a shipped file the manifest does not name, are both SR-163's, reported under its declared warn-versus-gate policy.

SR-167Performance-budget breaches red their hard-gated rowsapproval owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-167current content
SR-ID
SR-167
Title
Performance-budget breaches red their hard-gated rows
SN-Refs
SN-008;SN-012
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered harness shall fail the bar it runs exactly when an in-tier hard-gated performance-budget row breaches — its measured metric worse than its absolute Budget, or regressed beyond its declared Tolerance band around the committed baseline — while warn-gated rows and out-of-tier rows only warn, and a metric with no measurement this run is a reported skip, never a silent pass.
Rationale
Realizes SN-008 (a reader can believe a green — a breach on a row the project chose to hard-gate must red the run, not ride as a tracked number) and SN-012 (the perf layer is opt-in; a repo declaring no PB rows pays nothing, while one that does gets the same honest-gate discipline as every other check). THE TOLERANCE-BREACH EXIT CONTRACT WAS BUILT, DECOMPOSED AND PROVEN BEFORE ANY REQUIREMENT STATED IT - fully built (check_perf.py evaluate, the exit contract stated in the module docstring), fully decomposed (LLR-014 "Budget comparator", whose detail states it verbatim) and fully proven virtualized (TC-014 -> tests/test_check_perf.py: test_regression_beyond_tolerance_fails_when_gate_fail end-to-end against a bootstrapped scaffold, test_regression_uses_direction_and_tolerance at unit level, test_within_tolerance_regression_passes the passing arm, test_absolute_breach_fails_when_gate_fail the absolute arm) — yet stated by no requirement: LLR-014 rides SR-006 (the step plan and missing-tool refusal), IF-004/IF-031 ride SR-006 + SR-015 (PB back-link resolvability), and SR-033 states only the warn-tier, never-fails side, so the verdict the interfaces' contracts describe had no requirement home. This row is that home. One row for BOTH breach arms deliberately — absolute and regression are one delivered exit contract, the SR-157 one-verdict shape, and a regression-only framing is the symptom, not the boundary. THE REQUIREMENT NAMES NO ARTIFACT: check_perf.py is NOT on the §1R.2 declared B-05 artifact list — it is the harness's internal perf step (IF-004, check_perf -> check) — so the SR states the harness-verdict observable and the comparator is named only in acceptance as the current carrier, the SR-157/SR-158 pattern. Vacuous in THIS repository — no performance-budgets.csv exists here and §9's perf layer is declared absent — but the template ships the layer, and the owner's ruling is that a shipped layer owes a stated, tested obligation even where locally unused. IF-004/IF-031 req_refs gain this row in the same act (off-spine, approval drafted); LLR-014.sr_refs and TC-014.verifies are deliberately NOT re-pointed here — both rows are Approved, so amending their cells overrides attestation, which is the sitting's act: when taken, LLR-014 adds this row beside SR-006 and TC-014 verifies it beside LLR-014. Hat-derived (hat.PERFORMANCE): the charter's third clause — what happens when the budget is exceeded — is this row's entire subject, and no other lens in the roster asks it. That dependency is CLOSED: the PERFORMANCE charter is ruled `always`, so the lens this row derives from applies unconditionally rather than waiting on a tag; the rationale above already concedes the row exists because a layer ships rather than because a need asks, and naming the lens is what turns that from accretion into a reviewable derived requirement. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
An in-tier hard-gated performance-budget row whose measured metric breaches its absolute Budget, or falls outside its declared Tolerance band around the committed baseline, makes the perf step exit nonzero naming the row and the bar red with it; the same breach on a warn-gated row, or on a row outside the run tier, exits zero and warns; a hard-gated row with no measurement this run is a per-row reported skip, never silent; a row with no baseline or no tolerance cannot fail on the regression arm while its absolute arm still applies; a reviewed baseline update is the acceptance path for an intended regression, proven end to end against a bootstrapped scaffold.

SR-169The state view shows how the parts connectapproval owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-169current content
SR-ID
SR-169
Title
The state view shows how the parts connect
SN-Refs
SN-023
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered generator set shall render the adopted repository's declared components and the interfaces between them as a navigable graph — showing which components sit inside which at more than one level, drawing an interface whose endpoint lies inside an unopened container to that container's edge, and showing a stated "no interfaces declared" view rather than an empty one when the repository carries the component and interface registries but declares no interface in them (a repository carrying no such registry at all omits the view under SR-070, not under this row).
Rationale
Realizes SN-023's SECOND conjunct, in the need's own words: the relationships between the parts (which module talks to which, over what contract) are as much of the project's truth as its requirement tree, and scattering them hides architectural drift. ONE DECISION PER ROW: artifact integrity is SR-070's, view content is this tier's, and the content half divides exactly where SN-023 divides. Kept separate from SR-168 rather than filed as one content row because the two fail independently and are built by different means — the progress views read the requirement and work registries, this one reads the declared component and interface registries, and either can be complete while the other is absent. The empty-view clause is load-bearing: a repository that has declared no interfaces yet must get a stated, legible surface, because an empty graph is indistinguishable from a broken generator. WORDED FOR A COLD READER: the row had read "structural units", "seams terminating at the containing boundary" and "the artifact contract's omission rule" — vocabulary a first-time reader cannot resolve, the last of them an internal cross-reference with no id. Same obligations, plainer words, and the absent-registry case now names SR-070 explicitly; the empty-vs-absent split itself is unchanged. `phase` inherits the parent's 1; its children span 1 and 2, the ordinary child-later-than-parent case, which is intended.
AcceptanceCriteria
Declared components render with their containment preserved at each visible level; each declared interface connects its correct endpoints, and one whose endpoint lies inside an unopened container is drawn once at that container's edge rather than duplicated; descending a level and returning restores the reader's prior place; a repository whose component and interface registries are present but declare no interface renders the stated "no interfaces declared" view rather than an empty one.

SR-170Shared authority surfaces are the serial actor's aloneapproval owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-170current content
SR-ID
SR-170
Title
Shared authority surfaces are the serial actor's alone
SN-Refs
SN-027;SN-010;SN-008;SN-025
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall write the shared records it derives - the compiled activity log and the generated project-state artifacts - only from the serial merge step and only against the merged tree, never from a parallel work branch.
Rationale
ONE DECISION PER ROW: a shared serial actor is a shared MECHANISM, not one decision. This row holds the EXCLUSIVE-WRITER contract alone; ordering-and-all-or-nothing is SR-173's and identity allocation SR-174's, each with its own children. Realizes SN-027's serialization guarantee at the surfaces where a parallel writer would actually corrupt something, with SN-010 (the status surface a human reads regenerates at the seam, never on a lane), SN-008 (a first failure stops loudly rather than half-regenerating) and SN-025 (the surface a session reads is generated, never hand-copied). The serial LANDING (SR-156) and the rule that shared authority surfaces regenerate only there are two contracts that fail independently, since a perfectly serialized landing that still lets a lane commit a regenerated artifact satisfies the landing row and not this one. The three surfaces sit in ONE row rather than three because they share one decision, not three: the serial trunk actor is their sole writer. That decision is already ruled for the identity mint — a work branch never mints a work-item id — and this row is its requirement-tier home, which it did not have. `phase` inherits the parent's 4, matching every child.
AcceptanceCriteria
The compiled activity log and the generated project-state artifacts are written only by the serial merge step and never committed from a work branch; a work branch that regenerates one is refused or its result is not what lands. Ordering and partial-result behaviour are SR-173's; identity allocation is SR-174's; the harness work-branch freshness skip is SR-006's clause and is not restated here.

SR-171Bounded retry on a declared transient model limitapproval owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-171current content
SR-ID
SR-171
Title
Bounded retry on a declared transient model limit
SN-Refs
SN-006;SN-025
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall retry a declared transient provider limit under a bounded backoff, surfacing the wait so a reader can tell a throttled run from a wedged one, rather than ending the run.
Rationale
This obligation was live but stated ONLY in SR-026's acceptance cell, where it minted a requirement the shall never made. Deleting it would have dropped real behaviour, so it is stated here instead. Realizes SN-006 (a walk-away run survives the conditions it will actually meet - a rate limit is the ordinary one) and SN-025. The bound is what separates this from a retry loop: unbounded retry against a limit that is not transient is indistinguishable from a hang, which is the failure SN-006 exists to prevent. Surfacing the wait is the other half - a silent backoff and a wedged run look identical from outside. ONE `shall`, with both clauses kept: surfacing is stated as a property of HOW the retry happens rather than as a second obligation, which is the honest reading - an unsurfaced bounded retry and a wedge are indistinguishable from outside, so a retry nobody can see does not discharge this row. Whether surfacing is instead a decision that fails independently of the retry - a split rather than one obligation - is an open question for the sitting, flagged and not decided here.
AcceptanceCriteria
A declared transient limit is retried under the declared backoff and the run continues; the wait is visible on the run surface rather than silent; a limit that is not declared transient ends the run with its own outcome code rather than retrying; the retry count and interval come from a declared value, never a literal.

Verification

LLR LLR-174current content
LLR-ID
LLR-174
SR-Refs
SR-171
Title
Rate-limit recognition + bounded, surfaced wait
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/agent_loop.py
CodeSymbol
limit_reset_hint/seconds_until_reset/main
Detail
A rate limit is recognized only on an error signal (the JSON result's is_error, or a nonzero exit for a plain-text template) whose message carries a reset wording - a healthy transcript merely mentioning limits never reads as a throttle - and it wins the outcome ladder as WAITING, bypassing the stall guard. With --wait-on-limit set, a parsed reset within that ceiling is slept and the session retried, the sleep printed with its reset time; an unparseable wording sleeps min(--limit-retry-fallback, ceiling), printed, and retries. Past the ceiling, or without the flag, the run stops at EXIT_WAITING (5) with a banner naming the resume time. Both dials are declared flags with declared defaults (0 / 3600), never in-line literals. The outcome ladder itself (WAITING ahead of every other code) is LLR-028's home; this row owns the recognition gate, the two clocks and the wait/stop fork.
Rationale
The wait/stop fork keys on the one ceiling the human consented to, so an unbounded silent sleep is unrepresentable - the fallback nap is capped at the same ceiling for the same reason, and every arm prints what it is waiting for so a throttled run is tellable from a wedged one at the console.

SR-172A stalled session ends at its declared limitapproval owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-172current content
SR-ID
SR-172
Title
A stalled session ends at its declared limit
SN-Refs
SN-006;SN-025
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall end a session that has made no declared progress within its declared stall limit, reporting the stall as its own outcome rather than as success or as an ordinary failure.
Rationale
The sibling of SR-171, and from the same defect: the obligation lived only in SR-026's acceptance. Realizes SN-006 and SN-025. It is a SEPARATE row from SR-171 because the two fail independently and for opposite reasons - a backoff that never gives up is the failure this row catches, and a stall limit that fires on a legitimately slow provider is the failure SR-171's bound prevents. Stated as its own outcome because a stall reported as a plain failure sends a reader looking for a defect that is not there, and reported as success is a false green.
AcceptanceCriteria
A session with no declared progress inside the declared stall limit ends and reports the stall as its own distinct outcome; a session making declared progress is not ended by that limit; the limit is read from a declared value; the stall outcome is distinguishable from both success and an ordinary failure at the run surface.

LLR LLR-175current content
LLR-ID
LLR-175
SR-Refs
SR-172
Title
Consecutive no-progress stall guard
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/agent_loop.py
CodeSymbol
LaneState.note_session/main
Detail
Declared progress is a moved git HEAD: note_session resets the stall counter on a commit and increments it otherwise, so the window is consecutive and interleaved progress is never ended by the limit; a throttled (WAITING) session bypasses the counter entirely, and a timed-out one counts (it produced no commit). At --stall-limit consecutive no-commit sessions (a declared flag, default 3) the loop stops with EXIT_STALL (4) - its own exit code, distinct from success and from the error/config exits - under a STALL banner pointing at the latest iteration log. Which banner (the all-ERROR agent-unavailable wording vs the generic work stall) is stall_verdict's fork and LLR-028's home under SR-028; this row owns the counter, the declared limit and the distinct exit.
Rationale
The counter reads committed evidence (HEAD movement), not session prose, so a session cannot talk its way out of the guard - and the reset-on-commit is what keeps the limit a stall detector rather than a slow-progress killer.

SR-173Shared regeneration is ordered and commits no partial resultapproval owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-173current content
SR-ID
SR-173
Title
Shared regeneration is ordered and commits no partial result
SN-Refs
SN-008;SN-010
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall regenerate the shared derived artifacts in declared dependency order, stopping at the first failure and committing no partially regenerated set.
Rationale
THE TRANSACTIONALITY HALF of three obligations that fail independently: ordering and all-or-nothing are one decision about a regeneration run, and they are not the same decision as who is allowed to write (SR-170) or how identity is allocated (SR-174). A regeneration that runs in the right order and still commits half a set satisfies neither, which is what makes them separable. Realizes SN-008 - a first failure that stops loudly beats a half-regenerated set that reads as complete - and SN-010. TRUTH-MATCHED TO THE DELIVERED BEHAVIOR, and the direction is deliberate. The shall read "leaving no partially regenerated set BEHIND" while the acceptance read "not left COMMITTED" - two different guarantees in one row, the stronger one in the normative cell. Adjudicated from CODE TRUTH rather than from preference, and the code is unambiguous: the regeneration step touches git NOT AT ALL, so on a first failure it exits nonzero having run no later step, with HEAD unmoved and nothing committed - and it DELIBERATELY LEAVES the already-green steps' output dirty in the working tree, because the design assigns the commit to the caller (LLR-142's "never commits; the caller owns the commit"). The residue is not tolerated, it is REQUIRED: TC-170's own evidence test asserts `git status --porcelain` is NON-EMPTY after the failure, so an implementation that cleaned up after itself would fail the test that holds this row. "Behind" therefore claimed something the system does not do and is tested NOT to do; restating it to "committing no partially regenerated set" matches the proven guarantee rather than weakening the row, because the weaker-sounding word is the one the evidence supports and the stronger one was never true. The transactional rollback a reader might expect from "behind" exists, but it belongs to the WRAPPING callers (the integrator and intake each reset the branch to its last work commit on failure) and is their obligation, not this row's - which is why stating it here would have credited this row with a guarantee a bare invocation does not provide.
AcceptanceCriteria
Regeneration runs the declared artifact families in declared dependency order, with a producer before every consumer that reads it; a failure stops the run at that point and reports it rather than continuing, running no later family; a failed run moves the recorded history not at all and commits no partially regenerated set, while the already-regenerated output stays uncommitted in the working tree for the caller to commit or discard; an absent artifact family is skipped with a stated reason rather than treated as a failure.

M

B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall allocate each work-item identity at most once, so that no two concurrent actors receive the same identity and an identity freed by a deletion is never re-issued.
Rationale
Identity allocation is its own decision, not a consequence of serialization: serializing the writer makes a COLLISION unlikely and does nothing about RE-USE, which is the half that silently re-points every commit message and archived document citing the freed id. The non-reuse clause has a `shall` of its own here rather than living in SR-170's acceptance, which is a cell that cannot mint a requirement. Realizes SN-008 and SN-025.
AcceptanceCriteria
Two actors finishing against the same tree cannot obtain the same work-item identity; an identity freed by deleting its record is not re-issued to new work; allocation counts from the recorded high-water mark rather than from the live maximum.

The unified trunk-side intake mint + the context block + the ratification-level arms

CodeSymbol

Detail

The mint commit mirrors the claim's bookkeeping shape; every derived title is deterministic, so the sweep CLI re-run is idempotent.
Rationale
A detected event must FORCE a row into the registry with nobody watching, and an id two lanes could pick at once must stay unrepresentable (R1).

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-175current content
SR-ID
SR-175
Title
Declared inclusion rule for content dispatched to a model provider
SN-Refs
SN-026
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall compose what it dispatches to an external model runner under a DECLARED inclusion rule rather than an implicit one — the content eligible for each dispatched brief, and what is excluded, readable as a declared set — with the basis on which repository content crosses to a declared provider stated as the scope of the consent the enabling surface grants, never presented as a technical bound on what an invoked runner reads.
Rationale
Hat-derived (hat.DATA-PROTECTION, with hat.SECURITY C-SEC-5 and hat.LEGAL C-LEG-3 converging on the same boundary control — three charters, three reasons, one crossing; clause texts in docs/plans/2026-08-16-blind-derivation-c-hats.md): repository content is briefed to external model providers, and for an adopter that content's history carries every contributor's name, email and timestamp — personal data crossing with no declared basis or exclusion in any need. SN-026's own text governs WHICH model runs, never WHAT IT IS SHOWN, so this obligation is content beyond the parent's demand — the derived-requirement class, labelled so the deriving lens is reviewable and the need owner sees it fed back (the alternative, amending SN-026, was deliberately not taken alongside: one rule, one home — option analysis in docs/plans/2026-08-17-wi468-obligation-intake-options.md §1 option (b)). WHAT EXISTS TODAY IS A DISCIPLINE, NOT A DECLARATION: the push channel is measurably narrow — an allowlist-only planning surface, the project log excluded BY NAME in three composers, zero authorship fields formatted into any prompt — but the rule lives as a convention inside the composing functions, unfalsifiable because no requirement stated it; this row converts it into a stated one and the chain below pins the conventions as they exist. NOT YET MECHANIZED, stated rather than implied: the declared-set surface itself (a per-row inclusion/exclusion field on the provider registry, or a policy dial) and the planted-credential dispatch block in the acceptance are not built. THE PULL-CHANNEL LIMIT, the design-control honesty this row must carry: every enabled runner is launched with permission-bypass flags and can read the whole tree itself, so the declared rule can bound what the loop ASSEMBLES and state the scope of what the consent surface CONSENTS to — it cannot technically bound an invoked runner, the same no-design-control reading the frame applies to external actors at REL-003 and the B-06/B-07 cut. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-17 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
The inclusion rule for each brief class the loop dispatches is readable as a declared set naming what is eligible and what is excluded, so a composer can be checked against it rather than against convention; a planted credential inside brief-eligible content blocks dispatch (the security lens's fit criterion, on the same scanner the transcript path already runs); the basis and exclusion for repository content crossing to a declared provider are stated where the provider is declared, and the consent surface's documentation names them as the scope of what enabling consents to; the pull channel is presented as consent-scoped, never as filtered. The conventions this row makes declared are, today, the planning surface's allowlist-only registry read and the composers' by-name exclusions and clipped log reads; the declared-set surface itself is the row's stated build gap.

SR-176A privacy finding persists by class and location, never by valueapproval owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-176current content
SR-ID
SR-176
Title
A privacy finding persists by class and location, never by value
SN-Refs
SN-009
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
Any durable record the delivered kit produces of a secrets or privacy finding shall identify the finding by its class and location, never by the matched value.
Rationale
Hat-derived (hat.DATA-PROTECTION, C-DPR-2 — clause text in docs/plans/2026-08-16-blind-derivation-c-hats.md): the finding record is the one artifact guaranteed to contain the personal data it reports, created by the control itself — SN-009 defeating itself, since a scanner that catches a value and then echoes it into a committed artifact has published what it protected. The intake proposal's measurement (docs/plans/2026-08-17-wi468-obligation-intake-options.md §2 option (b)) grounds the row: the scanner mints no record of its own (check_privacy prints location, label and the MATCHED VALUE to stdout and writes nothing), but an unattended run's transcript is committed bookkeeping, so the durable copy of every finding is the session log — and the transcript path redacts credential shapes only. DELIBERATE NARROWING of the charter's ask, recorded here as the deriving decision: C-DPR-2 asks for a retention limit and an access rule, and a kit cannot honestly promise either over an adopter's git history (committed content is effectively forever; access is the host's) — what it CAN promise is that the matched value never reaches durable storage, which moots both. MECHANIZED TODAY for the always-on class: the transcript writer redacts the credential shapes and its summary line names class and count, never the value — pinned by the chain below. NOT YET MECHANIZED, stated rather than implied: the PII/identity classes the privacy gate adds are not in the redaction set, so for an adopter with the gate on a PII finding echoed into a session transcript would persist — closing that, on the same seam, is this row's build debt. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-17 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
A planted value matched by a scanning class appears 0 times in any tracked artifact after the run that caught it; the durable trace of the finding names the class and the location (a count line, a path), never the matched text; the unredacted raw stream stays in untracked scratch space only. The one path by which a finding becomes durable is the transcript's redaction seam, which holds today for the credential classes; the privacy-gate PII/identity classes are the row's stated gap.

SR SR-177current content
SR-ID
SR-177
Title
Fan-out utilisation reported from the run's own telemetry
SN-Refs
SN-027
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall report, per run, the utilisation of the fan-out it commissions — the lanes configured, the lanes actually occupied, and the work integrated per unit of wall time — derived from the run's own recorded telemetry, reported and never gated, with no declared improvement target.
Rationale
Hat-derived (hat.PERFORMANCE, C-PRF-1 — clause text in docs/plans/2026-08-16-blind-derivation-c-hats.md): the charter refuses a declared budget with no measurement behind it, and SN-027 is that finding inverted — the system's most complex machinery justified by a throughput claim no instrument measures, flagged unfalsifiable as written by three independent derivations. The intake proposal (docs/plans/2026-08-17-wi468-obligation-intake-options.md §3 option (b)) is DELIBERATELY LESS than the charter asks — the narrowing is the decision: C-PRF-1 wants a declared improvement over the serial semantic on a declared workload, but the wall time of an LLM loop is dominated by provider latency and model choice, so a numeric target would pin machine, provider and model conditions the kit does not control (one machine is one data point) — this row makes the throughput claim OBSERVABLE rather than BUDGETED. The concrete argument the instrument earns its keep: the lanes dial is undeclared in this repository and defaults to 1, so the machinery SN-027 justifies by fan-out runs SERIAL here and nothing today would say so — this report's first run would have printed lanes=1. SN-027's own `why` states the same structural claim, so the need and the instrument stop overclaiming together. NOT DECOMPOSED, stated rather than implied: nothing aggregates the existing per-session telemetry (wall seconds, api seconds, turns — the session-log headers and the iteration index) by lane or by run, so there is no seam to pin and no test to cite; the row lands Drafted-undecomposed with the aggregation surface as its whole build gap, and the telemetry columns alone do NOT satisfy it. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-17 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
A completed run's report states the lanes configured, the lanes actually occupied, and the work items integrated per wall-hour, derived from the run's recorded telemetry; the report is informational — no threshold, no exit-code change; a serial run reports its single lane rather than omitting the line, so the configured-versus-occupied gap is always visible. The report aggregates the run's own recorded per-session telemetry; the aggregation itself is the row's stated build gap.

SR-178Text that has moved away from its acceptance record is reportedapproval owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-178current content
SR-ID
SR-178
Title
Text that has moved away from its acceptance record is reported
SN-Refs
SN-029
Boundary-Refs
B-02
Requirement
The kit shall report any recorded artifact whose text has moved away from the copy recording its acceptance - stakeholder needs included, despite their carrying no status cell - regardless of any Status movement.
Rationale
THE DRIFT half, separable from SR-140 in the direction that matters: a record that exists and rides its approval commit, with nothing ever comparing live text against it, satisfies SR-140 completely and leaves every later amendment invisible. That is not a hypothetical - it is precisely the failure the git-history derivation had, and the reason a baseline on disk was owed at all. REGARDLESS OF STATUS MOVEMENT is the load-bearing clause, not a qualifier. The amendment machinery watches the Status flip, and the sanctioned staged-amendment path deliberately does not flip - so a rule keyed off the flip returns nothing on exactly the rows a sitting exists to judge. Reporting against the recorded copy is the only reading that survives that, because the copy is a baseline OUTSIDE the live file and a walk over the live file can never be one. THE STAKEHOLDER-NEED RIDER is in the normative text rather than left to the acceptance because a need has no Status cell at all: every amendment-watching rule in the kit is keyed off a field the need tier does not have, so without the clause the tier a sitting most wants to hold is the one tier the rule cannot reach. Needs are covered by the whole-file copy rather than by a per-need projection - the three need tables have different shapes, and a projection would have recorded the garbling. WHAT COUNTS AS MOVEMENT is one definition with one home, shared with the amend-without-flip guard: the id column and the Status cell are excluded STRUCTURALLY (the id is the join key rather than content, and folding Status in would make every flip read as an amendment and every amendment invisible behind its own flip), and the remainder splits into a ratified half and a traced half. Two readers of one basis rather than two rules that agree until they do not.
AcceptanceCriteria
An artifact whose normative text has moved since the copy that recorded its acceptance is reported whether or not its Status moved; a ratified cell moving under a row still claiming approval is reported, a merely traced cell moving is not, and a row below approval has no claim to fall from and is never reported; the status marker itself is never read as the movement, so a flip is neither an amendment nor a mask for one; and a stakeholder need is held to the same rule despite carrying no status cell for the amendment machinery to watch.

LLR LLR-158current content
LLR-ID
LLR-158
SR-Refs
SR-178
Title
The one comparison basis an amendment is measured by
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/check_trajectory.py
CodeSymbol
split_changed_cells/spine_cell_class/_RATIFIED_TEXT/staged_spine_amendments
Detail
RE-POINTED onto the mechanism that SURVIVED. This row used to describe a DIGEST engine (normative_text/sn_normative_text/digest/current_digests); the last_approved snapshot deleted it whole, because an approval that records what it blessed by COPYING the registries needs no canonical text to hash, no separator that cannot occur in a cell and no second exclusion list. The obligation is unchanged and is discharged by split_changed_cells(csv_path, id_col, head, row) -> {"ratified": {cell: (before, after)}, "traced": {...}}: the id column and Status are excluded STRUCTURALLY - the id is the join key rather than content, and folding Status in would make every flip read as an amendment and every amendment invisible behind its own flip - and the remainder is split by spine_cell_class, the SAME classifier the amend-without-flip guard reads, so the two can never disagree about what normative means. It is PUBLIC for exactly one reason: baseline_snapshot.is_drifted reads it as the drift basis (LLR-173), so the snapshot comparison and the staged-amendment guard are one rule with one home rather than two rules that agree until they do not. _RATIFIED_TEXT names the one Status value whose row TEXT is ratified (Approved — the status fold collapsed Verified and Planned into it) and requires the SAME value on both sides, so prose rewritten under an Approved row is never invisible. It returns the before/after PAIRS a brief has to render anyway, which is why it replaced a hash rather than being wrapped by one. Needs are covered by the whole-file copy rather than by a per-need projection: the three need tables have different shapes, and a projection would have hashed the garbling.
Rationale
A ledger that does not parse is not an anchor at all, and every reader then falls back silently to the git-history derivation it replaced. The copy-based baseline removes that failure mode rather than hardening it: the record is a file a human can open and `git diff`. What had to be kept is the DEFINITION of a change - which cells count, and which of them are ratified rather than merely traced - and that is one function, cited by both readers, rather than a hash whose inputs each reader re-derives.

SR SR-179current content
SR-ID
SR-179
Title
The acceptance record can only be written by copying live text
SN-Refs
SN-029
Boundary-Refs
B-02
Requirement
The kit shall refuse a recorded copy that is not byte-identical, in the commit that writes it, to the live text it claims to record - so the only path into the record runs through the live registry first.
Rationale
THE ANTI-LAUNDERING half, failing independently of both siblings: a comparison that reports drift faithfully against a record a hand edit wrote is a comparison against a forgery, so SR-178 can pass in full while this row is violated. It is what makes "the record holds what was actually blessed" a DECIDABLE property rather than a convention. IN THE COMMIT THAT WRITES IT is the whole strength of the rule and the reason it is stated as a refusal rather than a report. One comparison, made at the moment of the act, catches a hand edit, a partial copy and a copy-then-amend-live alike - three attacks that need three different detectors if the check is made after the fact. It is the replacement for the co-mutation guard the abandoned on-row anchor design would have needed: a stronger rule for less machinery. NOTE THE REVERSED SIGN, recorded so it is never read as a softening: under the anchor design a stamp written beside its own amendment HID the amendment, so same-commit was an error; under copies the copy MUST ride the approval commit, because riding it is the only thing that makes "this copy is what was blessed" decidable. Its companion rule, the SEED, is deliberately NOT here: refusing to create the first record without an explicit seed is a statement about how a record comes into being and rides its approval commit, which is SR-140's subject, so it sits there. This row governs every write AFTER that, which is where laundering actually lives. DELETION IS AN ATTACK ON THIS ROW, not on SR-140's: the cheapest laundering is never to forge the record but to remove the page, since the unanchored rule reports a row whose copy reads below it and deleting the copy deletes exactly that evidence. So a registry removed WHILE THE REST OF THE RECORD STANDS is refused, and a wholesale replacement of the record is not - retirement is legitimate, and a rule that fired on it would make its own design undeployable.
AcceptanceCriteria
A commit that writes a recorded copy differing from its live counterpart is refused, which is the one comparison that catches a hand edit, a partial copy and a copy-then-amend-live alike; a faithful copy passes; a copy removed while the rest of the record stands is refused, while a wholesale replacement of the record is not; the first record refuses to create itself without an explicit seed, so no automated step can bless a tree; and prose that is not a recorded registry is exempt.

LLR-ID

SR-Refs
SR-179
Title
The mirror invariant: the record is only ever written by copying live
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/check_trajectory.py
CodeSymbol
staged_snapshot_findings/_snapshot_survives
Detail
MINTED by the SR-140 split: SR-179 is the anti-laundering half, and its mechanism is a sibling of LLR-158's comparison basis rather than a part of LLR-173's record. staged_snapshot_findings(root, base, head) is a whole-commit rule, not a per-row one: in ANY commit that touches the last_approved record, every touched file must be byte-identical to its live counterpart AS OF THAT SAME COMMIT. One comparison is what makes a hand edit, a partial copy and a copy-then-amend-live all fail, and it is why no separate co-mutation guard is owed - the abandoned on-row anchor design would have needed one. It reads the INDEX and the commit-ish alike (matching _spine_revs' prefix contract), so it fires at the hook where the act happens rather than after the fact. THE DELETION ARM is the half a first reading misses and is deliberately asymmetric: _snapshot_survives is the two-line tree probe that separates a registry removed WHILE THE REST OF THE RECORD STANDS (refused - deleting the copy deletes exactly the evidence the unanchored rule reads, which is cheaper laundering than forging one) from the record going away WHOLE (silent - retirement and a wholesale replacement are both legitimate, and a rule that fired on them would make its own design undeployable). The prose README inside the record is exempt: it is not a recorded registry, and holding it to a live counterpart that does not exist would red every signing. It joins the failure set at check_trajectory's main aggregation, so unlike LLR-173's advisory unanchored arm this one is a real gate today.
Rationale
The record is worthless if anything but a copy can write it, and "only a copy writes it" is a claim no per-row check can make - a per-row rule sees the row it is given and never the commit that wrote it. Placing the invariant on the COMMIT is what converts a convention into a decidable property. It lives in check_trajectory rather than in baseline_snapshot because the writer must not also be the judge of its own writes: the module that copies has every reason to believe its copy is faithful, and the guard exists for the cases where it is not.

Integration
Method
Run the mirror-invariant half of the baseline-snapshot suite over real temp repos - the half TC-167 no longer covers, which is the record's own contents. (a) A clean copy passes, so the rule is silent on the legitimate act it exists to permit. (b) A HAND-EDITED record file fails, and (c) a PARTIAL copy fails - the two forgeries the single byte-identity comparison catches without a second detector. (d) THE DELETION ASYMMETRY, both arms executed rather than argued: a record file deleted WHILE THE REST OF THE RECORD STANDS fails (the cheap laundering - removing the page the unanchored rule reads), while deleting the WHOLE record is SILENT (retirement and wholesale replacement are legitimate). (e) The prose README inside the record is exempt, because it is not a recorded registry and has no live counterpart to be identical to.

Rule a decision by appending to docs/log.md's Decisions log and setting the row's Status; bless an amendment by moving the spine row's Status Modified→Approved — and from the first signing onward, run intake.py snapshot in the SAME commit, or the record of what was blessed does not move. The gate re-derives on its own.
